\displaystyle\bar{\Psi}(u)\leq\frac{\epsilon}{2}.

(B-1)

For any integer
N
, consider the following distribution
F_{\Psi,N}
defined through its Complementary Cumulative Distribution Function
\overline{F}_{\Psi,N}
:

\displaystyle\overline{F}_{\Psi,N}(v)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\begin{cases}1&\quad\mbox{if }v<0,\\
q&\quad\mbox{if }v\mbox{ in }[0,w),\\
\frac{q}{N}&\quad\mbox{if }v\mbox{ in }[w,N^{2}M),\\
0&\quad\mbox{if }v\mbox{ in }[N^{2}M,+\infty).\end{cases}

Note that
F_{\Psi,N}\mbox{ in }\pazocal{G}(w,I)
and that
F_{\Psi,N}
represents a three point mass distribution with mass at points
0
,
w
and
N^{2}M
.

Note also that
\mbox{opt}(F_{\Psi,N})=\max\left\{qw,qNM\right\}.

Since
M\geq w
and
N\geq 1
, we have
\mbox{opt}(F_{\Psi,N})=qNM
. Thus the performance of mechanism
\Psi
is given by

\displaystyle R(\Psi,F_{\Psi,N})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{{qNM}}\left({\int_{[0,w)}u\overline{F}_{\Psi,N}(u)d\Psi(u)+\int_{[w,N^{2}M)}u\overline{F}_{\Psi,N}(u)d\Psi(u)+\int_{[N^{2}M,+\infty)}u\overline{F}_{\Psi,N}(u)d\Psi(u)}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{NM}\left({\int_{[0,w)}ud\Psi(u)+\frac{1}{N}\int_{[w,N^{2}M)}ud\Psi(u)}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{NM}\left({\int_{[0,w)}ud\Psi(u)+\frac{1}{N}\int_{[w,M)}ud\Psi(u)+\frac{1}{N}\int_{[M,N^{2}M)}ud\Psi(u)}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{NM}\left({w\Psi(w)}+{\frac{1}{N}M(\Psi(M)-\Psi(0))}+{\frac{1}{N}N^{2}M(\Psi(N^{2}M)-\Psi(M))}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{w}{NM}+\frac{1}{N^{2}}+(1-\Psi(M)),

where in the last step we use the fact that for any
u\geq 0

\Psi(u)
is in
[0,1].

Let us now choose
N
large enough such that

\frac{w}{NM}+\frac{1}{N^{2}}\leq\frac{\epsilon}{2},

Combining the latter with (
B-1
), we get

\displaystyle R(\Psi,F_{\Psi,N})\leq\frac{\epsilon}{2}+\frac{\epsilon}{2}=\epsilon.

Taking
\epsilon\to 0
concludes the proof.
∎

B.1
Additional result for the cases
q\mbox{ in }\{0,1\}

Lemma B-1
.

For any mechanism
\Psi\mbox{ in }\pazocal{P}
, and any
\alpha\mbox{ in }[0,1]
, if
q\mbox{ in }\{0,1\}
, then

\displaystyle\inf_{F\in\pazocal{F}_{\alpha}(w,\{q\})}R(\Psi,F)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 0.

Proof of
Lemma

B-1
.

We will first show the case when
q=0
then the case when
q=1
. For both cases, we will exhibit worst case families of distributions for which the seller cannot achieve a non-trivial guarantee.

For any
r>0
, let us introduce the following distribution through its Complementary Cumulative Distribution Function:

\displaystyle\overline{F}_{r}(v)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\begin{cases}1&\quad\mbox{if }v\mbox{ in }[0,r),\\
0&\quad\mbox{if }v\mbox{ in }[r,+\infty).\end{cases}

The latter distribution represents a point mass at
r
.

Case
q=0
.

We have, for any
r<w
,
F_{r}\mbox{ in }\pazocal{F}_{\alpha}(w,\{0\})
and
\mbox{opt}(F_{r})=r
. Furthermore, for any mechanism
\Psi\mbox{ in }\pazocal{P}
, we have:

\displaystyle\inf_{F\in\pazocal{F}_{\alpha}(w,\{0\})}R(\Psi,F)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{\mathbb{E}_{\Psi}[p\overline{F}_{r}(p)]}{\mbox{opt}(F_{r})}=\frac{1}{r}\int_{0}^{r}ud\Psi(u)=\frac{1}{r}\int_{0}^{r}\int_{0}^{u}dsd\Psi(u)

\displaystyle\overset{(a)}{=}

\displaystyle\frac{1}{r}\int_{0}^{r}\int_{s}^{r}d\Psi(u)ds=\frac{1}{r}\int_{0}^{r}\left(\Psi(r)-\Psi(s)\right)ds

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\Psi(r)-\Psi(0).

Where in equality (a), we used Fubini–Tonelli theorem as
(s,u)\to 1
is a non-negative measurable function and
([0,r],d\Psi)
and
([0,r],dx)
are
\sigma
-finite measure spaces.

The right hand side above converges to zero as
r\to 0^{+}
since
\Psi\mbox{ in }\pazocal{P}=\pazocal{D}
and is therefore right continuous. We conclude the case
q=0.

Case
q=1
.

We have, for any
r>w
,
F_{r}\mbox{ in }\pazocal{F}_{\alpha}(w,\{1\})
and
\mbox{opt}(F_{r})=r
. Furthermore, for any mechanism
\Psi
, we have:

\displaystyle\inf_{F\in\pazocal{F}_{\alpha}(w,\{1\})}R(\Psi,F)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{\mathbb{E}_{\Psi}[p\overline{F}_{r}(p)]}{\mbox{opt}(F_{r})}=\frac{1}{r}\int_{0}^{r}ud\Psi(u)=\frac{1}{r}\int_{0}^{r}(\Psi(r)-\Psi(u))du.

Since
\Psi\mbox{ in }\pazocal{P}
, we have
\lim_{r\rightarrow+\infty}\Psi(r)=1
, therefore, for any
\epsilon>0
, there exists
A>0
such that:

\displaystyle 1-\Psi(r)=|\Psi(r)-1|<\epsilon\quad\mbox{if }r\in[A,+\infty).

(B-2)

Let
r\mbox{ in }(A,+\infty)
, we have:

\displaystyle\inf_{F\in\pazocal{F}_{\alpha}(w,\{1\})}R(\Psi,F)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{r}\int_{0}^{r}(\Psi(r)-\Psi(u))du

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{r}\left(\int_{[0,A)}\left(\Psi(r)-\Psi(u)\right)du+\int_{[A,r]}\left(\Psi(r)-\Psi(u)\right)du\right)

\displaystyle\overset{(a)}{\leq}

\displaystyle\frac{1}{r}\left({}_{[0,A)}du+\int_{[A,r]}\left(1-\Psi(u)\right)du\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{A}{r}+\frac{1}{r}\int_{A}^{r}\left(1-\Psi(u)\right)du

\displaystyle\overset{(b)}{\leq}

\displaystyle\frac{A}{r}+\frac{r-A}{r}\epsilon\overset{(c)}{\leq}\frac{A}{r}+\epsilon,

where in
(a)
we use the fact that for any
u\geq 0
, we have
0\leq\Psi(u)\leq 1.
And in
(b)
we use (
B-2
). In (c), we used the fact that
r-A\leq r.

Hence we conclude that for any
r\geq A/\epsilon
, we get that

\displaystyle\inf_{F\in\pazocal{F}_{\alpha}(w,\{1\})}R(\Psi,F)\leq 2\epsilon.

Since
\epsilon
was arbitrary, this completes the proof for the case
q=1
.
∎

Appendix C
Proofs and auxiliary results for
Section

3

Lemma C-1
.

For any
(s,q_{s}),(s^{\prime},q_{s^{\prime}})\mbox{ in }([0,+\infty)\times[0,1])^{2}
such that
s\leq s^{\prime}
and
q_{s}\geq q_{s^{\prime}}>0
, the distribution
G_{\alpha,t}(\cdot|(s,q_{s}),(s^{\prime},q_{s^{\prime}}))
with
t\geq s^{\prime}
, defined in (
3.1
), belongs to
\pazocal{F}_{\alpha}(s,q_{s})\cap\pazocal{F}_{\alpha}(s^{\prime},q_{s^{\prime}})
if and only if

\displaystyle q_{s}\geq\Gamma_{\alpha}\left(\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q_{s^{\prime}}\right)\frac{s}{s^{\prime}}\right).

Proof of
Lemma

C-1
.

Let us show each direction.

\Longrightarrow
) If the distribution
G_{\alpha,t}(\cdot|(s,q_{s}),(s^{\prime},q_{s^{\prime}}))
belongs to
\pazocal{F}_{\alpha}(s,q_{s})\cap\pazocal{F}_{\alpha}(s^{\prime},q_{s^{\prime}})
then
G_{\alpha,t}(\cdot|(s,q_{s}),(s^{\prime},q_{s^{\prime}}))
is
\alpha-
regular, therefore by
Lemma

1
applied to the interval
[0,s^{\prime}]
, we have that

\displaystyle q_{s}=\overline{G}_{\alpha,t}(s|(s,q_{s}),(s^{\prime},q_{s^{\prime}}))\geq\Gamma_{\alpha}\left(\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q_{s^{\prime}}\right)\frac{s}{s^{\prime}}\right),

and hence the first direction is established. Let us now show the other direction.

\Longleftarrow
) Suppose now that
q_{s}\geq\Gamma_{\alpha}\left(\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q_{s^{\prime}}\right)\frac{s}{s^{\prime}}\right)
. By definition of
G_{\alpha,t}(\cdot|(s,q_{s}),(s^{\prime},q_{s^{\prime}}))
, we have:

\displaystyle\overline{G}_{\alpha,t}(s|(s,q_{s}),(s^{\prime},q_{s^{\prime}}))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle q_{s}

\displaystyle\overline{G}_{\alpha,t}(s^{\prime}|(s,q_{s}),(s^{\prime},q_{s^{\prime}}))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle q_{s^{\prime}}.

Therefore, we only have to show that the distribution
G_{\alpha,t}(\cdot|(s,q_{s}),(s^{\prime},q_{s^{\prime}}))
is
\alpha
-regular. Using
Lemma

A-1
, the associated
\alpha
-virtual value function is given by

\displaystyle\phi_{G_{\alpha,t}(\cdot|(s,q_{s}),(s^{\prime},q_{s^{\prime}}))}^{\alpha}(v)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\begin{cases}-\frac{s}{\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q_{s}\right)}&\quad\mbox{if }v\mbox{ in }[0,s],\\
(1-\alpha)s-\frac{s^{\prime}-s}{\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(\frac{q_{s^{\prime}}}{q_{s}}\right)}&\quad\mbox{if }v\mbox{ in }(s,s^{\prime}].\end{cases}

Thus the
\alpha
-virtual value function is piece-wise constant. Now we need to show that
\phi_{G_{\alpha,t}(\cdot|(s,q_{s}),(s^{\prime},q_{s^{\prime}}))}^{\alpha}(v)
is non-decreasing. Next, we evaluate the difference between the two constant values that the virtual value function is taking.

\displaystyle(1-\alpha)s-\frac{s^{\prime}-s}{\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(\frac{q_{s^{\prime}}}{q_{s}}\right)}-(-\frac{s}{\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q_{s}\right)})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle s\frac{(1-\alpha)\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q_{s}\right)\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(\frac{q_{s^{\prime}}}{q_{s}}\right)+\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q_{s}\right)+\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(\frac{q_{s^{\prime}}}{q_{s}}\right)-\frac{s^{\prime}}{s}\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q_{s}\right)}{\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q_{s}\right)\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(\frac{q_{s^{\prime}}}{q_{s}}\right)}

\displaystyle\overset{(a)}{=}

\displaystyle s\frac{\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q_{s}\frac{q_{s^{\prime}}}{q_{s}}\right)-\frac{s^{\prime}}{s}\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q_{s}\right)}{\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q_{s}\right)\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(\frac{q_{s^{\prime}}}{q_{s}}\right)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle s\frac{\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q_{s^{\prime}}\right)-\frac{s^{\prime}}{s}\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q_{s}\right)}{\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q_{s}\right)\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(\frac{q_{s^{\prime}}}{q_{s}}\right)}\overset{(b)}{\geq}0,

where (a) stems from the fact that
\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(uv\right)=\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(u\right)+\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(v\right)+(1-\alpha)\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(u\right)\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(v\right)
and (b) is due to the fact that by assumption
q_{s}\geq\Gamma_{\alpha}\left(\frac{s}{s^{\prime}}\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q_{s^{\prime}}\right)\right)
and that the function
\Gamma_{\alpha}\left(\cdot\right)
is non increasing. This shows that the
\alpha
-virtual value function of
G_{\alpha,t}(\cdot|(s,q_{s}),(s^{\prime},q_{s^{\prime}}))
is non decreasing. This concludes the proof.
∎

Lemma C-2
.

Let
\alpha\mbox{ in }[0,1]
,
w>0
,
q\mbox{ in }(0,1)
, and
r\mbox{ in }[\underline{r}_{\alpha}(w,q),w)\cup[w,\overline{r}_{\alpha}(w,q)]
. Then the optimal price associated with
F_{\alpha}(\cdot|r,(w,q))
is given by
r
.

Proof of
Lemma

C-2
.

We compute the virtual value function for the function
F_{\alpha}(\cdot|r,(w,q))
. Since the definition of
F_{\alpha}(\cdot|r,(w,q))
depends on whether
r<w
or
r\geq w
, we treat each case separately.

Case 1:
r\in[\underline{r}_{\alpha}(w,q),w)
:

By applying
Lemma

A-1
for the pair
((r,1),(w,q))
, we get the virtual value function at
v\geq r
satisfies

\displaystyle\phi_{F_{\alpha}(\cdot|r,(w,q))}^{0}(v)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\alpha v+(1-\alpha)r-\frac{w-r}{\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q\right)}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle r\left(1+\frac{1}{\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q\right)}\right)-\frac{w}{\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q\right)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(1+\frac{1}{\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q\right)}\right)\left(r-\underline{r}_{\alpha}(w,q)\right),

since
r\geq\underline{r}_{\alpha}(w,q)
, then we conclude that
\phi_{F_{\alpha}(\cdot|r,(w,q))}^{0}(v)\geq 0
. Now, since
r
is the lower support of the distribution
F_{\alpha}(\cdot|r,(w,q))
in the case
r<w
and
F_{\alpha}(\cdot|r,(w,q))
is regular, we conclude that necessarily the optimal price is at
r
.

Case 2:
r\in[w,\overline{r}_{\alpha}(w,q)]
:

In this case, we assume
\overline{r}_{\alpha}(w,q)\geq w
, otherwise the set is empty.
Similarly, by applying
Lemma

A-1
for the pair
((0,1),(w,q)),
we get that the virtual value function at
v<r
satisfies

\displaystyle\phi_{F_{\alpha}(\cdot|r,(w,q))}^{0}(v)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\alpha v+\left(0-\frac{w}{\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q\right)}\right)\>=\>\alpha\left(v-\overline{r}_{\alpha}(w,q)\right).

Since
v<r\leq\overline{r}_{\alpha}(w,q)
, we conclude that
\phi_{F_{\alpha}(\cdot|r,(w,q))}^{0}(v)\leq 0
. Now, since
r
is the upper support of the distribution
F_{\alpha}(\cdot|r,(w,q))
in the case
r\geq w
and
F_{\alpha}(\cdot|r,(w,q))
is regular, we conclude that necessarily the optimal price is given by
r
.

∎

Lemma C-3
.

The distribution
G_{\alpha,r^{*}\vee w}(\cdot|(r^{*}\wedge w,q^{*}\vee q),(r^{*}\vee w,q^{*}\wedge q))
, defined in Eq. (
3.1
), belongs to
\{F\mbox{ in }\pazocal{F}_{\alpha}(w,q):r_{F}=r^{*},q_{F}=q^{*}\}
if and only if
(r^{*},q^{*})
belongs to
{\mathscr{}B}_{\alpha}(w,q)
.

Proof of
Lemma

C-3
.

One direction of the proof is direct. In particular,
if the distribution
G_{\alpha,r^{*}\vee w}(\cdot|(r^{*}\wedge w,q^{*}\vee q),(r^{*}\vee w,q^{*}\wedge q))
belongs to
\{F\mbox{ in }\pazocal{F}_{\alpha}(w,q):r_{F}=r^{*},q_{F}=q^{*}\}
then by definition we have
(r^{*},q^{*})\mbox{ in }{\mathscr{}B}_{\alpha}(w,q)
.

Let us now show the other direction, and suppose that
(r^{*},q^{*})
belongs to
{\mathscr{}B}_{\alpha}(w,q)
and let
F\mbox{ in }\pazocal{F}_{\alpha}(w,q)
be a corresponding distribution with
r_{F}=r^{*}
and
q_{F}=q^{*}
. We will first show that
G_{\alpha,r^{*}\vee w}(\cdot|(r^{*}\wedge w,q^{*}\vee q),(r^{*}\vee w,q^{*}\wedge q))
belongs to
\pazocal{F}_{\alpha}
and that the revenue curve of
F
is lower bounded by the revenue curve of
G_{\alpha,r^{*}\vee w}(\cdot|(r^{*}\wedge w,q^{*}\vee q),(r^{*}\vee w,q^{*}\wedge q))
. In a second step, we will show that the optimal revenue of
G_{\alpha,r^{*}\vee w}(\cdot|(r^{*}\wedge w,q^{*}\vee q),(r^{*}\vee w,q^{*}\wedge q))
is achieved at
r^{*}
.

Step 1:

We separate the cases
r^{*}<w
and
r^{*}\geq w
.

Case 1:

r^{*}<w
. By
Lemma

1
, note that we have that
q^{*}=\overline{F}(r^{*})\geq\Gamma_{\alpha}\left(\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q\right)\frac{r^{*}}{w}\right)
. By
Lemma

C-1
, applied to the following parameters
(s,q_{s})=(r^{*},q^{*})
and
(s^{\prime},q_{s^{\prime}})=(w,q)
,
G_{\alpha,w}(\cdot|(r^{*},q^{*}),(w,q))
belongs to
\pazocal{F}_{\alpha}
. Furthermore, by
Lemma

1
again, we have that

\displaystyle v\overline{F}(v)

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\begin{cases}v\overline{G}_{\alpha,w}(v|(r^{*},q^{*}),(w,q))&\quad\mbox{if }v\in[0,r^{*}],\\
v\overline{G}_{\alpha,w}(v|(r^{*},q^{*}),(w,q))&\quad\mbox{if }v\in(r^{*},w].\end{cases}

Case 2:

r^{*}\geq w
. By
Lemma

1
, we have that
q=\overline{F}(w)\geq\Gamma_{\alpha}\left(\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q^{*}\right)\frac{w}{r^{*}}\right)
and hence, by
Lemma

C-1
applied to the following parameters
(s,q_{s})=(w,q)
and
(s^{\prime},q_{s^{\prime}})=(r^{*},q^{*})
,
G_{\alpha,r^{*}}(\cdot|(w,q),(r^{*},q^{*}))
belongs to
\pazocal{F}_{\alpha}
. Furthermore, by
Lemma

1
again, we have that

\displaystyle v\overline{F}(v)

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\begin{cases}v\overline{G}_{\alpha,r^{*}}(v|(w,q),(r^{*},q^{*}))&\quad\mbox{if }v\mbox{ in }[0,w],\\
v\overline{G}_{\alpha,r^{*}}(v|(w,q),(r^{*},q^{*}))&\quad\mbox{if }v\mbox{ in }[w,r^{*}].\end{cases}

Therefore in both cases, we have that
v\overline{F}(v)\geq v\overline{G}_{\alpha,r^{*}\vee w}(v|(r^{*}\wedge w,q^{*}\vee q),(r^{*}\vee w,q^{*}\wedge q))
for all
v\mbox{ in }[0,+\infty)
and
G_{\alpha,r^{*}\vee w}(\cdot|(r^{*}\wedge w,q^{*}\vee q),(r^{*}\vee w,q^{*}\wedge q))
is
\alpha
-regular.

Step 2:

To conclude the proof we will show that the optimal revenue associated with the distribution
G_{\alpha,r^{*}\vee w}(\cdot|(r^{*}\wedge w,q^{*}\vee q),(r^{*}\vee w,q^{*}\wedge q))
is achieved at
r^{*}.
We will show that by contradiction.

Since
G_{\alpha,r^{*}\vee w}(\cdot|(r^{*}\wedge w,q^{*}\vee q),(r^{*}\vee w,q^{*}\wedge q))
is
\alpha
-regular, then the associated revenue function is unimodal and achieves its maximum at some point
r_{G}
in
[0,+\infty)
. Suppose for a moment
r_{G}\overline{G}_{\alpha,r^{*}\vee w}(r_{G}|(r^{*}\wedge w,q^{*}\vee q),(r^{*}\vee w,q^{*}\wedge q))>r^{*}\overline{G}_{\alpha,r^{*}\vee w}(r^{*}|(r^{*}\wedge w,q^{*}\vee q),(r^{*}\vee w,q^{*}\wedge q))
. Then, using the above lower-bounds, one would have

\displaystyle r_{G}\overline{F}(r_{G})

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle r_{G}\overline{G}_{\alpha,r^{*}\vee w}(r_{G}|(r^{*}\wedge w,q^{*}\vee q),(r^{*}\vee w,q^{*}\wedge q))

\displaystyle>

\displaystyle r^{*}\overline{G}_{\alpha,r^{*}\vee w}(r^{*}|(r^{*}\wedge w,q^{*}\vee q),(r^{*}\vee w,q^{*}\wedge q))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle r^{*}q^{*}=r_{F}\overline{F}(r_{F}),

which would contradict the optimality of
r_{F}
. Hence
G_{\alpha,r^{*}\vee w}(\cdot|(r^{*}\wedge w,q^{*}\vee q),(r^{*}\vee w,q^{*}\wedge q))
belongs to
\{F\mbox{ in }\pazocal{F}_{\alpha}(w,q):r_{F}=r^{*},q_{F}=q^{*}\}
.

∎

Lemma C-4
.

A pair
(r^{*},q^{*})
in
\mathbb{R}_{+}\times[0,1]
belongs to
{\mathscr{}B}_{\alpha}(w,q)
if and only if it belongs to
{\mathscr{}B}_{l}\cup{\mathscr{}B}_{h}
, where

\displaystyle{\mathscr{}B}_{l}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Bigg\{(r^{*},q^{*})\mbox{ in }[0,w)\times[0,1]:\>q^{*}\geq\max\left\{\Gamma_{\alpha}\left(\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q\right)\frac{r^{*}}{w}\right),\Gamma_{\alpha}\left(\frac{1}{\alpha}\right),\frac{q}{\Gamma_{\alpha}\left(\frac{w}{r^{*}}-1\right)}\right\}\Bigg\},

\displaystyle{\mathscr{}B}_{h}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Bigg\{(r^{*},q^{*})\mbox{ in }[w,+\infty)\times[0,1]:\>q^{*}\leq\Gamma_{\alpha}\left(\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q\right)\frac{r^{*}}{w}\right),\>q^{*}\geq q\Gamma_{\alpha}\left(\frac{1}{\alpha+\frac{w}{r^{*}-w}}\right)\Bigg\}.

Proof of
Lemma

C-4
.

By
Lemma

C-3
, we have that
(r^{*},q^{*})\mbox{ in }{\mathscr{}B}_{\alpha}(w,q)
if and only if the distribution
G_{\alpha,r^{*}\vee w}(\cdot|(r^{*}\wedge w,q^{*}\vee q),(r^{*}\vee w,q^{*}\wedge q))
belongs to
\{F\mbox{ in }\pazocal{F}_{\alpha}(w,q):r_{F}=r^{*},q_{F}=q^{*}\}
.

Case 1:

Suppose
r^{*}<w
. By definition, we have that

\displaystyle v\overline{G}_{\alpha,w}(v|(r^{*},q^{*}),(w,q))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\begin{cases}v\Gamma_{\alpha}\left(\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q^{*}\right)\frac{v}{r^{*}}\right)&\quad\mbox{if }v\in[0,r^{*}],\\
vq^{*}\Gamma_{\alpha}\left(\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(\frac{q}{q^{*}}\right)\frac{v-r^{*}}{w-r^{*}}\right)&\quad\mbox{if }v\in(r^{*},w],\\
0&\quad\mbox{if }v\in[w,\infty).\end{cases}

By applying
Lemma

C-1
to the following parameters
(s,q_{s}):=(r^{*},q^{*})
and
(s^{\prime},q_{s^{\prime}}):=(w,q)
, we have that
G_{\alpha,w}(\cdot|(r^{*},q^{*}),(w,q))
belongs to
\pazocal{F}_{\alpha}(w,q)
and
\pazocal{F}_{\alpha}(r^{*},q^{*})
if and only if
q^{*}=\overline{F}(r^{*})\geq\Gamma_{\alpha}\left(\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q\right)\frac{r^{*}}{w}\right)
.

Furthermore, using
Lemma

A-2
, the revenue function
v\mapsto v\Gamma_{\alpha}\left(\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q\right)\frac{v}{w}\right)
is maximized at
r_{1}=r^{*}/(\alpha\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q^{*}\right))
and the revenue function
v\mapsto vq^{*}\Gamma_{\alpha}\left(\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(\frac{q}{q^{*}}\right)\frac{v-r^{*}}{w-r^{*}}\right)
is maximized at

\displaystyle r_{2}=\frac{1}{\alpha}\left(\frac{w-r^{*}}{\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(\frac{q}{q^{*}}\right)}-(1-\alpha)r^{*}\right).

Thus, when
G_{\alpha,w}(\cdot|(r^{*},q^{*}),(w,q))
belongs to
\pazocal{F}_{\alpha}(w,q)
, the optimal revenue associated with
G_{\alpha,w}(\cdot|(r^{*},q^{*}),(w,q))
is achieved at
r^{*}
if and only if
r_{2}\leq r^{*}\leq r_{1}
. We have
r_{2}\leq r^{*}
if and only if:

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\alpha}\left(\frac{w-r^{*}}{\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(\frac{q}{q^{*}}\right)}-(1-\alpha)r^{*}\right)\leq r^{*}

iff

\displaystyle\frac{w-r^{*}}{\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(\frac{q}{q^{*}}\right)}\leq r^{*}\quad\mbox{iff}\quad\frac{w}{r^{*}}-1\leq\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(\frac{q}{q^{*}}\right)\quad\mbox{iff}\quad q^{*}\geq\frac{q}{\Gamma_{\alpha}\left(\frac{w}{r^{*}}-1\right)},

and
r^{*}\leq r_{1}
if and only if:

\displaystyle r^{*}\leq\frac{r^{*}}{\alpha\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q^{*}\right)}

iff

\displaystyle\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q\right)\leq\frac{1}{\alpha}\quad\mbox{iff}\quad q^{*}\geq\Gamma_{\alpha}\left(\frac{1}{\alpha}\right).

Therefore
r_{2}\leq r^{*}\leq r_{1}
is equivalent to

\displaystyle q^{*}\geq\max\left\{\Gamma_{\alpha}\left(\frac{1}{\alpha}\right),\frac{q}{\Gamma_{\alpha}\left(\frac{w}{r^{*}}-1\right)}\right\}.

We have established that when
r^{*}<w
,
G_{\alpha,w}(\cdot|(r^{*},q^{*}),(w,q))
belongs to
\{F\mbox{ in }\pazocal{F}_{\alpha}(w,q):r_{F}=r^{*},q_{F}=q^{*}\}
if and only if

\displaystyle q^{*}\geq\max\left\{\Gamma_{\alpha}\left(\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q\right)\frac{r^{*}}{w}\right),\Gamma_{\alpha}\left(\frac{1}{\alpha}\right),\frac{q}{\Gamma_{\alpha}\left(\frac{w}{r^{*}}-1\right)}\right\}.

We have hence established that when
r^{*}\leq w
,
(r^{*},q^{*})
belongs to
{\mathscr{}B}_{\alpha}(w,q)
if and only if
(r^{*},q^{*})
belongs to
{\mathscr{}B}_{l}
.

Case 2:

Suppose now
r^{*}\geq w
. By definition, we have that

\displaystyle v\overline{G}_{\alpha,r^{*}}(v|(w,q),(r^{*},q^{*}))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\begin{cases}v\Gamma_{\alpha}\left(\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q\right)\frac{v}{w}\right)&\quad\mbox{if }v\in[0,w],\\
vq\Gamma_{\alpha}\left(\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(\frac{q^{*}}{q}\right)\frac{v-w}{r^{*}-w}\right)&\quad\mbox{if }v\in(w,r^{*}],\\
0&\quad\mbox{if }v\mbox{ in }(r^{*},\infty).\end{cases}

By applying
Lemma

C-1
to the following parameters
(s,q_{s}):=(w,q)
and
(s^{\prime},q_{s^{\prime}}):=(r^{*},q^{*})
, we have that
G_{\alpha,r^{*}}(\cdot|(w,q),(r^{*},q^{*}))
belongs to
\pazocal{F}_{\alpha}(w,q)
and
\pazocal{F}_{\alpha}(w^{*},\{q^{*}\})
if and only if
q=\overline{F}(w)\geq\Gamma_{\alpha}\left(\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q^{*}\right)\frac{w}{r^{*}}\right)
which can be rewritten as
q^{*}\leq\Gamma_{\alpha}\left(\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q\right)\frac{r^{*}}{w}\right)
.

Using
Lemma

A-2
, the oracle price for the revenue function
v\mapsto vq\Gamma_{\alpha}\left(\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(\frac{q^{*}}{q}\right)\frac{v-w}{r^{*}-w}\right)
is achieved at

\displaystyle r^{\prime}=\frac{1}{\alpha}\left(\frac{r^{*}-w}{\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(\frac{q^{*}}{q}\right)}-(1-\alpha)w\right).

Given that
r^{*}
is at the end of its support and that the revenue curve is unimodal, the optimal revenue associated
G_{\alpha,r^{*}}(\cdot|(w,q),(r^{*},q^{*}))
is achieved at
r^{*}
if and only if
r^{*}\leq r^{\prime}
, which, in turn, is true if and only if:

\displaystyle r^{*}\leq\frac{1}{\alpha}\left(\frac{r^{*}-w}{\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(\frac{q^{*}}{q}\right)}-(1-\alpha)w\right)

iff

\displaystyle\alpha r^{*}+(1-\alpha)w\leq\frac{r^{*}-w}{\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(\frac{q^{*}}{q}\right)}

iff

\displaystyle\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(\frac{q^{*}}{q}\right)\leq\frac{r^{*}-w}{w+\alpha(r^{*}-w)}

iff

\displaystyle\Gamma_{\alpha}\left(\frac{1}{\alpha+\frac{w}{r^{*}-w}}\right)\leq\frac{q^{*}}{q}\quad\mbox{iff}\quad q\Gamma_{\alpha}\left(\frac{1}{\alpha+\frac{w}{r^{*}-w}}\right)\leq q^{*}.

We have established that when
r^{*}\geq w
,
G_{\alpha,r^{*}}(\cdot|(w,q),(r^{*},q^{*}))
belongs to
\{F\mbox{ in }\pazocal{F}_{\alpha}(w,q):r_{F}=r^{*},q_{F}=q^{*}\}
if and only if

\displaystyle q\Gamma_{\alpha}\left(\frac{1}{\alpha+\frac{w}{r^{*}-w}}\right)\leq q^{*}\leq\Gamma_{\alpha}\left(\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q\right)\frac{r^{*}}{w}\right).

In turn, we have hence established that when
r^{*}\geq w
,
(r^{*},q^{*})\mbox{ in }{\mathscr{}B}_{\alpha}(w,q)
if and only if
(r^{*},q^{*})\mbox{ in }{\mathscr{}B}_{h}
.
This concludes the proof.
∎

Lemma C-5
.

Let
J_{w,q}=\{r:\mbox{ there exists }q^{*}\mbox{ s.t. }(r^{*},q^{*})\mbox{ in }{\mathscr{}B}_{\alpha}(w,q)\}
. We have
J_{w,q}=[\underline{r}_{\alpha}(w,q),w)\cup[w,\overline{r}_{\alpha}(w,q)]
and for any
r^{*}\mbox{ in }J_{w,q}
, the function
\widetilde{R}_{r^{*},w,q}(\cdot)

is decreasing in the set
\{q^{*}:(r^{*},q^{*})\mbox{ in }{\mathscr{}B}_{\alpha}(w,q)\}
.

Proof of
Lemma

C-5
.

We will show that
J_{w,q}=[\underline{r}_{\alpha}(w,q),w)\cup[w,\overline{r}_{\alpha}(w,q)]
by analyzing the cases when
r^{*}<w
and
r^{*}\geq w
separately.

Suppose first that
r^{*}<w
. In this case, we show that there exists a value
q^{*}
such that
(r^{*},q^{*})\in{\mathscr{}B}_{l}
if and only if
r^{*}\geq\underline{r}_{\alpha}(w,q)
.

If there exists
(r^{*},q^{*})\in{\mathscr{}B}_{l}
then we have y
Lemma

C-4
that
\frac{q}{\Gamma_{\alpha}\left(\frac{w}{r^{*}}-1\right)}\leq q^{*}
and since
q^{*}\leq 1
, we have
\frac{q}{\Gamma_{\alpha}\left(\frac{w}{r^{*}}-1\right)}\leq 1
, which implies that
r^{*}\geq\frac{w}{1+\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q\right)}=\underline{r}_{\alpha}(w,q)
.
Now if
r^{*}\in[\underline{r}_{\alpha}(w,q),w)
, note that
(r^{*},1)\in{\mathscr{}B}_{l}
, as we have seen above that
1\geq\frac{q}{\Gamma_{\alpha}\left(\frac{w}{r^{*}}-1\right)}
. Furthermore, we have

\displaystyle 1\geq\Gamma_{\alpha}\left(\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q\right)\frac{r^{*}}{w}\right)\quad\mbox{and}\quad 1\geq\Gamma_{\alpha}\left(\frac{1}{\alpha}\right)

since
\Gamma_{\alpha}\left(x\right)\leq 1
for all
x\geq 0
.

Now suppose that
r^{*}\geq w
. In this case, we show that there exists a value
q^{*}
such that
(r^{*},q^{*})\in{\mathscr{}B}_{h}
if and only if
\overline{r}_{\alpha}(w,q)\geq w
(which is equivalent to
q\geq\Gamma_{\alpha}\left(\frac{1}{\alpha}\right)
) and
r^{*}\leq\overline{r}_{\alpha}(w,q)
.

If there exists
(r^{*},q^{*})\in{\mathscr{}B}_{h}
then, by
Lemma

C-4
, we have
q\Gamma_{\alpha}\left(\frac{1}{\alpha+\frac{w}{r^{*}-w}}\right)\leq q^{*}\leq\Gamma_{\alpha}\left(\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q\right)\frac{r^{*}}{w}\right)
. Note that we have

\displaystyle q\Gamma_{\alpha}\left(\frac{1}{\alpha+\frac{w}{r^{*}-w}}\right)\leq\Gamma_{\alpha}\left(\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q\right)\frac{r^{*}}{w}\right)

iff

\displaystyle q^{\alpha-1}\left(\Gamma_{\alpha}\left(\frac{1}{\alpha+\frac{w}{r^{*}-w}}\right)\right)^{\alpha-1}\geq\left(\Gamma_{\alpha}\left(\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q\right)\frac{r^{*}}{w}\right)\right)^{\alpha-1}

iff

\displaystyle\left(1+\left(1-\alpha\right)\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q\right)\right)\left(1+\frac{1-\alpha}{\alpha+\frac{w}{r^{*}-w}}\right)\geq 1+(1-\alpha)\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q\right)\frac{r^{*}}{w}

iff

\displaystyle\frac{1-\alpha}{\alpha+\frac{1}{\frac{r^{*}}{w}-1}}+\left(1-\alpha\right)\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q\right)\left(1+\frac{1-\alpha}{\alpha+\frac{1}{\frac{r^{*}}{w}-1}}\right)\geq(1-\alpha)\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q\right)\frac{r^{*}}{w}

iff

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\alpha+\frac{1}{\frac{r^{*}}{w}-1}}+\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q\right)\left(1+\frac{1-\alpha}{\alpha+\frac{1}{\frac{r^{*}}{w}-1}}\right)\geq\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q\right)\frac{r^{*}}{w}

iff

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\alpha+\frac{1}{\frac{r^{*}}{w}-1}}+\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q\right)\left(\frac{1-\alpha}{\alpha+\frac{1}{\frac{r^{*}}{w}-1}}-(\frac{r^{*}}{w}-1)\right)\geq 0

iff

\displaystyle\frac{1+\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q\right)\left(1-\alpha-\alpha\frac{r^{*}}{w}+\alpha\right)}{\alpha+\frac{1}{\frac{r^{*}}{w}-1}}\geq 0

iff

\displaystyle\frac{1-\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q\right)\alpha\frac{r^{*}}{w}}{\alpha+\frac{1}{\frac{r^{*}}{w}-1}}\geq 0

iff

\displaystyle 1-\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q\right)\alpha\frac{r^{*}}{w}\geq 0\quad\mbox{iff}\quad r^{*}\leq\frac{w}{\alpha\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q\right)}=\overline{r}_{\alpha}(w,q).

Additionally, since
r^{*}\geq w
, the above inequality implies that
\overline{r}_{\alpha}(w,q)\geq w
(which in turns implies that
q\geq\Gamma_{\alpha}\left(\frac{1}{\alpha}\right)
).

Now if
q\geq\Gamma_{\alpha}\left(\frac{1}{\alpha}\right)
then
\overline{r}_{\alpha}(w,q)\geq w
and therefore, if
r^{*}\in[w,\overline{r}_{\alpha}(w,q)]
, we always have that
(r^{*},\Gamma_{\alpha}\left(\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q\right)\frac{r^{*}}{w}\right))\in{\mathscr{}B}_{h}
, as we showed above that
q\Gamma_{\alpha}\left(\frac{1}{\alpha+\frac{w}{r^{*}-w}}\right)\leq\Gamma_{\alpha}\left(\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q\right)\frac{r^{*}}{w}\right)
.

Next, we show the
\widetilde{R}_{r^{*},w,q}(\cdot)
monotonicity property by analyzing the cases when
r^{*}<w
and
r^{*}\geq w
separately.

Suppose first that
r^{*}<w
. In this case, we have

\displaystyle\widetilde{R}_{r^{*},w,q}(q^{*})

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{=}}

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{r^{*}}\frac{p}{r^{*}}\frac{\Gamma_{\alpha}\left(\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q^{*}\right)\frac{p}{r^{*}}\right)}{q^{*}}d\Psi(p)+\int_{r^{*}}^{w}\frac{p}{r^{*}}\Gamma_{\alpha}\left(\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(\frac{q}{q^{*}}\right)\frac{p-r^{*}}{w-r^{*}}\right)d\Psi(p)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{r^{*}}\frac{p}{r^{*}}\frac{\Gamma_{\alpha}\left(\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q^{*}\right)\frac{p}{r^{*}}\right)}{\Gamma_{\alpha}\left(\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q^{*}\right)\right)}d\Psi(p)+\int_{r^{*}}^{w}\frac{p}{r^{*}}\Gamma_{\alpha}\left(\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(\frac{q}{q^{*}}\right)\frac{p-r^{*}}{w-r^{*}}\right)d\Psi(p)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{=}}

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{r^{*}}\frac{p}{r^{*}}\left(\Gamma_{\alpha}\left(\frac{1-p/r^{*}}{1/\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q^{*}\right)+(1-\alpha)}\right)\right)^{-1}d\Psi(p)+\int_{r^{*}}^{w}\frac{p}{r^{*}}\Gamma_{\alpha}\left(\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(\frac{q}{q^{*}}\right)\frac{p-r^{*}}{w-r^{*}}\right)d\Psi(p),

where the last equality follows from the fact that for
u\geq v
,
\Gamma_{\alpha}\left(u\right)/\Gamma_{\alpha}\left(v\right)=\Gamma_{\alpha}\left((u-v)/(1+(1-\alpha)v)\right)
.
Each term on the RHS above is decreasing in
q^{*}
, since
\Gamma_{\alpha}\left(\cdot\right)
is decreasing. Hence
\widetilde{R}_{r^{*},w,q}(q^{*})
is decreasing in this case.

Suppose now that
r^{*}\geq w
and
q\geq\Gamma_{\alpha}\left(\frac{1}{\alpha}\right)
so that the interval
[w,\overline{r}_{\alpha}(w,q)]
is non-empty. In this case, we have

\displaystyle\widetilde{R}_{r^{*},w,q}(q^{*})

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{=}}

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{w}\frac{p}{r^{*}}\frac{\Gamma_{\alpha}\left(\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q\right)\frac{p}{w}\right)}{q^{*}}d\Psi(p)+\int_{w}^{r^{*}}\frac{p}{r^{*}}\frac{q\>\Gamma_{\alpha}\left(\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(\frac{q^{*}}{q}\right)\frac{p-w}{r^{*}-w}\right)}{q^{*}}d\Psi(p)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{w}\frac{p}{r^{*}}\frac{\Gamma_{\alpha}\left(\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q\right)\frac{p}{w}\right)}{q^{*}}d\Psi(p)+\int_{w}^{r^{*}}\frac{p}{r^{*}}\frac{\>\Gamma_{\alpha}\left(\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(\frac{q^{*}}{q}\right)\frac{p-w}{r^{*}-w}\right)}{\Gamma_{\alpha}\left(\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(\frac{q^{*}}{q}\right)\right)}d\Psi(p)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{=}}

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{w}\frac{p}{r^{*}}\frac{\Gamma_{\alpha}\left(\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q\right)\frac{p}{w}\right)}{q^{*}}d\Psi(p)+\int_{w}^{r^{*}}\frac{p}{r^{*}}\left(\>\Gamma_{\alpha}\left(\frac{\frac{r^{*}-p}{r^{*}-w}}{1/\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(\frac{q^{*}}{q}\right)+(1-\alpha)\frac{p-w}{r^{*}-w}}\right)\right)^{-1}d\Psi(p),

where the last equality follows from the fact that for
u\geq v
,
\Gamma_{\alpha}\left(u\right)/\Gamma_{\alpha}\left(v\right)=\Gamma_{\alpha}\left((u-v)/(1+(1-\alpha)v)\right)
.
Each term in the above equality is decreasing in
q^{*}
, therefore the result also holds for this case.
This concludes the proof.
∎

Appendix D
Proofs and auxiliary results for
Section

4

We define the following useful notation used throughout this section:

\displaystyle\tilde{v}_{\alpha}

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\begin{cases}1&\quad\mbox{if }\alpha=0,\\
\alpha^{\frac{\alpha}{1-\alpha}}&\quad\mbox{if }\alpha\text{ in }(0,1),\\
e^{-1}&\quad\mbox{if }\alpha=1,\end{cases}

(D-1)

\displaystyle\underline{q}_{\alpha}

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\begin{cases}0&\quad\mbox{if }\alpha=0,\\
\Gamma_{\alpha}\left(\frac{1}{\alpha}\right)&\quad\mbox{if }\alpha\text{ in }(0,1].\end{cases}

(D-2)

One can easily check that, for any
\alpha
in
[0,1]
,
\underline{q}_{\alpha}<\Gamma_{\alpha}\left(\tilde{v}_{\alpha}\right)
.

Proof of
Theorem

2
.

The proof is divided into three separate parts.

In a first step, we simplify the problem given in (
4.1
) in
Section

4
. We show in
Proposition

D-1
that the seller’s posted price has to counter at most 3 worst-case distributions, where two of these are fixed, and the third one is a function of the price selected. In a second step, we analyze the case of regular distributions, and in a third step, we analyze the case of mhr distributions.

Recall the definition of
\underline{q}_{\alpha}
introduced in (
D-2
).

Proposition D-1

(Worst-case Distributions against Deterministic Mechanisms)
.

For any
\alpha\mbox{ in }\left[0,1\right]
, we have

\displaystyle\pazocal{R}\left(\pazocal{P}_{d},\pazocal{F}_{\alpha}(w,q)\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\begin{cases}\sup_{p\in\left[0,1\right]}\min\left\{\frac{Rev\left(p|\overline{F}_{\alpha}\left(\cdot|\mu_{\alpha,q}\left(p\right),(1,q)\right)\right)}{\mbox{opt}\left(F_{\alpha}\left(\cdot|\mu_{\alpha,q}\left(p\right),(1,q)\right)\right)},\frac{Rev\left(p|\delta_{1}\right)}{Rev\left(1|\delta_{1}\right)},\frac{Rev\left(p|\overline{F}_{\alpha}\left(\cdot|\overline{r}_{\alpha}(1,q),(1,q)\right)\right)}{\mbox{opt}\left(F_{\alpha}\left(\cdot|\overline{r}_{\alpha}(1,q),(1,q)\right)\right)}\right\},\quad\mbox{ if }q\in[\underline{q}_{\alpha},1),\\
\sup_{p\in\left[0,1\right]}\min\left\{\frac{Rev\left(p|\overline{F}_{\alpha}\left(\cdot|\mu_{\alpha,q}\left(p\right),(1,q)\right)\right)}{\mbox{opt}\left(F_{\alpha}\left(\cdot|\mu_{\alpha,q}\left(p\right),(1,q)\right)\right)},\frac{Rev\left(p|\delta_{1}\right)}{Rev\left(1|\delta_{1}\right)}\right\},\quad\mbox{ if }q\in(0,\underline{q}_{\alpha}),\end{cases}

with

\displaystyle\mu_{\alpha,q}\left(p\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 1-\frac{\sqrt{\Delta_{\alpha,q}(p)}-\alpha\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q\right)\left(1-p\right)}{2q^{\alpha-1}}

\displaystyle\Delta_{\alpha,q}(p)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(\alpha\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q\right)\left(1-p\right)\right)^{2}+4\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q\right)\left(1-p\right)q^{\alpha-1},

In the above result, the initial price
w
is normalized to 1 without loss of generality. The above establishes that, when restricting attention to deterministic prices, one can restrict attention to worst-case distributions consisting of a GPD distribution with support starting at
\mu_{\alpha,q}\left(p\right)
and truncated at 1, a mass at 1, or a GPD distribution truncated at
\overline{r}_{\alpha}(1,q)
(when
q\geq\underline{q}_{\alpha}
). The proof is deferred to
Section

D.1
.

We now leverage the above reduction to explicitly derive optimal deterministic mechanisms against regular and mhr distributions.

Let us introduce the following functions that represent the ratios of the worst families for
p\geq\underline{r}_{\alpha}(1,q)

\displaystyle R_{1,\alpha}\left(p,q\right)

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\frac{Rev\left(p|\overline{F}_{\alpha}\left(\cdot|\mu_{\alpha,q}\left(p\right),(1,q)\right)\right)}{\mbox{opt}\left(F_{\alpha}\left(\cdot|\mu_{\alpha,q}\left(p\right),(1,q)\right)\right)},

\displaystyle R_{2,\alpha}\left(p,q\right)

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\frac{Rev\left(p|\delta_{1}\right)}{Rev\left(1|\delta_{1}\right)},

\displaystyle R_{3,\alpha}\left(p,q\right)

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\frac{Rev\left(p|\overline{F}_{\alpha}\left(\cdot|\overline{r}_{\alpha}(1,q),(1,q)\right)\right)}{\mbox{opt}\left(F_{\alpha}\left(\cdot|\overline{r}_{\alpha}(1,q),(1,q)\right)\right)},\quad\text{defined only for $q\geq\underline{q}_{\alpha}$}.

We next analyze some properties of the above defined ratios. To that end, recall the definitions of
\tilde{v}_{\alpha}
introduced in (
D-1
) and of
\underline{q}_{\alpha}
in (
D-2
).

Lemma D-1
.

We have the following properties:

1.

If
q\in[\underline{q}_{\alpha},\Gamma_{\alpha}\left(\tilde{v}_{\alpha}\right))
, then there exists
p_{13,\alpha,q}
in
\left[\underline{r}_{\alpha}(1,q),1\right]
such that
R_{1,\alpha}\left(\cdot,q\right)\geq R_{3,\alpha}\left(\cdot,q\right)
in
\left[\underline{r}_{\alpha}(1,q),p_{13,\alpha,q}\right]
and
R_{1,\alpha}\left(\cdot,q\right)\leq R_{3,\alpha}\left(\cdot,q\right)
in
\left[p_{13,\alpha,q},1\right]
. Else, if
q\in\left[\Gamma_{\alpha}\left(\tilde{v}_{\alpha}\right),1\right]
, then
R_{1,\alpha}\left(p,q\right)\geq R_{3,\alpha}\left(p,q\right)
for all
p
in
\left[\underline{r}_{\alpha}(1,q),1\right]
.

2.

For any
q\in(0,1)
, there exists
p_{12,\alpha,q}
such that
R_{1,\alpha}\left(\cdot,q\right)\geq R_{2,\alpha}\left(\cdot,q\right)
in
\left[\underline{r}_{\alpha}(1,q),p_{12,\alpha,q}\right]
and
R_{1,\alpha}\left(\cdot,q\right)\leq R_{2,\alpha}\left(\cdot,q\right)
in
\left[p_{12,\alpha,q},1\right]
.

3.

If
q\geq\underline{q}_{\alpha}
, then we have that
R_{3,\alpha}\left(\cdot,q\right)
is non decreasing in
\left[\underline{r}_{\alpha}(1,q),1\right]
.

4.

For
\alpha\mbox{ in }\{0,1\}
,
R_{1,\alpha}\left(\cdot,q\right)
is non increasing in
\left[\underline{r}_{\alpha}(1,q),1\right]
,
R_{2,\alpha}\left(\cdot,q\right)
is non decreasing in
\left[\underline{r}_{\alpha}(1,q),1\right]
.

Lemma D-2
.

For
\alpha\mbox{ in }\{0,1\}
, there exists a unique
\hat{q}_{\alpha}
in
[\underline{q}_{\alpha},\Gamma_{\alpha}\left(\tilde{v}_{\alpha}\right)]
solution to the equation
p_{13,\alpha,q}=p_{12,\alpha,q}
, and we have for
q
in
[\underline{q}_{\alpha},\Gamma_{\alpha}\left(\tilde{v}_{\alpha}\right)]:\quad p_{13,\alpha,q}\leq p_{12,\alpha,q}\quad\text{if and only if}\quad q\leq\hat{q}_{\alpha}
. Furthermore, we have the following expressions:

\displaystyle\hat{q}_{0}=\frac{1}{4},\quad p_{13,0,q}=1-\frac{\left(1-2q\right)^{2}}{1-q},\quad p_{12,0,q}=1-\frac{\left(1-\sqrt{q}\right)^{2}}{1-q},

\displaystyle\hat{q}_{1}=\hat{q},\quad p_{13,1,q}=\mu_{1,q}^{-1}\left(W\left(\frac{1}{\log\left(q^{-1}\right)}\right)\right),\quad p_{12,1,q}=\mu_{1,q}^{-1}\left(\frac{1}{W\left(\frac{e}{q}\right)}\right),

Where
\hat{q}
is the unique solution in
[0,1]
to the equation
W\left(\frac{1}{\log(q^{-1})}\right)W\left(\frac{e}{q}\right)=1
,
W
is the Lambert function defined as the inverse of
x\to xe^{x}
in
\left[0,+\infty\right)
. Numerically
\hat{q}\in[0.52,0.53]
.

The proof can be found in
Section

D.1
. We proceed by analyzing three main cases
q
in
\left(0,\hat{q}_{\alpha}\right]
,
q
in
\left(\hat{q}_{\alpha},\Gamma_{\alpha}\left(\tilde{v}_{\alpha}\right)\right]
and
q
in
\left(\Gamma_{\alpha}\left(\tilde{v}_{\alpha}\right),1\right)
.

Below, we fix
\alpha
in
\{0,1\}
.

Case
q
in
\left(0,\hat{q}_{\alpha}\right]
:

We analyze the two sub-cases
q
in
(0,\underline{q}_{\alpha})
and
q
in
[\underline{q}_{\alpha},\hat{q}_{\alpha}]
, which both will lead the same final result. Note that the first sub-case is empty for
\alpha=0
.

Sub-case:

\alpha=1
and
q
in
(0,\underline{q}_{\alpha})
: In this case, based on
proposition

D-1
, we have:

\displaystyle\pazocal{R}\left(\pazocal{P}_{d},\pazocal{F}_{\alpha}\left(w,q\right)\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\max\Biggl\{\max_{p\in\left[\underline{r}_{\alpha}(1,q),p_{12,\alpha,q}\right]}\min\left(R_{1,\alpha}\left(p,q\right),R_{2,\alpha}\left(p,q\right)\right),

\displaystyle\max_{p\in\left[p_{12,\alpha,q},1\right]}\min\left(R_{1,\alpha}\left(p,q\right),R_{2,\alpha}\left(p,q\right)\right)\Biggl\}.

Now let us simplify each term.

•

Using
Lemma

D-1
-2, we have that,
R_{1,\alpha}\left(\cdot,q\right)
is above
R_{2,\alpha}\left(\cdot,q\right)
in
\left[\underline{r}_{\alpha}(1,q),p_{12,\alpha,q}\right]
, therefore:

\displaystyle\max_{p\in\left[\underline{r}_{\alpha}(1,q),p_{12,\alpha,q}\right]}\min\left(R_{1,\alpha}\left(p,q\right),R_{2,\alpha}\left(p,q\right)\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\max_{p\in\left[\underline{r}_{\alpha}(1,q),p_{12,\alpha,q}\right]}R_{2,\alpha}\left(p,q\right)\overset{\left(a\right)}{=}R_{2,\alpha}\left(p_{12,\alpha,q},q\right),

where in (a), we used the result in
Lemma

D-1
-4 that states that
R_{2,\alpha}\left(\cdot,q\right)
is non decreasing in
\left[\underline{r}_{\alpha}(1,q),1\right]
.

•

Using
Lemma

D-1
-2, we have that,
R_{1,\alpha}\left(\cdot,q\right)
is below
R_{2,\alpha}\left(\cdot,q\right)
in
\left[p_{12,\alpha,q},1\right]
, therefore:

\displaystyle\max_{p\in\left[p_{12,\alpha,q},1\right]}\min\left(R_{1,\alpha}\left(p,q\right),R_{2,\alpha}\left(p,q\right)\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\max_{p\in\left[p_{12,\alpha,q},1\right]}R_{1,\alpha}\left(p,q\right)\overset{\left(b\right)}{=}R_{1,\alpha}\left(p_{12,\alpha,q},q\right),

where in (b), we used the result in
Lemma

D-1
-4 that states that
R_{1,\alpha}\left(\cdot,q\right)
is non increasing in
\left[\underline{r}_{\alpha}(1,q),1\right]
.

Therefore, we have

\displaystyle\pazocal{R}\left(\pazocal{P}_{d},\pazocal{F}_{\alpha}\left(w,q\right)\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\max\{R_{2,\alpha}\left(p_{12,\alpha,q},q\right),R_{1,\alpha}\left(p_{12,\alpha,q},q\right)\}\overset{\left(c\right)}{=}p_{12,\alpha,q},

where in (c), we used the fact that, by definition,
R_{2,\alpha}\left(p_{12,\alpha,q},q\right)=R_{1,\alpha}\left(p_{12,\alpha,q},q\right)
. We also note that the value above is achieved at
p=p_{12,\alpha,q}
.

Sub-case:

q
in
[\underline{q}_{\alpha},\hat{q}_{\alpha}]
: In this case, based on
Lemma

D-2
, we have that
p_{13,\alpha,q}\leq p_{12,\alpha,q},
thus we have

\displaystyle\pazocal{R}\left(\pazocal{P}_{d},\pazocal{F}_{\alpha}\left(w,q\right)\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\max\Biggl\{\max_{p\in\left[\underline{r}_{\alpha}(1,q),p_{13,\alpha,q}\right]}\min\left(R_{1,\alpha}\left(p,q\right),R_{2,\alpha}\left(p,q\right),R_{3,\alpha}\left(p,q\right)\right),

\displaystyle\max_{p\in\left[p_{13,\alpha,q},p_{12,\alpha,q}\right]}\min\left(R_{1,\alpha}\left(p,q\right),R_{2,\alpha}\left(p,q\right),R_{3,\alpha}\left(p,q\right)\right),

\displaystyle\max_{p\in\left[p_{12,\alpha,q},1\right]}\min\left(R_{1,\alpha}\left(p,q\right),R_{2,\alpha}\left(p,q\right),R_{3,\alpha}\left(p,q\right)\right)\Biggl\}.

Now let us simplify each term.

•

Using
Lemma

D-1
-1, we have that, for
q\in[\underline{q}_{\alpha},\hat{q}_{\alpha}]\subseteq[\underline{q}_{\alpha},\Gamma_{\alpha}\left(\tilde{v}_{\alpha}\right)]
,
R_{1,\alpha}\left(\cdot,q\right)
is above
R_{3,\alpha}\left(\cdot,q\right)
in
\left[\underline{r}_{\alpha}(1,q),p_{13,\alpha,q}\right]
, therefore:

\displaystyle\max_{p\in\left[\underline{r}_{\alpha}(1,q),p_{13,\alpha,q}\right]}\min\left(R_{1,\alpha}\left(p,q\right),R_{2,\alpha}\left(p,q\right),R_{3,\alpha}\left(p,q\right)\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\max_{p\in\left[\underline{r}_{\alpha}(1,q),p_{13,\alpha,q}\right]}\min\left(R_{2,\alpha}\left(p,q\right),R_{3,\alpha}\left(p,q\right)\right).

•

Using
Lemma

D-1
-1 and
Lemma

D-1
-2, we have that both
R_{3,\alpha}\left(p,q\right)\geq R_{1,\alpha}\left(p,q\right)\geq R_{2,\alpha}\left(p,q\right)
in
\left[p_{13,\alpha,q},p_{12,\alpha,q}\right]
, therefore:

\displaystyle\max_{p\in\left[p_{13,\alpha,q},p_{12,\alpha,q}\right]}\min\left(R_{1,\alpha}\left(p,q\right),R_{2,\alpha}\left(p,q\right),R_{3,\alpha}\left(p,q\right)\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\max_{p\in\left[p_{13,\alpha,q},p_{12,\alpha,q}\right]}R_{2,\alpha}\left(p,q\right).

•

Using
Lemma

D-1
-1 and
Lemma

D-1
-2, we have that both
R_{2,\alpha}\left(\cdot,q\right)
and
R_{3,\alpha}\left(\cdot,q\right)
are above
R_{1,\alpha}\left(\cdot,q\right)
in
\left[p_{12,\alpha,q},1\right]
, therefore:

\displaystyle\max_{p\in\left[p_{12,\alpha,q},1\right]}\min\left(R_{1,\alpha}\left(p,q\right),R_{2,\alpha}\left(p,q\right),R_{3,\alpha}\left(p,q\right)\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\max_{p\in\left[p_{12,\alpha,q},1\right]}R_{1,\alpha}\left(p,q\right).

Therefore, we have

\displaystyle\pazocal{R}\left(\pazocal{P}_{d},\pazocal{F}_{\alpha}\left(w,q\right)\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\max\Biggl\{\max_{p\in\left[\underline{r}_{\alpha}(1,q),p_{13,\alpha,q}\right]}\min\left(R_{2,\alpha}\left(p,q\right),R_{3,\alpha}\left(p,q\right)\right),\max_{p\in\left[p_{13,\alpha,q},p_{12,\alpha,q}\right]}R_{2,\alpha}\left(p,q\right),

\displaystyle\max_{p\in\left[p_{12,\alpha,q},1\right]}R_{1,\alpha}\left(p,q\right)\Biggl\}

\displaystyle\overset{\left(a\right)}{=}

\displaystyle\max\Biggl\{\max_{p\in\left[p_{13,\alpha,q},p_{12,\alpha,q}\right]}R_{2,\alpha}\left(p,q\right),\max_{p\in\left[p_{12,\alpha,q},1\right]}R_{1,\alpha}\left(p,q\right)\Biggl\},

where in (a), we used the fact that

\displaystyle\max_{p\in\left[\underline{r}_{\alpha}(1,q),p_{13,\alpha,q}\right]}\min\left(R_{2,\alpha}\left(p,q\right),R_{3,\alpha}\left(p,q\right)\right)\leq\max_{p\in\left[\underline{r}_{\alpha}(1,q),p_{13,\alpha,q}\right]}R_{2,\alpha}\left(p,q\right)\overset{\left(b\right)}{\leq}\max_{p\in\left[p_{13,\alpha,q},p_{12,\alpha,q}\right]}R_{2,\alpha}\left(p,q\right),

and in (b), we used the result in
Lemma

D-1
-4 that states that
R_{2,\alpha}\left(\cdot,q\right)
is non decreasing in
\left[\underline{r}_{\alpha}(1,q),1\right]
.

Using the latter and also the fact that
R_{1,\alpha}\left(\cdot,q\right)
is non increasing in
\left[\underline{r}_{\alpha}(1,q),1\right]
we have

\displaystyle\pazocal{R}\left(\pazocal{P}_{d},\pazocal{F}_{\alpha}\left(w,q\right)\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\max\{R_{2,\alpha}\left(p_{12,\alpha,q},q\right),R_{1,\alpha}\left(p_{12,\alpha,q},q\right)\}

\displaystyle\overset{\left(c\right)}{=}

\displaystyle p_{12,\alpha,q},

where in (c), we used the fact that, by definition,
R_{2,\alpha}\left(p_{12,\alpha,q},q\right)=R_{1,\alpha}\left(p_{12,\alpha,q},q\right)
. We also note that the value above is achieved at
p=p_{12,\alpha,q}
.

We conclude that, for
q
in
\left(0,\hat{q}_{\alpha}\right]
,
\pazocal{R}\left(\pazocal{P}_{d},\pazocal{F}_{\alpha}\left(w,q\right)\right)=p_{12,\alpha,q}
and is achieved at
p=p_{12,\alpha,q}
.
For
\alpha=0
, using the expressions in
Lemma

D-2
, we obtain
\left(0,\hat{q}_{\alpha}\right]\overset{\alpha=0}{=}(0,\frac{1}{4}]
and:

\displaystyle\pazocal{R}\left(\pazocal{P}_{d},\pazocal{F}_{\alpha}\left(w,q\right)\right)

\displaystyle\overset{\alpha=0}{=}

\displaystyle p_{12,0,q}=1-\frac{\left(1-\sqrt{q}\right)^{2}}{1-q},\quad\mbox{which is achieved at $p=1-\frac{\left(1-\sqrt{q}\right)^{2}}{1-q}$}.

For
\alpha=1
, using the expressions in
Lemma

D-2
, we obtain
\left(0,\hat{q}_{\alpha}\right]\overset{\alpha=1}{=}(0,\hat{q}]
and:

\displaystyle\pazocal{R}\left(\pazocal{P}_{d},\pazocal{F}_{\alpha}\left(w,q\right)\right)

\displaystyle\overset{\alpha=1}{=}

\displaystyle p_{12,1,q}=\mu_{1,q}^{-1}\left(\frac{1}{W\left(\frac{e}{q}\right)}\right),\quad\mbox{which is achieved at $p=\mu_{1,q}^{-1}\left(\frac{1}{W\left(\frac{e}{q}\right)}\right)$}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 1-\frac{1}{\log\left(q^{-1}\right)}\left(W\left(\frac{e}{q}\right)+\frac{1}{W\left(\frac{e}{q}\right)}-2\right):=\beta_{q}\left(\frac{e}{q}\right).

Case
q
in
\left(\hat{q}_{\alpha},\Gamma_{\alpha}\left(\tilde{v}_{\alpha}\right)\right]:

In this case, based on
Lemma

D-2
, we have that
p_{12,\alpha,q}\leq p_{13,\alpha,q},
thus we have

\displaystyle\pazocal{R}\left(\pazocal{P}_{d},\pazocal{F}_{\alpha}\left(w,q\right)\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\max\Biggl\{\max_{p\in\left[\underline{r}_{\alpha}(1,q),p_{12,\alpha,q}\right]}\min\left(R_{1,\alpha}\left(p,q\right),R_{2,\alpha}\left(p,q\right),R_{3,\alpha}\left(p,q\right)\right),

\displaystyle\max_{p\in\left[p_{12,\alpha,q},p_{13,\alpha,q}\right]}\min\left(R_{1,\alpha}\left(p,q\right),R_{2,\alpha}\left(p,q\right),R_{3,\alpha}\left(p,q\right)\right),

\displaystyle\max_{p\in\left[p_{13,\alpha,q},1\right]}\min\left(R_{1,\alpha}\left(p,q\right),R_{2,\alpha}\left(p,q\right),R_{3,\alpha}\left(p,q\right)\right)\Biggl\}.

Now let us simplify each term.

•

Using
Lemma

D-1
-1, we have that, for
q\in\left(\hat{q}_{\alpha},\Gamma_{\alpha}\left(\tilde{v}_{\alpha}\right)\right]\subseteq[\underline{q}_{\alpha},\Gamma_{\alpha}\left(\tilde{v}_{\alpha}\right)]
,
R_{1,\alpha}\left(\cdot,q\right)\geq R_{3,\alpha}\left(\cdot,q\right)
in
\left[\underline{r}_{\alpha}(1,q),p_{13,\alpha,q}\right]
, therefore:

\displaystyle\max_{p\in\left[\underline{r}_{\alpha}(1,q),p_{12,\alpha,q}\right]}\min\left(R_{1,\alpha}\left(p,q\right),R_{2,\alpha}\left(p,q\right),R_{3,\alpha}\left(p,q\right)\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\max_{p\in\left[\underline{r}_{\alpha}(1,q),p_{12,\alpha,q}\right]}\min\left(R_{2,\alpha}\left(p,q\right),R_{3,\alpha}\left(p,q\right)\right).

•

Using
Lemma

D-1
-1 and
Lemma

D-1
-2, we have that both
R_{2,\alpha}\left(p,q\right)\geq R_{1,\alpha}\left(p,q\right)\geq R_{3,\alpha}\left(p,q\right)
in
\left[p_{12,\alpha,q},p_{13,\alpha,q}\right]
, therefore:

\displaystyle\max_{p\in\left[p_{12,\alpha,q},p_{13,\alpha,q}\right]}\min\left(R_{1,\alpha}\left(p,q\right),R_{2,\alpha}\left(p,q\right),R_{3,\alpha}\left(p,q\right)\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\max_{p\in\left[p_{12,\alpha,q},p_{13,\alpha,q}\right]}R_{3,\alpha}\left(p,q\right).

•

Using
Lemma

D-1
-1 and
Lemma

D-1
-2, we have that both
R_{2,\alpha}\left(\cdot,q\right)
and
R_{3,\alpha}\left(\cdot,q\right)
are above
R_{1,\alpha}\left(\cdot,q\right)
in
\left[p_{13,\alpha,q},1\right]
, therefore:

\displaystyle\max_{p\in\left[p_{13,\alpha,q},1\right]}\min\left(R_{1,\alpha}\left(p,q\right),R_{2,\alpha}\left(p,q\right),R_{3,\alpha}\left(p,q\right)\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\max_{p\in\left[p_{13,\alpha,q},1\right]}R_{1,\alpha}\left(p,q\right).

Therefore, we have

\displaystyle\pazocal{R}\left(\pazocal{P}_{d},\pazocal{F}_{\alpha}\left(w,q\right)\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\max\Biggl\{\max_{p\in\left[\underline{r}_{\alpha}(1,q),p_{12,\alpha,q}\right]}\min\left(R_{2,\alpha}\left(p,q\right),R_{3,\alpha}\left(p,q\right)\right),\max_{p\in\left[p_{12,\alpha,q},p_{13,\alpha,q}\right]}R_{3,\alpha}\left(p,q\right),

\displaystyle\max_{p\in\left[p_{13,\alpha,q},1\right]}R_{1,\alpha}\left(p,q\right)\Biggl\}

\displaystyle\overset{\left(a\right)}{=}

\displaystyle\max\Biggl\{\max_{p\in\left[p_{12,\alpha,q},p_{13,\alpha,q}\right]}R_{3,\alpha}\left(p,q\right),\max_{p\in\left[p_{13,\alpha,q},1\right]}R_{1,\alpha}\left(p,q\right)\Biggl\},

where in (a), we used the fact that

\displaystyle\max_{p\in\left[\underline{r}_{\alpha}(1,q),p_{12,\alpha,q}\right]}\min\left(R_{2,\alpha}\left(p,q\right),R_{3,\alpha}\left(p,q\right)\right)\leq\max_{p\in\left[\underline{r}_{\alpha}(1,q),p_{12,\alpha,q}\right]}R_{3,\alpha}\left(p,q\right)\overset{\left(b\right)}{\leq}\max_{p\in\left[p_{12,\alpha,q},p_{13,\alpha,q}\right]}R_{3,\alpha}\left(p,q\right),

and in (b), we used the result in
Lemma

D-1
-3 that states that
R_{3,\alpha}\left(\cdot,q\right)
is non decreasing in
\left[\underline{r}_{\alpha}(1,q),1\right]
. Therefore, using the property
Lemma

D-1
-4, we have, additionally, that
R_{1,\alpha}\left(\cdot,q\right)
is non increasing in
\left[\underline{r}_{\alpha}(1,q),1\right]
and therefore

\displaystyle\pazocal{R}\left(\pazocal{P}_{d},\pazocal{F}_{\alpha}\left(w,q\right)\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\max\{R_{3,\alpha}\left(p_{13,\alpha,q},q\right),R_{1,\alpha}\left(p_{13,\alpha,q},q\right)\}

\displaystyle\overset{\left(c\right)}{=}

\displaystyle R_{3,\alpha}\left(p_{13,\alpha,q},q\right),\quad\mbox{which is achieved at $p=p_{13,\alpha,q}$}.

where in (c), we used the fact that
R_{3,\alpha}\left(p_{13,\alpha,q},q\right)=R_{1,\alpha}\left(p_{13,\alpha,q},q\right)
.

For
\alpha=0
, using the expressions in
Lemma

D-2
, we obtain
(\hat{q}_{\alpha},\Gamma_{\alpha}\left(\tilde{v}_{\alpha}\right)]\overset{\alpha=0}{=}(\frac{1}{4},\frac{1}{2}]
and:

\displaystyle\pazocal{R}\left(\pazocal{P}_{d},\pazocal{F}_{\alpha}\left(w,q\right)\right)

\displaystyle\overset{\alpha=0}{=}

\displaystyle R_{3,0}\left(p_{13,0,q},q\right)=\frac{3-4q}{4\left(1-q\right)},\quad\mbox{which is achieved at $p_{13,0,q}=\frac{q\left(3-4q\right)}{1-q}$}.

For
\alpha=1
, using the expressions in
Lemma

D-2
, we obtain
(\hat{q}_{\alpha},\Gamma_{\alpha}\left(\tilde{v}_{\alpha}\right)]\overset{\alpha=1}{=}(\hat{q},e^{-e^{-1}}]
and:

\displaystyle\pazocal{R}\left(\pazocal{P}_{d},\pazocal{F}_{\alpha}\left(w,q\right)\right)

\displaystyle\overset{\alpha=1}{=}

\displaystyle R_{3,1}\left(p_{13,1,q},q\right)=\mu_{1,q}^{-1}\left(W\left(\frac{1}{\log\left(q^{-1}\right)}\right)\right)e\log\left(q^{-1}\right)e^{-\log\left(q^{-1}\right)\mu_{1,q}^{-1}\left(W\left(\frac{1}{\log\left(q^{-1}\right)}\right)\right)}:=\rho\left(q\right)

\displaystyle\mbox{which is achieved at $p=\mu_{1,q}^{-1}\left(W\left(\frac{1}{\log\left(q^{-1}\right)}\right)\right)=\beta_{q}\left(\frac{1}{\log\left(q^{-1}\right)}\right)$}.

Case
q
in
(\Gamma_{\alpha}\left(\tilde{v}_{\alpha}\right),1)
: Using
Lemma

D-1
-1, we have that for
q
in
(\Gamma_{\alpha}\left(\tilde{v}_{\alpha}\right),1)
,
R_{1,\alpha}\left(\cdot,q\right)
is above
R_{3,\alpha}\left(\cdot,q\right)
in
\left[\underline{r}_{\alpha}(1,q),1\right]
. Therefore, we have

\displaystyle\pazocal{R}\left(\pazocal{P}_{d},\pazocal{F}_{\alpha}\left(w,q\right)\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\max_{p\in\left[\underline{r}_{\alpha}(1,q),1\right]}\min\left(R_{1,\alpha}\left(p,q\right),R_{2,\alpha}\left(p,q\right),R_{3,\alpha}\left(p,q\right)\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\max_{p\in\left[\underline{r}_{\alpha}(1,q),1\right]}\min\left(R_{2,\alpha}\left(p,q\right),R_{3,\alpha}\left(p,q\right)\right)

\displaystyle\overset{\left(a\right)}{=}

\displaystyle\min\left(R_{2,\alpha}\left(1,q\right),R_{3,\alpha}\left(1,q\right)\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\min\left(1,R_{3,\alpha}\left(1,q\right)\right)\overset{\left(b\right)}{=}R_{3,\alpha}\left(1,q\right).

In (a), we used the results in
Lemma

D-1
-3 that states that
R_{3,\alpha}\left(\cdot,q\right)
is non decreasing in
\left[\underline{r}_{\alpha}(1,q),1\right]
and
Lemma

D-1
-4, that states that
R_{2,\alpha}\left(\cdot,q\right)
is non decreasing in
\left[\underline{r}_{\alpha}(1,q),1\right]
as in this case
q\geq\Gamma_{\alpha}\left(\tilde{v}_{\alpha}\right)\geq\underline{q}_{\alpha}
. In (b), we used the fact that
R_{3,\alpha}\left(1,q\right)\leq 1
.

For
\alpha=0
, using the expressions in
Lemma

D-2
, we obtain
(\Gamma_{\alpha}\left(\tilde{v}_{\alpha}\right),1)\overset{\alpha=0}{=}(\frac{1}{2},1)
and:

\displaystyle\pazocal{R}\left(\pazocal{P}_{d},\pazocal{F}_{\alpha}\left(w,\{q\}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\overset{\alpha=0}{=}

\displaystyle R_{3,0}\left(1,q\right)=1-q,\quad\mbox{which is achieved at $p=1$}.

For
\alpha=1
, using the expressions in
Lemma

D-2
, we obtain
(\Gamma_{\alpha}\left(\tilde{v}_{\alpha}\right),1)\overset{\alpha=1}{=}(e^{-e^{-1}},1)
and:

\displaystyle\pazocal{R}\left(\pazocal{P}_{d},\pazocal{F}_{\alpha}\left(w,q\right)\right)

\displaystyle\overset{\alpha=1}{=}

\displaystyle R_{3,1}\left(1,q\right)=eq\log\left(q^{-1}\right),\quad\mbox{which is achieved at $p=1$}.

This completes the proof of
Theorem

2
.
∎

D.1
Proofs of auxiliary results

Proof of
Proposition

D-1
.

Following the reasoning in
Section

4
in Eq.(
4.1
), we have that:

\displaystyle\pazocal{R}\left(\pazocal{P}_{d},\pazocal{F}_{\alpha}(w,q)\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sup_{p\in[0,w]}\min\left\{\min_{r\in[\underline{r}_{\alpha}(w,q),p)}\frac{p\overline{F}_{\alpha}(p|r,(w,q))}{r},\frac{p}{w},\min_{r\in[w,\overline{r}_{\alpha}(w,q)]}\frac{p\overline{F}_{\alpha}(p|r,(w,q))}{\mbox{opt}(F_{\alpha}(\cdot|r,(w,q)))}\right\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sup_{\frac{p}{w}\in\left[0,1\right]}\min\Biggl\{\inf_{\frac{r}{w}\in[\underline{r}_{\alpha}(1,q),\frac{p}{w})}\frac{\frac{p}{w}\overline{F}_{\alpha}(p/w|r/w,(1,q))}{\frac{r}{w}},\frac{p}{w},\inf_{\frac{r}{w}\in\left[1,\overline{r}_{\alpha}(1,q)\right]}\frac{\frac{p}{w}\overline{F}_{\alpha}(p/w|r/w,(1,q))}{\frac{r}{w}\overline{F}_{\alpha}(r/w|r/w,(1,q))}\Biggr\}

\displaystyle\overset{\left(a\right)}{=}

\displaystyle\sup_{\tilde{p}\in\left[0,1\right]}\min\Biggl\{\inf_{\tilde{r}\in[\underline{r}_{\alpha}(1,q),\tilde{p})}\frac{\tilde{p}\overline{F}_{\alpha}\left(\tilde{p}|\tilde{r},(1,q)\right)}{\tilde{r}},\tilde{p},\inf_{\tilde{r}\in\left[1,\overline{r}_{\alpha}(1,q)\right]}\frac{\tilde{p}\overline{F}_{\alpha}\left(\tilde{p}|\tilde{r},(1,q)\right)}{\tilde{r}\overline{F}_{\alpha}\left(\tilde{r}|\tilde{r},(1,q)\right)}\Biggr\},

where in
(a)
we used two changes of variables to remove the dependency on
w
, namely
\tilde{p}=p/w
and
\tilde{r}=r/w
. Note that when
q<\underline{q}_{\alpha}
, the last term in the brackets does not affect the worst-case. Thus we conclude that

\displaystyle\pazocal{R}\left(\pazocal{P}_{d},\pazocal{F}_{\alpha}(w,q)\right)=\sup_{p\in\left[0,1\right]}\min\Biggl\{\inf_{r\in[\underline{r}_{\alpha}(1,q),p)}\frac{p\overline{F}_{\alpha}\left(p|r,(1,q)\right)}{r},p,\inf_{r\in\left[1,\overline{r}_{\alpha}(1,q)\right]}\frac{p\overline{F}_{\alpha}\left(p|r,(1,q)\right)}{r\overline{F}_{\alpha}\left(r|r,(1,q)\right)}\Biggr\}.

(D-3)

For each (normalized) price
p
in
[0,1]
, we have three terms that determine the worst case performance. We analyze each term separately. The second term is just the identity stemming from nature selecting a point mass at
1
. We next analyze the first and third terms with the brackets in (
D-3
).

Third term.

The third term is only present if
q\geq\underline{q}_{\alpha}
(ensuring that
\left[1,\overline{r}_{\alpha}(1,q)\right]\neq\emptyset
). In this case, for any
p
in
[0,1]
and , the third term can be shown to be equal to

\displaystyle\inf_{r\in\left[1,\overline{r}_{\alpha}(1,q)\right]}\frac{p\overline{F}_{\alpha}\left(p|r,(1,q)\right)}{r\overline{F}_{\alpha}\left(r|r,(1,q)\right)}=\frac{p\overline{F}_{\alpha}\left(p|\overline{r}_{\alpha}(1,q),(1,q)\right)}{\sup_{r\in\left[1,\overline{r}_{\alpha}(1,q)\right]}r\overline{F}_{\alpha}\left(r|r,(1,q)\right)}.

Indeed, fix
q\geq\underline{q}_{\alpha}
. For any
r\in\left[1,\overline{r}_{\alpha}(1,q)\right]
,
\overline{F}_{\alpha}\left(p|r,(1,q)\right)=\Gamma_{\alpha}\left(\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q\right)p\right)=\overline{F}_{\alpha}\left(p|\overline{r}_{\alpha}(1,q),(1,q)\right)
. By
Lemma

A-2
applied to
\beta:=\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q\right)
and
w^{\prime}:=0
, we have that the function
v\to v\overline{F}_{\alpha}\left(v|\overline{r}_{\alpha}(1,q),(1,q)\right)
is maximized at
\overline{r}_{\alpha}(1,q)
thus it achieves its maximum at
\overline{r}_{\alpha}(1,q)
on the interval
[1,\overline{r}_{\alpha}(1,q)].
Hence we get that

\displaystyle\inf_{r\in\left[1,\overline{r}_{\alpha}(1,q)\right]}\frac{p\overline{F}_{\alpha}\left(p|r,(1,q)\right)}{r\overline{F}_{\alpha}\left(r|r,(1,q)\right)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\inf_{r\in\left[1,\overline{r}_{\alpha}(1,q)\right]}\frac{p\overline{F}_{\alpha}\left(p|\overline{r}_{\alpha}(1,q),(1,q)\right)}{r\overline{F}_{\alpha}\left(r|\overline{r}_{\alpha}(1,q)\left(1,q\right)\right)}=\frac{p\overline{F}_{\alpha}\left(p|\overline{r}_{\alpha}(1,q),(1,q)\right)}{\sup_{r\in\left[1,\overline{r}_{\alpha}(1,q)\right]}r\overline{F}_{\alpha}\left(r|\overline{r}_{\alpha}(1,q)\left(1,q\right)\right)}

(D-4)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{p\overline{F}_{\alpha}\left(p|\overline{r}_{\alpha}(1,q),(1,q)\right)}{\mbox{opt}(F_{\alpha}\left(\cdot|\overline{r}_{\alpha}(1,q),\left(1,q\right)\right))}.

One can easily check that

\displaystyle\mbox{opt}(F_{\alpha}\left(\cdot|\overline{r}_{\alpha}(1,q),\left(1,q\right)\right))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\lim_{v\to\overline{r}_{\alpha}(1,q)}v\overline{F}_{\alpha}\left(v|\overline{r}_{\alpha}(1,q),\left(1,q\right)\right)=\frac{\tilde{v}_{\alpha}}{\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q\right)},

where
\tilde{v}_{\alpha}
was defined in (
D-1
).

First term.

For any
p
in
[0,1],
the first term in (
D-3
) can be rewritten as

\displaystyle\inf_{r\in[\underline{r}_{\alpha}(1,q),p)}\frac{p\overline{F}_{\alpha}\left(p|r,(1,q)\right)}{r}=\inf_{r\in[\underline{r}_{\alpha}(1,q),p)}\Phi(r),

where

\displaystyle\Phi(r):=\frac{p}{r}\Gamma_{\alpha}\left(\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q\right)\frac{p-r}{1-r}\right).

We study the function
\Phi(\cdot)
and by analyzing its derivative, determine exactly where its minimum is achieved. In particular, we will establish the following claim.
On
[\underline{r}_{\alpha}(1,q),p)
, the function
\Phi(\cdot)
is minimized at

\displaystyle\mu_{\alpha,q}\left(p\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 1-\frac{\sqrt{\left(\alpha\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q\right)\left(1-p\right)\right)^{2}+4\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q\right)\left(1-p\right)q^{\alpha-1}}-\alpha\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q\right)\left(1-p\right)}{2q^{\alpha-1}}.

For any
p
in
[0,1],
at any
r
in
[\underline{r}_{\alpha}(1,q),p)
,
\Phi(\cdot)
is differentiable with derivative given by

\displaystyle\frac{d\Phi}{dr}(r)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{p}{r^{2}}\Gamma_{\alpha}\left(\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q\right)\frac{p-r}{1-r}\right)-\frac{p}{r}\left(\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q\right)\frac{-(1-r)+(p-r)}{(1-r)^{2}}\right)\left(\Gamma_{\alpha}\left(\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q\right)\frac{p-r}{1-r}\right)\right)^{2-\alpha}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{p}{r^{2}}\Gamma_{\alpha}\left(\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q\right)\frac{p-r}{1-r}\right)-\frac{p}{r}\left(\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q\right)\frac{-(1-p)}{(1-r)^{2}}\right)\left(\Gamma_{\alpha}\left(\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q\right)\frac{p-r}{1-r}\right)\right)^{2-\alpha}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{p}{r^{2}(1-r)^{2}}\left(\Gamma_{\alpha}\left(\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q\right)\frac{p-r}{1-r}\right)\right)^{2-\alpha}\left[\left(\Gamma_{\alpha}\left(\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q\right)\frac{p-r}{1-r}\right)\right)^{-(1-\alpha)}(1-r)^{2}-\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q\right)r(1-p)\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{p}{r^{2}(1-r)^{2}}\left(\Gamma_{\alpha}\left(\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q\right)\frac{p-r}{1-r}\right)\right)^{2-\alpha}\left[\left(1+(1-\alpha)\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q\right)\frac{p-r}{1-r}\right)(1-r)^{2}-\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q\right)r(1-p)\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{p}{r^{2}(1-r)^{2}}\left(\Gamma_{\alpha}\left(\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q\right)\frac{p-r}{1-r}\right)\right)^{2-\alpha}\left[(1-r)^{2}+(1-\alpha)\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q\right)(p-r)(1-r)-\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q\right)r(1-p)\right].

Note that the sign of the derivative of
\Phi
is determined by that of the quadratic

\displaystyle\varphi(r)

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle-\left[(1-r)^{2}+(1-\alpha)\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q\right)(p-r)(1-r)-\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q\right)r(1-p)\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\Biggl[\left(1+(1-\alpha)\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q\right)\right)(1-r)^{2}-(1-\alpha)\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q\right)(1-p)(1-r)

\displaystyle+\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q\right)(1-r)(1-p)-\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q\right)(1-p)\Biggl]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\left[\left(1+(1-\alpha)\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q\right)\right)(1-r)^{2}+\alpha\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q\right)(1-p)(1-r)-\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q\right)(1-p)\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\left[q^{\alpha-1}(1-r)^{2}+\alpha\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q\right)(1-p)(1-r)-\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q\right)(1-p)\right].

Let

\displaystyle\Delta_{\alpha,q}(p)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(\alpha\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q\right)(1-p)\right)^{2}+4q^{\alpha-1}\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q\right)(1-p).

The above is positive and hence the quadratic
\varphi(r)
admits two roots given by

\displaystyle r_{1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 1+\frac{\alpha\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q\right)(1-p)+\sqrt{\Delta_{\alpha,q}(p)}}{2q^{\alpha-1}},

\displaystyle r_{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 1+\frac{\alpha\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q\right)(1-p)-\sqrt{\Delta_{\alpha,q}(p)}}{2q^{\alpha-1}}.

It is clear that
r_{1}\geq 1
. We next establish that
r_{2}
belongs to
\left[\underline{r}_{\alpha}(1,q),p\right].

\displaystyle p-r_{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle p-1-\frac{\alpha\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q\right)(1-p)-\sqrt{\Delta_{\alpha,q}(p)}}{2q^{\alpha-1}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{-(2q^{\alpha-1}+\alpha\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q\right))(1-p)+\sqrt{\Delta_{\alpha,q}(p)}}{2q^{\alpha-1}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{-\left(4q^{2(\alpha-1)}+\left(\alpha\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q\right)\right)^{2}+4q^{\alpha-1}\alpha\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q\right)\right)(1-p)^{2}+\left(\alpha\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q\right)(1-p)\right)^{2}+4q^{\alpha-1}\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q\right)(1-p)}{2q^{\alpha-1}((2q^{\alpha-1}+\alpha\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q\right))(1-p)+\sqrt{\Delta_{\alpha,q}(p)})}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 4(1-p)q^{\alpha-1}\frac{-\left(q^{(\alpha-1)}+\alpha\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q\right)\right)(1-p)+\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q\right)}{2q^{\alpha-1}((2q^{\alpha-1}+\alpha\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q\right))(1-p)+\sqrt{\Delta_{\alpha,q}(p)})}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 4(1-p)q^{\alpha-1}\frac{-(1+\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q\right))(1-p)+\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q\right)}{2q^{\alpha-1}((2q^{\alpha-1}+\alpha\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q\right))(1-p)+\sqrt{\Delta_{\alpha,q}(p)})}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 4(1-p)q^{\alpha-1}\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q\right)\frac{p/\underline{r}_{\alpha}(1,q)-1}{2q^{\alpha-1}((2q^{\alpha-1}+\alpha\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q\right))(1-p)+\sqrt{\Delta_{\alpha,q}(p)})}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle 0,

where the last inequality follows since
p\geq\underline{r}_{\alpha}(1,q)
.

Now, we also have

\displaystyle r_{2}-\underline{r}_{\alpha}(1,q)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 1+\frac{\alpha\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q\right)(1-p)-\sqrt{\Delta_{\alpha,q}(p)}}{2q^{\alpha-1}}-\frac{1}{1+\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q\right)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\alpha\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q\right)(1-p)-\sqrt{\Delta_{\alpha,q}(p)}}{2q^{\alpha-1}}+\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q\right)\underline{r}_{\alpha}(1,q)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\alpha\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q\right)(1-p)+2q^{\alpha-1}\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q\right)\underline{r}_{\alpha}(1,q)-\sqrt{\Delta_{\alpha,q}(p)}}{2q^{\alpha-1}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{(\alpha\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q\right))^{2}(1-p)^{2}+4q^{2(\alpha-1)}(\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q\right))^{2}(\underline{r}_{\alpha}(1,q))^{2}+4q^{\alpha-1}(\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q\right))^{2}\alpha\underline{r}_{\alpha}(1,q)(1-p)}{2q^{\alpha-1}(\alpha\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q\right)(1-p)+2q^{\alpha-1}\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q\right)\underline{r}_{\alpha}(1,q)+\sqrt{\Delta_{\alpha,q}(p)})}

\displaystyle-\frac{\left(\alpha\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q\right)(1-p)\right)^{2}+4q^{\alpha-1}\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q\right)(1-p)}{2q^{\alpha-1}(\alpha\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q\right)(1-p)+2q^{\alpha-1}\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q\right)\underline{r}_{\alpha}(1,q)+\sqrt{\Delta_{\alpha,q}(p)})}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 4\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q\right)q^{\alpha-1}\frac{q^{\alpha-1}\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q\right)(\underline{r}_{\alpha}(1,q))^{2}+\alpha\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q\right)\underline{r}_{\alpha}(1,q)(1-p)-(1-p)}{2q^{\alpha-1}(\alpha\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q\right)(1-p)+2q^{\alpha-1}\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q\right)\underline{r}_{\alpha}(1,q)+\sqrt{\Delta_{\alpha,q}(p)})}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 4\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q\right)q^{\alpha-1}\frac{q^{\alpha-1}\underline{r}_{\alpha}(1,q)(p-\underline{r}_{\alpha}(1,q))}{2q^{\alpha-1}(\alpha\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q\right)(1-p)+2q^{\alpha-1}\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q\right)\underline{r}_{\alpha}(1,q)+\sqrt{\Delta_{\alpha,q}(p)})}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle 0,

where the last inequality follows since
p\geq\underline{r}_{\alpha}(1,q)
. Hence we have established that
r_{2}
belongs to
[\underline{r}_{\alpha}(1,q),p)
, and
r_{1}\geq 1\geq p
.

Now, note that the sign of
\varphi
is non-negative on
[r_{2},r_{1}]
and non-positive on
[0,r_{2}]
. We deduce that the function
\Phi
is non increasing on
[\underline{r}_{\alpha}(1,q),r_{2}]
and non decreasing on
[r_{2},p)
, thus, on
[\underline{r}_{\alpha}(1,q),p)
,
\Phi
achieves its minimum at
r_{2}=\mu_{\alpha,q}\left(p\right)
. In other words we have established that for any
p
in
[0,1],

\displaystyle\inf_{r\in[\underline{r}_{\alpha}(1,q),p)}\frac{p\overline{F}_{\alpha}\left(p|r,(1,q)\right)}{r}=\frac{p\overline{F}_{\alpha}\left(p|\mu_{\alpha,q}\left(p\right),(1,q)\right)}{\mu_{\alpha,q}\left(p\right)}=\frac{p\overline{F}_{\alpha}\left(p|\mu_{\alpha,q}\left(p\right),(1,q)\right)}{\mbox{opt}(F_{\alpha}\left(\cdot|\mu_{\alpha,q}\left(p\right),(1,q)\right))}.

(D-5)

Combining equations (
D-3
), (
D-4
) and (
D-5
) yields the result.
∎

Proof of
Lemma

D-1
.

We first start by studying the function
p\to\mu_{\alpha,q}\left(p\right)
. We have

\displaystyle\mu_{\alpha,q}\left(p\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 1-\frac{\sqrt{\left(\alpha\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q\right)\left(1-p\right)\right)^{2}+4\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q\right)\left(1-p\right)q^{\alpha-1}}-\alpha\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q\right)\left(1-p\right)}{2q^{\alpha-1}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 1+\alpha\frac{\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q\right)\left(1-p\right)}{2q^{\alpha-1}}-\sqrt{\left(\alpha\frac{\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q\right)\left(1-p\right)}{2q^{\alpha-1}}\right)^{2}+2\frac{\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q\right)\left(1-p\right)}{2q^{\alpha-1}}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1-2\left(1-\alpha\right)\frac{\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q\right)\left(1-p\right)}{2q^{\alpha-1}}}{1+\alpha\frac{\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q\right)\left(1-p\right)}{2q^{\alpha-1}}+\sqrt{\left(\alpha\frac{\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q\right)\left(1-p\right)}{2q^{\alpha-1}}\right)^{2}+2\frac{\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q\right)\left(1-p\right)}{2q^{\alpha-1}}}}.

The numerator of the above ratio
p\to 1-2\left(1-\alpha\right)\frac{\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q\right)\left(1-p\right)}{2q^{\alpha-1}}
is clearly non-decreasing, and the denominator
p\to 1+\alpha\frac{\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q\right)\left(1-p\right)}{2q^{\alpha-1}}+\sqrt{\left(\alpha\frac{\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q\right)\left(1-p\right)}{2q^{\alpha-1}}\right)^{2}+2\frac{\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q\right)\left(1-p\right)}{2q^{\alpha-1}}}
is clearly non-increasing. Therefore, by composition,
p\to\mu_{\alpha,q}\left(p\right)
is non-decreasing and
\mu_{\alpha,q}\left(p\right)\mbox{ in }\left[\mu_{\alpha,q}\left(\frac{1}{1+\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q\right)}\right),\mu_{\alpha,q}\left(1\right)\right]
with:

\displaystyle\mu_{\alpha,q}\left(\frac{1}{1+\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q\right)}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 1+\alpha\frac{\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q\right)^{2}}{2q^{\alpha-1}\left(1+\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q\right)\right)}-\sqrt{\left(\alpha\frac{\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q\right)^{2}}{2q^{\alpha-1}\left(1+\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q\right)\right)}\right)^{2}+2\frac{\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q\right)^{2}}{2q^{\alpha-1}\left(1+\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q\right)\right)}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 1+\alpha\frac{\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q\right)^{2}}{2q^{\alpha-1}\left(1+\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q\right)\right)}-\frac{\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q\right)}{2q^{\alpha-1}\left(1+\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q\right)\right)}\sqrt{\alpha^{2}\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q\right)^{2}+4q^{\alpha-1}\left(1+\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q\right)\right)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 1+\frac{\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q\right)}{2q^{\alpha-1}\left(1+\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q\right)\right)}\left(\alpha\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q\right)-\sqrt{\alpha^{2}\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q\right)^{2}+4\left(1+\left(1-\alpha\right)\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q\right)\right)\left(1+\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q\right)\right)}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 1+\frac{\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q\right)}{2q^{\alpha-1}\left(1+\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q\right)\right)}\left(\alpha\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q\right)-\sqrt{\left(2+\left(2-\alpha\right)\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q\right)\right)^{2}}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 1-\frac{\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q\right)}{2q^{\alpha-1}\left(1+\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q\right)\right)}2\left(1+\left(1-\alpha\right)\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q\right)\right)=\frac{1}{1+\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q\right)}.

\displaystyle\mu_{\alpha,q}\left(1\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 1+\alpha\frac{\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q\right)\left(1-1\right)}{2q^{\alpha-1}}-\sqrt{\left(\alpha\frac{\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q\right)\left(1-1\right)}{2q^{\alpha-1}}\right)^{2}+2\frac{\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q\right)\left(1-1\right)}{2q^{\alpha-1}}}=1.

Therefore
\mu_{\alpha,q}\left(p\right)\mbox{ in }\left[\frac{1}{1+\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q\right)},1\right]
and
p\to\mu_{\alpha,q}(p)
is an increasing function in
\left[\frac{1}{1+\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q\right)},1\right]
and, for any
p\mbox{ in }\left[\frac{1}{1+\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q\right)},1\right]
, its inverse is given by

\displaystyle\mu_{\alpha,q}^{-1}\left(p\right):=1-\frac{q^{\alpha-1}\left(1-p\right)^{2}}{\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q\right)\left(1-\alpha\left(1-p\right)\right)}.

Next, we will show each point separately.

First point

If
q\geq\underline{q}_{\alpha}
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{R_{3,\alpha}\left(p,q\right)}{R_{1,\alpha}\left(p,q\right)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q\right)}{\tilde{v}_{\alpha}}\mu_{\alpha,q}\left(p\right)\frac{\Gamma_{\alpha}\left(\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q\right)p\right)}{\Gamma_{\alpha}\left(\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q\right)\frac{p-\mu_{\alpha,q}\left(p\right)}{1-\mu_{\alpha,q}\left(p\right)}\right)}

\displaystyle\overset{\left(a\right)}{=}

\displaystyle\frac{\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q\right)}{\tilde{v}_{\alpha}}\mu_{\alpha,q}\left(p\right)\Gamma_{\alpha}\left(\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q\right)\frac{p-\frac{p-\mu_{\alpha,q}\left(p\right)}{1-\mu_{\alpha,q}\left(p\right)}}{1+\left(1-\alpha\right)\frac{p-\mu_{\alpha,q}\left(p\right)}{1-\mu_{\alpha,q}\left(p\right)}\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q\right)}\right),

where in (a), we used the identity
\frac{\Gamma_{\alpha}\left(x\right)}{\Gamma_{\alpha}\left(y\right)}=\Gamma_{\alpha}\left(\frac{x-y}{1+\left(1-\alpha\right)y}\right)
. Let us now focus on simplifying the term (A) inside
\Gamma_{\alpha}\left(\cdot\right)
. We have

\displaystyle(A)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q\right)\mu_{\alpha,q}\left(p\right)\left(1-p\right)}{1-\mu_{\alpha,q}\left(p\right)+\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q\right)\left(1-\alpha\right)\left(p-\mu_{\alpha,q}\left(p\right)\right)}

\displaystyle\overset{\left(b\right)}{=}

\displaystyle\frac{\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q\right)\mu_{\alpha,q}\left(p\right)\frac{q^{\alpha-1}\left(1-\mu_{\alpha,q}\left(p\right)\right)^{2}}{\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q\right)\left(1-\alpha\left(1-\mu_{\alpha,q}\left(p\right)\right)\right)}}{1-\mu_{\alpha,q}\left(p\right)+\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q\right)\left(1-\alpha\right)\left(1-\mu_{\alpha,q}\left(p\right)-\frac{q^{\alpha-1}\left(1-\mu_{\alpha,q}\left(p\right)\right)^{2}}{\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q\right)\left(1-\alpha\left(1-\mu_{\alpha,q}\left(p\right)\right)\right)}\right)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{q^{\alpha-1}\mu_{\alpha,q}\left(p\right)\left(1-\mu_{\alpha,q}\left(p\right)\right)}{1-\alpha\left(1-\mu_{\alpha,q}\left(p\right)\right)+\left(1-\alpha\right)\left(\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q\right)-\alpha\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q\right)\left(1-\mu_{\alpha,q}\left(p\right)\right)-q^{\alpha-1}\left(1-\mu_{\alpha,q}\left(p\right)\right)\right)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{q^{\alpha-1}\mu_{\alpha,q}\left(p\right)\left(1-\mu_{\alpha,q}\left(p\right)\right)}{1+\left(1-\alpha\right)\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q\right)-\left(1-\mu_{\alpha,q}\left(p\right)\right)\left(\alpha+\alpha\left(1-\alpha\right)\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q\right)-\left(1-\alpha\right)q^{\alpha-1}\right)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{q^{\alpha-1}\mu_{\alpha,q}\left(p\right)\left(1-\mu_{\alpha,q}\left(p\right)\right)}{q^{\alpha-1}-\left(1-\mu_{\alpha,q}\left(p\right)\right)q^{\alpha-1}}=1-\mu_{\alpha,q}\left(p\right),

where in (b), we used that

\displaystyle p=\mu_{\alpha,q}^{-1}\left(\mu_{\alpha,q}\left(p\right)\right)=1-\frac{q^{\alpha-1}\left(1-\mu_{\alpha,q}\left(p\right)\right)^{2}}{\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q\right)\left(1-\alpha\left(1-\mu_{\alpha,q}\left(p\right)\right)\right)}.

Therefore we obtain that:

\displaystyle\frac{R_{3,\alpha}\left(p,q\right)}{R_{1,\alpha}\left(p,q\right)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q\right)}{\tilde{v}_{\alpha}}\mu_{\alpha,q}\left(p\right)\Gamma_{\alpha}\left(1-\mu_{\alpha,q}\left(p\right)\right).

Since
p\to\mu_{\alpha,q}\left(p\right)
is non-decreasing and
\Gamma_{\alpha}\left(\cdot\right)
is non-increasing, then by composition, we have
p\mapsto R_{13,\alpha,q}\left(p\right)=\frac{R_{3,\alpha}\left(p,q\right)}{R_{1,\alpha}\left(p,q\right)}
is non-decreasing in
\left[\underline{r}_{\alpha}(1,q),1\right]
and
\frac{R_{3,\alpha}\left(p,q\right)}{R_{1,\alpha}\left(p,q\right)}\mbox{ in }\left[R_{13,\alpha,q}\left(\underline{r}_{\alpha}(1,q)\right),R_{13,\alpha,q}\left(1\right)\right]
, we have

\displaystyle R_{13,\alpha,q}\left(\underline{r}_{\alpha}(1,q)\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{R_{3,\alpha}\left(\frac{1}{1+\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q\right)},q\right)}{R_{1,\alpha}\left(\frac{1}{1+\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q\right)},q\right)}=\frac{\frac{\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q\right)}{1+\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q\right)}\Gamma_{\alpha}\left(\frac{\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q\right)}{1+\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q\right)}\right)}{\tilde{v}_{\alpha}}\overset{\left(a\right)}{\leq}1,

where in (a), we used the fact that the revenue function
x\to x\Gamma_{\alpha}\left(x\right)
is maximized at
x=\frac{1}{\alpha}
(with the convention that for
\alpha=0
,
1/\alpha=\infty
) and the maximum value achieved is
\tilde{v}_{\alpha}
. Furthermore, we have

\displaystyle R_{13,\alpha,q}\left(1\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{R_{3,\alpha}\left(1,q\right)}{R_{1,\alpha}\left(1,q\right)}=\frac{\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q\right)}{\tilde{v}_{\alpha}}.

Note that
R_{13,\alpha,q}\left(1\right)\geq 1
iff
q\leq\Gamma_{\alpha}\left(\tilde{v}_{\alpha}\right)
. For
q\mbox{ in }(\underline{r}_{\alpha}(1,q),\Gamma_{\alpha}\left(\tilde{v}_{\alpha}\right))
, we define
p_{13,\alpha,q}=R_{13,\alpha,q}^{-1}\left(1\right)
.

Therefore, we have that when
q\mbox{ in }\left[\underline{q}_{\alpha},\Gamma_{\alpha}\left(\tilde{v}_{\alpha}\right)\right)
, then
R_{3,\alpha}\left(p,q\right)\leq R_{1,\alpha}\left(p,q\right)
if
p\leq p_{13,\alpha,q}
and
R_{3,\alpha}\left(p,q\right)\geq R_{1,\alpha}\left(p,q\right)
if
p\geq p_{13,\alpha,q}
. And if
q\mbox{ in }\left[\Gamma_{\alpha}\left(\tilde{v}_{\alpha}\right),1\right]
then
R_{1,\alpha}\left(p,q\right)\geq R_{3,\alpha}\left(p,q\right)
for all
p\mbox{ in }\left[\underline{r}_{\alpha}(1,q),1\right]
.

Second point

We have

\displaystyle\frac{R_{1,\alpha}\left(p,q\right)}{R_{2,\alpha}\left(p,q\right)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\mu_{\alpha,q}\left(p\right)}\Gamma_{\alpha}\left(\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q\right)\frac{p-\mu_{\alpha,q}\left(p\right)}{1-\mu_{\alpha,q}\left(p\right)}\right)=\frac{1}{\mu_{\alpha,q}\left(p\right)}\Gamma_{\alpha}\left(\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q\right)\left(1-\frac{1-p}{1-\mu_{\alpha,q}\left(p\right)}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\overset{\left(a\right)}{=}

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\mu_{\alpha,q}\left(p\right)}\Gamma_{\alpha}\left(\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q\right)\left(1-\frac{q^{\alpha-1}\left(1-\mu_{\alpha,q}\left(p\right)\right)}{\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q\right)\left(1-\alpha\left(1-\mu_{\alpha,q}\left(p\right)\right)\right)}\right)\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\mu_{\alpha,q}\left(p\right)}\Gamma_{\alpha}\left(\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q\right)-\frac{q^{\alpha-1}}{\left(\frac{1}{1-\mu_{\alpha,q}\left(p\right)}-\alpha\right)}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\mu_{\alpha,q}\left(p\right)}\Gamma_{\alpha}\left(\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q\right)-\frac{q^{\alpha-1}}{\left(\frac{1}{1-\mu_{\alpha,q}\left(p\right)}-\alpha\right)}\right),

where in (a), we used:

\displaystyle p=\mu_{\alpha,q}^{-1}\left(\mu_{\alpha,q}\left(p\right)\right)=1-\frac{q^{\alpha-1}\left(1-\mu_{\alpha,q}\left(p\right)\right)^{2}}{\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q\right)\left(1-\alpha\left(1-\mu_{\alpha,q}\left(p\right)\right)\right)}.

Therefore, by composition, we have
p\to R_{12,\alpha,q}\left(p\right)=\frac{R_{1,\alpha}\left(p,q\right)}{R_{2,\alpha}\left(p,q\right)}
is non-increasing in
\left[\underline{r}_{\alpha}(1,q),1\right]
and
\frac{R_{1,\alpha}\left(p,q\right)}{R_{2,\alpha}\left(p,q\right)}\mbox{ in }\left[R_{12,\alpha,q}\left(1\right),R_{12,\alpha,q}\left(\underline{r}_{\alpha}(1,q)\right)\right]
, we have

\displaystyle R_{12,\alpha,q}\left(\underline{r}_{\alpha}(1,q)\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\underline{r}_{\alpha}(1,q)}\Gamma_{\alpha}\left(\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q\right)-\frac{q^{\alpha-1}}{\left(\frac{1}{1-\underline{r}_{\alpha}(1,q)}-\alpha\right)}\right)
