\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(1+\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q\right)\right)\Gamma_{\alpha}\left(\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q\right)-\frac{q^{\alpha-1}}{\frac{1+\left(1-\alpha\right)\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q\right)}{\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q\right)}}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(1+\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q\right)\right)\geq 1,

\displaystyle\lim_{p\to 1}R_{12,\alpha,q}\left(p\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\lim_{\mu\to 1}\frac{1}{\mu}\Gamma_{\alpha}\left(\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q\right)-\frac{q^{\alpha-1}}{\left(\frac{1}{1-\mu}-\alpha\right)}\right)=0.

We define
p_{12,\alpha,q}=R_{12,\alpha,q}^{-1}\left(1\right)
. Therefore,
R_{1,\alpha}\left(p,q\right)\leq R_{2,\alpha}\left(p,q\right)
if
p\leq p_{12,\alpha,q}
and
R_{1,\alpha}\left(p,q\right)\geq R_{2,\alpha}\left(p,q\right)
if
p\geq p_{12,\alpha,q}
.

Third point

If
q
belongs to
[\underline{q}_{\alpha},1]
, we have

\displaystyle R_{3,\alpha}\left(p,q\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q\right)}{\tilde{v}_{\alpha}}\Gamma_{\alpha}\left(\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q\right)p\right)

\displaystyle\frac{\partial R_{3,\alpha}\left(p,q\right)}{\partial p}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q\right)}{\tilde{v}_{\alpha}}\left(\Gamma_{\alpha}\left(\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q\right)p\right)-\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q\right)p\Gamma_{\alpha}\left(\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q\right)p\right)^{2-\alpha}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q\right)}{\tilde{v}_{\alpha}}\Gamma_{\alpha}\left(\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q\right)p\right)\left(1-\frac{\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q\right)p}{1+\left(1-\alpha\right)\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q\right)p}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\begin{cases}\frac{\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q\right)^{2}}{\tilde{v}_{\alpha}}\Gamma_{\alpha}\left(\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q\right)p\right)\left(\frac{\frac{1}{\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q\right)}}{1+\left(1-\alpha\right)\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q\right)p}\right)&\quad\mbox{if }\alpha=0,\\
\alpha\frac{\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q\right)^{2}}{\tilde{v}_{\alpha}}\Gamma_{\alpha}\left(\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q\right)p\right)\left(\frac{\overline{r}_{\alpha}(1,q)-p}{1+\left(1-\alpha\right)\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q\right)p}\right)&\quad\mbox{if }\alpha\in(0,1].\\
\end{cases}

Therefore, if
q\mbox{ in }[\underline{q}_{\alpha},1]
, then
\overline{r}_{\alpha}(1,q)\geq 1
and therefore
R_{3,\alpha}\left(\cdot,q\right)
is non-decreasing in
\left[\underline{r}_{\alpha}(1,q),1\right]
.

Fourth point

Case 1: Regular
\alpha=0

In this case, the first function is expressed as follows:

\displaystyle R_{1,0}\left(p,q\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{p}{\mu_{0,q}\left(p\right)\left(1+\left(\frac{1}{q}-1\right)\frac{p-\mu_{0,q}\left(p\right)}{1-\mu_{0,q}\left(p\right)}\right)}=\frac{pq\left(\mu_{0,q}\left(p\right)-1\right)}{\mu_{0,q}\left(p\right)\left(p\left(q-1\right)-q+\mu_{0,q}\left(p\right)\right)}

\displaystyle\mbox{with }\mu_{0,q}\left(p\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 1-\frac{\sqrt{4\left(q^{-1}-1\right)\left(1-p\right)q^{-1}}}{2q^{-1}}=1-\sqrt{\left(1-p\right)\left(1-q\right)}.

Therefore

\displaystyle R_{1,0}\left(p,q\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{p}{\left(1-\sqrt{\left(1-p\right)\left(1-q\right)}\right)\left(1+\left(\frac{1}{q}-1\right)\frac{p-1+\sqrt{\left(1-p\right)\left(1-q\right)}}{1-1+\sqrt{\left(1-p\right)\left(1-q\right)}}\right)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{p}{\left(1-\sqrt{\left(1-p\right)\left(1-q\right)}\right)\left(1+\frac{1-q}{q}\frac{p-1+\sqrt{\left(1-p\right)\left(1-q\right)}}{\sqrt{\left(1-p\right)\left(1-q\right)}}\right)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{p}{\left(1-\sqrt{\left(1-p\right)\left(1-q\right)}\right)\left(1+\frac{1-q}{q}\left(1-\frac{\sqrt{1-p}}{\sqrt{1-q}}\right)\right)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{pq}{\left(1-\sqrt{\left(1-p\right)\left(1-q\right)}\right)\left(q+1-q-\sqrt{\left(1-q\right)\left(1-p\right)}\right)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{pq}{\left(1-\sqrt{\left(1-p\right)\left(1-q\right)}\right)^{2}}.

We have, for all
p\mbox{ in }\left[q,1\right]
:

\displaystyle\frac{\partial R_{1,0}}{\partial p}\left(p,q\right)=-\frac{q\sqrt{1-p}\left(\sqrt{1-q}-\sqrt{1-p}\right)}{\left(1-p\right)\left(1-\sqrt{\left(1-p\right)\left(1-q\right)}\right)^{3}}\leq 0\quad\forall p\mbox{ in }\left[q,1\right],

and it is easy to see that function
p\to R_{2}\left(p,q\right)
is non-decreasing.

Case 2: mhr case
\alpha=1

For
\alpha=1
, the first function is expressed as follows:

\displaystyle R_{1,1}\left(p,q\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{p}{\mu_{1,q}\left(p\right)}e^{-\log\left(q^{-1}\right)\frac{p-\mu_{1,q}\left(p\right)}{1-\mu_{1,q}\left(p\right)}}

\displaystyle\mbox{with }\mu_{1,q}\left(p\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 1-\frac{\sqrt{\log\left(q^{-1}\right)\left(1-p\right))^{2}+4\log\left(q^{-1}\right)\left(1-p\right)}-\log\left(q^{-1}\right)\left(1-p\right)}{2}

\displaystyle\mbox{and }p

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mu_{1,q}^{-1}\left(\mu_{1,q}\left(p\right)\right)=1-\frac{\left(1-\mu_{1,q}\left(p\right)\right)^{2}}{\log\left(q^{-1}\right)\mu_{1,q}\left(p\right)}.

We therefore have

\displaystyle R_{1,1}\left(p,q\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1-\frac{\left(1-\mu_{1,q}\left(p\right)\right)^{2}}{\log\left(q^{-1}\right)\mu_{1,q}\left(p\right)}}{\mu_{1,q}\left(p\right)}e^{-\log\left(q^{-1}\right)\frac{1-\mu_{1,q}\left(p\right)-\frac{\left(1-\mu_{1,q}\left(p\right)\right)^{2}}{\log\left(q^{-1}\right)\mu_{1,q}\left(p\right)}}{1-\mu_{1,q}\left(p\right)}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(\frac{1}{\mu_{1,q}\left(p\right)}-\frac{1}{\log\left(q^{-1}\right)}\left(\frac{1}{\mu_{1,q}\left(p\right)}-1\right)^{2}\right)e^{-\log\left(q^{-1}\right)+\frac{1}{\mu_{1,q}\left(p\right)}-1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{q}{e\log\left(q^{-1}\right)}\left(\frac{\log\left(q^{-1}\right)}{\mu_{1,q}\left(p\right)}-\frac{1}{\mu_{1,q}\left(p\right)^{2}}+\frac{2}{\mu_{1,q}\left(p\right)}-1\right)e^{\frac{1}{\mu_{1,q}\left(p\right)}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{q}{e\log\left(q^{-1}\right)}\left(\frac{1}{\mu_{1,q}\left(p\right)^{2}}-\frac{\log\left(q^{-1}\right)+2}{\mu_{1,q}\left(p\right)}+1\right)e^{\frac{1}{\mu_{1,q}\left(p\right)}}=:\tilde{R_{1}}\left(\frac{1}{\mu_{1,q}\left(p\right)}\right),

with

\displaystyle\tilde{R_{1}}\left(x\right)=-\frac{q}{e\log\left(q^{-1}\right)}\left(x^{2}-\left(2+\log\left(q^{-1}\right)\right)x+1\right)e^{x}\quad\mbox{for }x\mbox{ in }\left[1,1+\log\left(q^{-1}\right)\right].

On another hand, we have

\displaystyle\frac{d\tilde{R_{1}}\left(x\right)}{dx}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{q}{e\log\left(q^{-1}\right)}\left(2x-2-\log\left(q^{-1}\right)+x^{2}-\left(2+\log\left(q^{-1}\right)\right)x+1\right)e^{x}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{q}{e\log\left(q^{-1}\right)}\left(x^{2}-\log\left(q^{-1}\right)x-\left(1+\log\left(q^{-1}\right)\right)\right)e^{x}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{q}{e\log\left(q^{-1}\right)}\left(x+1\right)\left(x-\left(1+\log\left(q^{-1}\right)\right)\right)e^{x}\geq 0\quad\forall x\mbox{ in }\left[1,1+\log\left(q^{-1}\right)\right].

For all
p
in
[\frac{1}{1+\log\left(q^{-1}\right)},1]
, we have
\mu_{1,q}\left(p\right)
in
[\frac{1}{1+\log\left(q^{-1}\right)},1]
, therefore
\frac{1}{\mu_{1,q}\left(p\right)}
in
[1,1+\log\left(q^{-1}\right)]
.

Therefore, by composition,
p\to R_{1,1}\left(p,q\right)
is non increasing. The function
p\to R_{2,1}\left(p,q\right)=p
is non-decreasing.
∎

Proof of
Lemma

D-2
.

Case 1: Regular case
\alpha=0
.

In this case, for
q\mbox{ in }(\underline{q}_{\alpha},\Gamma_{\alpha}\left(\tilde{v}_{\alpha}\right)]\overset{\alpha=0}{=}\left(0,\frac{1}{2}\right]
,
p_{13,0,q}
is a solution to the following equation

\displaystyle\frac{\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q\right)}{\tilde{v}_{\alpha}}\mu_{\alpha,q}\left(p\right)\Gamma_{\alpha}\left(1-\mu_{\alpha,q}\left(p\right)\right)=1

iff

\displaystyle\left(\frac{1}{q}-1\right)\frac{1-\sqrt{\left(1-p\right)\left(1-q\right)}}{1+\sqrt{\left(1-p\right)\left(1-q\right)}}=1

iff

\displaystyle\frac{1-2q}{1-q}=\frac{\sqrt{\left(1-p\right)\left(1-q\right)}}{1-q}

iff

\displaystyle p=p_{13,0,q}=1-\frac{\left(1-2q\right)^{2}}{1-q}.

and
p_{12,0,q}
is solution to the following equation

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\mu_{\alpha,q}\left(p\right)}\Gamma_{\alpha}\left(\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q\right)-\frac{q^{\alpha-1}}{\left(\frac{1}{1-\mu_{\alpha,q}\left(p\right)}-\alpha\right)}\right)=1

iff

\displaystyle\frac{q}{\left(1-\sqrt{\left(1-p\right)\left(1-q\right)}\right)^{2}}=1

iff

\displaystyle\sqrt{q}=1-\sqrt{\left(1-p\right)\left(1-q\right)}

iff

\displaystyle\sqrt{\left(1-p\right)}=\frac{1-\sqrt{q}}{\sqrt{1-q}}

iff

\displaystyle p=p_{12,0,q}=1-\frac{\left(1-\sqrt{q}\right)^{2}}{1-q}.

Therefore

\displaystyle p_{13,0,q}\leq p_{12,0,q}

iff

\displaystyle 1-\frac{\left(1-2q\right)^{2}}{1-q}\leq 1-\frac{\left(1-\sqrt{q}\right)^{2}}{1-q}

iff

\displaystyle\left(1-2q\right)^{2}\geq\left(1-\sqrt{q}\right)^{2}

iff

\displaystyle 2q\leq\sqrt{q}

iff

\displaystyle q\leq\frac{1}{4}:=\hat{q}_{0}.

Furthermore, note that
\hat{q}_{0}\in[\underline{q}_{\alpha},\Gamma_{\alpha}\left(\tilde{v}_{\alpha}\right)]
, since
\underline{q}_{\alpha}\overset{\alpha=0}{=}0
and
\Gamma_{\alpha}\left(\tilde{v}_{\alpha}\right)\overset{\alpha=0}{=}\frac{1}{2}
.

Case 2: mhr case
\alpha=1
.

In this case, for
q\mbox{ in }(\underline{q}_{\alpha},\Gamma_{\alpha}\left(\tilde{v}_{\alpha}\right)]\overset{\alpha=1}{=}\left(e^{-1},e^{-e^{-1}}\right]
,
p_{13,1,q}
is solution to the following equation

\displaystyle\frac{\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q\right)}{\tilde{v}_{\alpha}}\mu_{\alpha,q}\left(p\right)\Gamma_{\alpha}\left(1-\mu_{\alpha,q}\left(p\right)\right)\overset{\alpha=1}{=}\log\left(q^{-1}\right)e\mu_{1,q}\left(p\right)e^{\mu_{1,q}\left(p\right)-1}=1

iff

\displaystyle\mu_{1,q}\left(p\right)e^{\mu_{1,q}\left(p\right)-1}=\frac{1}{\log\left(q^{-1}\right)}

iff

\displaystyle p=p_{13,1,q}=\mu_{1,q}^{-1}\left(W\left(\frac{1}{\log\left(q^{-1}\right)}\right)\right).

And
p_{12,1,q}
is solution to the following equation

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\mu_{\alpha,q}\left(p\right)}\Gamma_{\alpha}\left(\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q\right)-\frac{q^{\alpha-1}}{\left(\frac{1}{1-\mu_{\alpha,q}\left(p\right)}-\alpha\right)}\right)\overset{\alpha=1}{=}\frac{1}{\mu_{1,q}\left(p\right)}e^{\log\left(q\right)+\frac{1}{\frac{1}{1-\mu_{1,q}\left(p\right)}-1}}=1

iff

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\mu_{1,q}\left(p\right)}e^{\frac{1}{\mu_{1,q}\left(p\right)}}\frac{q}{e}=1

iff

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\mu_{1,q}\left(p\right)}e^{\frac{1}{\mu_{1,q}\left(p\right)}}=\frac{e}{q}

iff

\displaystyle p=p_{12,1,q}=\mu_{1,q}^{-1}\left(\frac{1}{W\left(\frac{e}{q}\right)}\right).

Therefore

\displaystyle p_{13,1,q}\leq p_{12,1,q}

iff

\displaystyle\mu_{1,q}^{-1}\left(W\left(\frac{1}{\log\left(q^{-1}\right)}\right)\right)\leq\mu_{1,q}^{-1}\left(\frac{1}{W\left(\frac{e}{q}\right)}\right)

iff

\displaystyle W\left(\frac{1}{\log\left(q^{-1}\right)}\right)\leq\frac{1}{W\left(\frac{e}{q}\right)}\quad\mbox{as }\mu_{1,q}^{-1}\left(.\right)\mbox{ is increasing}

iff

\displaystyle W\left(\frac{1}{\log\left(q^{-1}\right)}\right)W\left(\frac{e}{q}\right)\leq 1.

We now study
q\mapsto g\left(q\right):=W\left(\frac{1}{\log\left(q^{-1}\right)}\right)W\left(\frac{e}{q}\right)
in
\left(e^{-1},e^{-e^{-1}}\right]
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{dg\left(q\right)}{dq}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{W\left(\frac{e}{q}\right)W\left(-\frac{1}{\log\left(q\right)}\right)\left(W\left(\frac{e}{q}\right)+\log\left(q\right)W\left(-\frac{1}{\log\left(q\right)}\right)+\log\left(q\right)+1\right)}{q\log\left(q\right)\left(W\left(\frac{e}{q}\right)+1\right)\left(W\left(-\frac{1}{\log\left(q\right)}\right)+1\right)}.

We next analyze the sign of the derivative.

\displaystyle sign\left(\frac{dg\left(q\right)}{dq}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle sign\left(W\left(\frac{e}{q}\right)+\log\left(q\right)W\left(-\frac{1}{\log\left(q\right)}\right)+\log\left(q\right)+1\right)=sign\left(h\left(q\right)\right)

\displaystyle\mbox{with }h\left(q\right)

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle W\left(\frac{e}{q}\right)+\log\left(q\right)W\left(-\frac{1}{\log\left(q\right)}\right)+\log\left(q\right)+1

\displaystyle\frac{dh\left(q\right)}{dq}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\left(W\left(\frac{e}{q}\right)+1\right)W\left(-\frac{1}{\log\left(q\right)}\right)^{2}+W\left(-\frac{1}{\log\left(q\right)}\right)+1}{q\left(W\left(\frac{e}{q}\right)+1\right)\left(W\left(-\frac{1}{\log\left(q\right)}\right)+1\right)}\geq 0\quad\forall q\mbox{ in }\left[e^{-1},e^{-e^{-1}}\right].

Therefore
q\mapsto h\left(q\right)
is non-decreasing and we have
h\left(e^{-1}\right)=W\left(e^{2}\right)-W\left(1\right)>0
. Therefore
g
is increasing in
\left(e^{-1},e^{-e^{-1}}\right]
. Furthermore, we have
g\left(e^{-1}\right)=W\left(1\right)W\left(e^{2}\right)<1
and
g\left(e^{-e^{-1}}\right)=W\left(e^{1+1/e}\right)>1
.
Therefore there exists a unique
\hat{q}_{1}
solution in
\left(e^{-1},e^{-e^{-1}}\right]
to the equation
W\left(\frac{1}{\log\left(q^{-1}\right)}\right)W\left(\frac{e}{q}\right)=1
.

Therefore, we have, for
q\mbox{ in }\left(e^{-1},e^{-e^{-1}}\right]

\displaystyle p_{13,1,q}\leq p_{12,1,q}

iff

\displaystyle g\left(q\right)\leq 1\quad\text{iff}\quad q\leq\hat{q}_{1}.

∎

Appendix E
Proofs and auxiliary results for
Section

5

E.1
Proofs and auxiliary results for
Section

5.1

Proof of
Proposition

2
.
.

Fix
\alpha\mbox{ in }[0,1]
,
\Psi\mbox{ in }\pazocal{P}
,
q\mbox{ in }(0,1)
,
N>1
, and a finite sequence of increasing reals
\mathbb{A}=\{a_{i}\}_{i=1}^{N}
such that
0<a_{1}\leq w\leq a_{N}
. The proof uses two building blocks associated with uniformly bounding the losses stemming from truncating a mechanism and the losses stemming from local transfers of mass in a mechanism.

Define the two mechanisms
\Psi_{a_{N}}
in
\pazocal{P}
and
\Psi_{\mathbb{A}}\mbox{ in }\pazocal{P}_{\mathbb{A}}
as follows

\displaystyle\Psi_{a_{N}}(v)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\begin{cases}\Psi(v)&\quad\mbox{if }v\in[0,a_{N}),\\
1&\quad\mbox{if }v\geq a_{N}.\end{cases}

\displaystyle\Psi_{\mathbb{A}}(x)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\begin{cases}0&\quad\mbox{if }x\in[0,a_{1}).\\
\Psi(a_{i+1})&\quad\mbox{if }x\in[a_{i},a_{i+1}),\quad\mbox{for }1\leq i\leq N-1,\\
1&\quad\mbox{if }x\in[a_{N},\infty).\end{cases}

\Psi_{a_{N}}
is a truncated version of
\Psi
at
a_{N}
and
\Psi_{\mathbb{A}}
is a discretized and truncated version of
\Psi
.

Let
F\mbox{ in }\pazocal{F}_{\alpha}(w,q)
. Next we analyze
R(\Psi,F)-R(\Psi_{\mathbb{A}},F)
by decomposing it as follows.

\displaystyle R(\Psi,F)-R(\Psi_{\mathbb{A}},F)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle R(\Psi,F)-R(\Psi_{a_{N}},F)+R(\Psi_{a_{N}},F)-R(\Psi_{\mathbb{A}},F).

(E-1)

To uniformly bound the maximal losses stemming from truncation
R(\Psi,F)-R(\Psi_{a_{N}},F)
, we establish the following result, whose proof is deferred to
Section

E.1.1
.

Lemma E-1

(Truncation)
.

Fix a mechanism
\Psi\mbox{ in }\pazocal{P}
,
b\geq w
,
q\mbox{ in }(0,1)
and let

\displaystyle\Psi_{b}(v)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\begin{cases}\Psi(v)&\quad\mbox{if }v\in[0,b),\\
1&\quad\mbox{if }v\geq b.\end{cases}

Then for any distribution
F\mbox{ in }\pazocal{F}_{\alpha}(w,q)
,

\displaystyle R(\Psi_{b},F)\geq R(\Psi,F)-\frac{1}{q\left(1+(q^{-1}-1)b/w\right)}\mathbf{1}\{b\leq\overline{r}_{\alpha}(w,q)\}.

In particular, the result upper bounds the maximal performance losses that can stem from truncating a pricing mechanism at
b
.

To uniformly bound the the impact of discretization
R(\Psi_{a_{N}},F)-R(\Psi_{\mathbb{A}},F)
, we first establish a result (whose proof is deferred to
Section

E.1.1
) that bounds the performance losses stemming from transferring mass locally in a mechanism.

Lemma E-2

(Local transfer of mass)
.

Fix a mechanism
\Psi\mbox{ in }\pazocal{P}
,
0<\epsilon<v
, and let

\displaystyle\Psi_{\epsilon,v}(x)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\begin{cases}\Psi(x)&\quad\mbox{if }x\in[0,v-\epsilon),\\
\Psi(v)&\quad\mbox{if }x\in[v-\epsilon,v),\\
\Psi(x)&\quad\mbox{if }x\geq v.\end{cases}

Then, for any distribution
F\mbox{ in }\pazocal{F}_{\alpha}(w,q)

\displaystyle R(\Psi_{\epsilon,v},F)\geq R(\Psi,F)-\frac{\epsilon}{v}\left(\Psi(v)-\Psi(v-\epsilon)\right).

Applying
lemma

E-2
on
(v,\epsilon)=(a_{i},a_{i}-a_{i-1})
,
N-1
times consecutively for
2\leq i\leq N
on the mechanism
\Psi_{a_{N}}
, we obtain

\displaystyle R(\Psi_{a_{N}},F)-R(\Psi_{\mathbb{A}},F)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{i=2}^{N}\frac{a_{i}-a_{i-1}}{a_{i}}(\Psi(a_{i})-\Psi(a_{i-1}))

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(a)}}{{\leq}}

\displaystyle\frac{\Delta(\mathbb{A})}{a_{1}}\sum_{i=2}^{N}(\Psi(a_{i})-\Psi(a_{i-1}))=\frac{\Delta(\mathbb{A})}{a_{1}}(\Psi(a_{N})-\Psi(a_{1}))\leq\frac{\Delta(\mathbb{A})}{a_{1}}.

where
(a)
follows from
a_{i}-a_{i-1}\leq\sup_{i}(a_{i}-a_{i-1})=\sigma(\mathbb{A})
and
a_{i}\geq a_{1}>0
.
Using
Lemma

E-1
, we have

\displaystyle R(\Psi_{b},F)\geq R(\Psi,F)-\frac{1}{q\left(1+(q^{-1}-1)a_{N}/w\right)}\mathbf{1}\{a_{N}\leq\overline{r}_{\alpha}(w,q)\}.

Returning to the decomposition in (
E-1
), we have established

\displaystyle R(\Psi,F)-R(\Psi_{\mathbb{A}},F)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{\Delta(\mathbb{A})}{a_{1}}+\frac{1}{q(1+\left(q^{-1}-1)a_{N}/w\right)}\mathbf{1}\{a_{N}\leq\overline{r}_{\alpha}(w,q)\}.

Noting that the inequality above applies for any
F
in
\pazocal{F}_{\alpha}(w,q)
and that the mechanism
\Psi_{\mathbb{A}}
does not depend on
F
, the result follows.
∎

Proof of
Theorem

3
.
This result is a special case of
Theorem

4
.

E.1.1
Proofs of auxiliary results

Proof of
Lemma

E-1
.
.

Let
Rev(q)=qF^{-1}(1-q)
denote the revenue curve of associated with
F
in the quantity space. Let
r_{F}
denote the optimal oracle price,
q_{F}
the corresponding quantity, and recall, from
Lemma

C-5
that
r_{F}\leq\overline{r}_{\alpha}(w,q)
. By definition, we have

\displaystyle R(\Psi_{b},F)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle R(\Psi,F)+\int_{b}^{+\infty}\frac{Rev(q_{b})-Rev(q_{x})}{\mbox{opt}(F)}d\Psi(x).

Case 1.
Suppose first
b>\overline{r}_{\alpha}(w,q)
. In this case, then
r_{F}\leq b
. Given that
F
is regular, the revenue curve is monotone for
q\leq q_{b}
, and we have
Rev(q_{b})-Rev(q_{x})
for
x\geq b
. We then have

\displaystyle R(\Psi_{b},F)\geq R(\Psi,F).

Case 2.
Suppose now that
b\leq\overline{r}_{\alpha}(w,q)
. In this case, we divide the analysis into two subcases.

Case a).
Suppose first that
r_{F}\leq b
.
We have for any
x\geq b\geq r
, by monotonicity of the revenue curve,
Rev(q_{b})-Rev(q_{x})\geq 0
, and therefore

\displaystyle R(\Psi_{b},F)\geq R(\Psi,F).

Case b).

r_{F}>b

We have:

\displaystyle R(\Psi_{b},F)

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle R(\Psi,F)+\int_{b}^{+\infty}\frac{Rev(q_{b})-Rev(q_{x})}{\mbox{opt}(F)}d\Psi(x)

(E-1)

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle R(\Psi,F)+\int_{b}^{+\infty}\left(\frac{Rev(q_{b})}{\mbox{opt}(F)}-1\right)d\Psi(x)

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle R(\Psi,F)+\left(\frac{Rev(q_{b})}{\mbox{opt}(F)}-1\right).

Recall that by assumption
b\geq w
and hence
q_{b}\leq q
. Using concavity of the revenue curve in the quantity space (which follows from regularity of
F
), we have

\displaystyle Rev(q_{b})

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle Rev(q_{F})+\frac{Rev(q)-Rev(q_{F})}{q-q_{F}}(q_{b}-q_{F}).

This implies that

\displaystyle\frac{Rev(q_{b})}{Rev(q_{F})}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle 1+\left(\frac{Rev(q)}{Rev(q_{F})}-1\right)\frac{q_{b}-q_{F}}{q-q_{F}}\>\geq\>\frac{q-q_{b}}{q-q_{F}}+\frac{Rev(q)}{Rev(q_{F})}\frac{q_{b}-q_{F}}{q-q_{F}}\>\geq\>\frac{q-q_{b}}{q-q_{F}}.

Noting that
F
is regular and using
Lemma

1
, we have

\displaystyle q_{b}\leq\Gamma_{0}\left(\Gamma_{0}^{-1}\left(q\right)\frac{b}{w}\right)=\frac{1}{1+(q^{-1}-1)b/w}.

Therefore

\displaystyle\frac{Rev(q_{b})}{\mbox{opt}(F)}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\frac{q}{q-q_{F}}\left(1-\frac{1}{q\left(1+\left(q^{-1}-1\right)b/w\right)}\right)\geq 1-\frac{1}{q\left(1+\left(q^{-1}-1\right)b/w\right)}.

Returning to (
E-1
), we deduce

\displaystyle R(\Psi_{b},F)\geq R(\Psi,F)-\frac{1}{q(1+(q^{-1}-1)b/w)}.

Combining both cases, the result follows.
∎

Proof of
Lemma

E-2
.
.

Let
r_{F}
denote the optimal oracle price,
q_{F}
the corresponding quantity. We have

\displaystyle R(\Psi_{\epsilon,v},F)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle R(\Psi,F)+\int_{v-\epsilon}^{v}\frac{Rev(q_{v-\epsilon})-Rev(q_{x})}{\mbox{opt}(F)}d\Psi(x).

Case 1.
Suppose
r_{F}\leq v-\epsilon
. In this case, using the regularity of
F
and the unimodality of the revenue curve, we have for any
x\geq v-\epsilon\geq r_{F}
,
Rev(q_{v-\epsilon})-Rev(q_{x})\geq 0
, and

\displaystyle R(\Psi_{\epsilon,v},F)\geq R(\Psi,F).

Case 2.
Suppose now
v-\epsilon<r_{F}\leq v
. In this case, we have

\displaystyle R(\Psi_{\epsilon,v},F)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle R(\Psi,F)+\int_{v-\epsilon}^{v}\frac{Rev(q_{v-\epsilon})-Rev(q_{x})}{opt(F)}d\Psi(x)

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle R(\Psi,F)+\int_{v-\epsilon}^{v}\left(\frac{Rev(q_{v-\epsilon})}{\mbox{opt}(F)}-1\right)d\Psi(x)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle R(\Psi,F)+\left(\frac{Rev(q_{v-\epsilon})}{\mbox{opt}(F)}-1\right)(\Psi(v)-\Psi(v-\epsilon)).

In this case, we have
\mbox{opt}(F)=r_{F}q_{F}\leq vq_{v-\epsilon}
. Therefore

\displaystyle R(\Psi_{\epsilon,v},F)

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle R(\Psi,F)+\left(\frac{v-\epsilon}{v}-1\right)(\Psi(v)-\Psi(v-\epsilon))

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle R(\Psi,F)-\frac{\epsilon}{v}(\Psi(v)-\Psi(v-\epsilon)).

Case 3.
Suppose now
v<r_{F}
. In this case,
for any
v-\epsilon\leq x\leq v<r_{F}
, by monotonicity of the revenue curve,
Rev(q_{v-\epsilon})-Rev(q_{x})\leq 0
, and furthermore,
Rev(q_{x})\leq Rev(q_{v})\leq\mbox{opt}(F)
. In turn, we have

\displaystyle R(\Psi_{\epsilon,v},F)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle R(\Psi,F)+\int_{v-\epsilon}^{v}\frac{Rev(q_{v-\epsilon})-Rev(q_{x})}{\mbox{opt}(F)}d\Psi(x)

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle R(\Psi,F)+\int_{v-\epsilon}^{v}\left(\frac{Rev(q_{v-\epsilon})}{Rev(q_{v})}-\frac{Rev(q_{x})}{Rev(q_{v})}\right)d\Psi(x)

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle R(\Psi,F)+\int_{v-\epsilon}^{v}\left(\frac{Rev(q_{v-\epsilon})}{Rev(q_{v})}-1\right)d\Psi(x)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle R(\Psi,F)+\left(\frac{Rev(q_{v-\epsilon})}{Rev(q_{v})}-1\right)(\Psi(v)-\Psi(v-\epsilon))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle R(\Psi,F)+\left((1-\frac{\epsilon}{v})\frac{q_{v-\epsilon}}{q_{v}}-1\right)(\Psi(v)-\Psi(v-\epsilon))

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle R(\Psi,F)+\left((1-\frac{\epsilon}{v})-1\right)(\Psi(v)-\Psi(v-\epsilon))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle R(\Psi,F)-\frac{\epsilon}{v}(\Psi(v)-\Psi(v-\epsilon)).

Combining the three cases yields the result.
∎

E.2
Proofs for
Section

5.2

Proof of
Proposition

3
.
.

The proof is divided into two steps. In the first step, we will show the lower bound by analyzing the performance of a specific mechanism. Then in a second step, we will derive the upper through the analysis of a family of hard cases when
q
is close to
0
.

Step 1: Lower bound

Let us define the following measure:

\displaystyle d\Psi(u)=\begin{cases}0&\quad\mbox{if }u<wq,\\
\frac{1}{u\log(\frac{1}{q})}&\quad\mbox{if }u\mbox{ in }[qw,w)\\
0&\quad\mbox{if }u\geq w.\end{cases}

We have that
\Psi(u)
is a distribution since

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}d\Psi(u)=\frac{1}{\log(\frac{1}{q})}\int_{wq}^{w}\frac{1}{u}du=1,

Using
Theorem

1
and the fact that
\pazocal{F}_{\alpha}(w,q)\subseteq\pazocal{F}_{0}(w,q)
for any
\alpha\in[0,1]
, we have

\displaystyle\inf_{F\in\pazocal{F}_{\alpha}(w,q)}R(\Psi,F)

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\inf_{F\in\pazocal{F}_{0}(w,q)}R(\Psi,F)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\min\Biggl\{\inf_{x\in[\underline{r}_{0}(w,q),w)}\frac{1}{\mbox{opt}(F_{0}(\cdot|x,(w,q)))}\int_{0}^{\infty}u\overline{F}_{0}(u|x,(w,q))d\Psi(u),

\displaystyle\inf_{x\in[w,\overline{r}_{0}(w,q)]}\frac{1}{\mbox{opt}(F_{0}(\cdot|x,(w,q)))}\int_{0}^{\infty}u\overline{F}_{0}(u|x,(w,q)))d\Psi(u)\Biggr\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\min\Biggl\{\inf_{x\in[wq,w)}\frac{1}{x}\Biggl[\int_{0}^{x}ud\Psi(u)+\int_{x}^{w}u\overline{G}_{0,w}(u|(x,1),(w,q))d\Psi(u)\Biggl],

\displaystyle\inf_{x\in[w,\infty)}\frac{1}{x\overline{G}_{0,x}(x|(0,1),(w,q))}\int_{0}^{x}u\overline{G}_{0,x}(u|(0,1),(w,q))d\Psi(u)\Biggr\}.

We will analyze each term separately depending whether
x\mbox{ in }[wq,w)
or
x\mbox{ in }[w,\infty).

Case 1:
x\mbox{ in }[wq,w)

We have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{x}\Biggl[\int_{0}^{x}ud\Psi(u)+\int_{x}^{w}u\overline{G}_{0,w}(u|(x,1),(w,q))d\Psi(u)\Biggl]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{x\log(\frac{1}{q})}\Biggl[\int_{wq}^{x}udu+\int_{x}^{w}\overline{G}_{0,w}(u|(x,1),(w,q))du\Biggr]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{x\log(\frac{1}{q})}\Biggl[x-wq+\int_{x}^{w}\frac{1}{1+(1/q-1)\frac{u-x}{w-x}}du\Biggr],

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{x\log(\frac{1}{q})}\left(x-wq+\Biggl[(w-x)\frac{\log(1+(1/q-1)\frac{u-x}{w-x})}{(1/q-1)}\Biggl]_{u=x}^{u=w}\right),

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{x\log(\frac{1}{q})}\left(x-wq+\log(\frac{1}{q})\frac{w-x}{(1/q-1)}\right),

Hence we get that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{x}\Biggl[\int_{0}^{x}ud\Psi(u)+\int_{x}^{w}u\overline{G}_{0,w}(u|(x,1),(w,q))d\Psi(u)\Biggl]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\log(\frac{1}{q})}\left(1+\frac{wq}{x}(\frac{\log(q)}{q-1}-1)-\frac{\log(\frac{1}{q})}{(1/q-1)}\right)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(a)}}{{\geq}}

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\log(\frac{1}{q})}\left(1+\frac{wq}{w}(\frac{\log(q)}{q-1}-1)-\frac{\log(\frac{1}{q})}{(1/q-1)}\right)=\frac{1-q}{\log(\frac{1}{q})},

where (a) is due to the fact that
\log(q)\leq q-1\leq 0
, and
x\leq w.

Hence we conclude that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{x}\Biggl[\int_{0}^{x}ud\Psi(u)+\int_{x}^{w}u\overline{G}_{0,w}(u|(x,1),(w,q))d\Psi(u)\Biggl]\geq\frac{1-q}{\log(\frac{1}{q})}.

(E-1)

Case 2:
x\mbox{ in }[w,\infty)

Let us now analyze the second term, we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{x\overline{G}_{0,x}(x|(0,1),(w,q))}\int_{0}^{x}u\overline{G}_{0,x}(u|(0,1),(w,q))d\Psi(u)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{x\overline{G}_{0,x}(x|(0,1),(w,q))}\Biggl[\int_{0}^{w}u\overline{G}_{0,x}(u|(0,1),(w,q))d\Psi(u)+\int_{w}^{x}u\overline{G}_{0,x}(u|(0,1),(w,q))d\Psi(u)\Biggl]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{x\overline{G}_{0,x}(x|(0,1),(w,q))\log(\frac{1}{q})}\int_{wq}^{w}\frac{1}{1+(\frac{1}{q}-1)\frac{u}{w}}du=\frac{\Biggl[w\log(1+(\frac{1}{q}-1)\frac{u}{w})\Biggl]_{u=wq}^{u=w}}{x\overline{G}_{0,x}(x|(0,1),(w,q))(\frac{1}{q}-1)\log(\frac{1}{q})}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{w}{(\frac{1}{q}-1)\log(\frac{1}{q})}(\log(\frac{1}{q})-\log(2-q))\frac{1}{x\overline{G}_{0,x}(x|(0,1),(w,q))}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{w}{(\frac{1}{q}-1)}(1-\frac{\log(2-q)}{\log(\frac{1}{q})})\frac{1}{x\overline{G}_{0,x}(x|(0,1),(w,q))}.

The revenue function
x\to x\overline{G}_{0,x}(x|(0,1),(w,q))
is non-decreasing in
[0,+\infty)
, therefore

\displaystyle x\overline{G}_{0,x}(x|(0,1),(w,q))\leq\lim_{x\to+\infty}x\overline{G}_{0,x}(x|(0,1),(w,q))=\frac{w}{(\frac{1}{q}-1)}.

Hence

\displaystyle\frac{1}{x\overline{G}_{0,x}(x|(0,1),(w,q))}\int_{0}^{x}u\overline{G}_{0,x}(u|(0,1),(w,q))d\Psi(u)

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle(1-\frac{\log(2-q)}{\log(\frac{1}{q})}),

(E-2)

By combining (
E-1
) and (
E-1
), we get that

\displaystyle\inf_{F\in\pazocal{F}_{\alpha}(w,q)}R(\Psi,F)\geq\min\left(\frac{1-q}{\log(\frac{1}{q})},1-\frac{\log(2-q)}{\log(\frac{1}{q})}\right).

For
q\mbox{ in }[0,1-\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}]
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{1-q}{\log(\frac{1}{q})}\geq\frac{\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}}{\log(\frac{1}{q})}\geq\frac{\log(2)}{\log(\frac{1}{q})}

and

\displaystyle 1-\frac{\log(2-q)}{\log(\frac{1}{q})}=\frac{\log(\frac{1}{q(2-q)})}{\log(\frac{1}{q})}=\frac{\log(\frac{1}{1-(1-q)^{2}})}{\log(\frac{1}{q})}\geq\frac{\log(\frac{1}{1-(\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}})^{2}})}{\log(\frac{1}{q})}=\frac{\log(2)}{\log(\frac{1}{q})}.

Hence we we get that

\displaystyle\inf_{F\in\pazocal{F}_{\alpha}(w,q)}R(\Psi,F)\geq\frac{\log(2)}{\log(\frac{1}{q})}.

This conclude the lower bound.

Step 2: Upper bound

Let
q\mbox{ in }(0,1)
and
K\mbox{ in }\mathbb{N}^{*}
. Define
\varepsilon=q^{\frac{1}{K}}\mbox{ in }(q,1)
and
a_{k}=w\varepsilon^{k}\mbox{ in }[qw,w)
for
k=1\cdots K
. Consider the family of distributions
F_{0}(\cdot|a_{k},(w,q))\mbox{ in }\pazocal{F}_{0}(w,q)
.

Using Yao’s principle
(
Yao 1977
)
, we have

\displaystyle\sup_{\Psi\in\pazocal{P}}\inf_{F\in\pazocal{F}_{0}(w,q)}R(\Psi,F)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sup_{p\geq 0}\frac{1}{K}\sum_{i=1}^{K}\frac{p\bar{F}_{0}(p|a_{i},(w,q))}{\mbox{opt}(F_{0}(\cdot|a_{i},(w,q)))}

(E-3)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{K}\max_{1\leq k\leq K}\sup_{p\in[a_{k+1},a_{k})}\sum_{i=1}^{K}\frac{p\bar{F}_{0}(p|a_{i},(w,q))}{\mbox{opt}(F_{0}(\cdot|a_{i},(w,q)))}.

Now let’s analyze the sup on each interval
[a_{k+1},a_{k}).
For all
1\leq k\leq K
, the revenue curve associated with
\bar{F}_{0}(\cdot|a_{i},(w,q))
is monotone non-increasing on
[a_{i},w)
as the optimal reserve price of
F_{0}(\cdot|a_{i},(w,q))
is
a_{i}
.

Furthermore, the revenue curve is convex in
[0,w)
as we have:

\displaystyle(p\bar{F}_{0}(p|a_{i},(w,q)))^{{}^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\begin{cases}1&\quad\text{if }p\leq a_{i}\\
\frac{w(1-\frac{a_{i}}{wq})}{(w-a_{i})(1+(\frac{1}{q}-1)\frac{p-a_{i}}{w-a_{i}})^{2}}&\quad\text{if }p<w\\
0&\quad\text{if }p\geq w.\end{cases}

Therefore the derivative of the revenue function
p\bar{F}_{0}(p|a_{i},(w,q))
is non-decreasing because
a_{i}\geq wq
and
p\mapsto\frac{1}{(w-a_{i})(1+(\frac{1}{q}-1)\frac{p-a_{i}}{w-a_{i}})^{2}}
is non-increasing. Hence the function

p\mapsto\sum_{i=1}^{K}\frac{p\bar{F}_{0}(p|a_{i},(w,q))}{\mbox{opt}(F_{0}(\cdot|a_{i},(w,q)))}

is convex on
[a_{k+1},a_{k}).
Thus, the sup on an interval must be attained at one of the extreme points of the interval.

Therefore by (
E-3
), we have that

\displaystyle\sup_{\Psi\in\pazocal{P}}\inf_{F\in\pazocal{F}_{0}(w,q)}R(\Psi,F)\leq\sup_{p\geq 0}G(p)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{K}\max_{1\leq k\leq K}\sum_{i=1}^{K}\frac{a_{k}\bar{F}_{0}(a_{k}|a_{i},(w,q))}{\mbox{opt}(F_{0}(\cdot|a_{i},(w,q)))}.

(E-4)

Now let us analyze the elementary term,
\frac{a_{k}\bar{F}_{0}(a_{k}|a_{i},(w,q))}{\mbox{opt}(F_{0}(\cdot|a_{i},(w,q)))}
for any
i,k
. Note that
\mbox{opt}(F_{0}(\cdot|a_{i},(w,q))=a_{i}
by
Lemma

C-4
.

There are two cases of interest either
i\leq k
or
i>k
, let us analyze each case separately.

Case 1,
i\leq k
:

We have
\bar{F}_{0}(a_{k}|a_{i},(w,q))=1,
thus

\displaystyle\frac{a_{k}\bar{F}_{0,a_{i},w,q}(a_{k})}{\mbox{opt}(F_{0,a_{i},w,q})}=\frac{a_{k}}{a_{i}}=\varepsilon^{k-i},

which implies that

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{k}\frac{a_{k}\bar{F}_{0}(a_{k}|a_{i},(w,q))}{\mbox{opt}(F_{0}(\cdot|a_{i},(w,q)))}=\sum_{i=1}^{k}\varepsilon^{k-i}=\sum_{i=0}^{k-1}\varepsilon^{i}=\frac{1-\varepsilon^{k}}{1-\varepsilon}\leq\frac{1}{1-\varepsilon}.

Hence we conclude that

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{k}\frac{a_{k}\bar{F}_{0}(a_{k}|a_{i},(w,q))}{\mbox{opt}(F_{0}(\cdot|a_{i},(w,q)))}\leq\frac{1}{1-\varepsilon}.

(E-5)

Case 2,
i\geq k
:

We have that

\displaystyle\frac{a_{k}\bar{F}_{0}(a_{k}|a_{i},(w,q))}{\mbox{opt}(F_{0}(\cdot|a_{i},(w,q)))}=\frac{a_{k}}{a_{i}}\frac{1}{1+(\frac{1}{q}-1)\frac{a_{k}-a_{i}}{w-a_{i}}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{k-i}\frac{1-\varepsilon^{i}}{1-\varepsilon^{i}+\frac{1}{q}(\varepsilon^{k}-\varepsilon^{i})-\varepsilon^{k}+\varepsilon^{i}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{k-i}\frac{1-\varepsilon^{i}}{1-\varepsilon^{k}+\frac{1}{q}(\varepsilon^{k}-\varepsilon^{i})}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\varepsilon^{-i}-1}{(\varepsilon^{-k}-1)+\frac{1}{q}(1-\varepsilon^{i-k})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{\varepsilon^{-i}-1}{(\varepsilon^{-k}-1)+\frac{1}{q}(1-\varepsilon)},

where in the last inequality we used the fact that
\varepsilon\leq 1.

Hence we conclude that

\displaystyle\sum_{i=k+1}^{K}\frac{a_{k}\bar{F}_{0}(a_{k}|a_{i},(w,q))}{\mbox{opt}(F_{0}(\cdot|a_{i},(w,q)))}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{i=k+1}^{K}\frac{\varepsilon^{-i}-1}{(\varepsilon^{-k}-1)+\frac{1}{q}(1-\varepsilon)}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(a)}}{{\leq}}

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\frac{1}{\varepsilon}-1}\frac{\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{K+1}}-\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{k+1}}}{(\varepsilon^{-k}-1)+\frac{1}{q}(1-\varepsilon)}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{1-\varepsilon}\frac{\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{K}}-\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{k}}}{(\varepsilon^{-k}-1)+\frac{1}{q}(1-\varepsilon)}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(b)}}{{\leq}}

\displaystyle\frac{1}{1-\varepsilon}\frac{\frac{1}{q}}{(\varepsilon^{-k}-1)+\frac{1}{q}(1-\varepsilon)},

where in (a) we used
\varepsilon^{-i}-1\leq\varepsilon^{-i}
and (b) we used the fact that
\varepsilon\geq 0
and that
\varepsilon^{K}=q.
From the last inequality we conclude that

\displaystyle\sum_{i=k+1}^{K}\frac{a_{k}\bar{F}_{0}(a_{k}|a_{i},(w,q))}{\mbox{opt}(F_{0}(\cdot|a_{i},(w,q)))}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{1-\varepsilon}\frac{1}{q(\varepsilon^{-k}-1)+(1-\varepsilon)}\leq\frac{1}{(1-\varepsilon)^{2}}.

(E-6)

By combining the last two cases, in particular (
E-4
), (
E-5
) and (
E-6
), we get that

\displaystyle\sup_{\Psi\in\pazocal{P}}\inf_{F\in\pazocal{F}_{0}(w,q)}R(\Psi,F)\leq\frac{1}{K}\left(\frac{1}{1-\varepsilon}+\frac{1}{(1-\varepsilon)^{2}}\right).

By choosing
K=\log(1/q)
, thus
\varepsilon=e^{-1}
, we get:

\displaystyle\sup_{\Psi\in\pazocal{P}}\inf_{F\in\pazocal{F}_{0}(w,q)}R(\Psi,F)\leq\frac{c_{2}}{\log(1/q)}\mbox{ with }c_{2}=\frac{1}{1-e^{-1}}\left(1+\frac{1}{1-e^{-1}}\right)

This concludes the proof.
∎

Proof of
Proposition

4
.
.

This proof is divided into two steps. In the first step, we will show the lower bound by analyzing the performance of a specific mechanism. Then in a second step, we will derive the upper through the analysis of a family of hard cases when
q
is close to
1
.

Throughout the proof we will assume that
q\geq 3/4
since we are interested in the limit when when
q
is close to
1
.

Step 1: Lower bound

Let us define the following measure parameterized by
a,b\geq 0
:

\displaystyle d\Psi(u)=\begin{cases}a&\quad\mbox{if }u=w\\
b\frac{(u\overline{G}_{0,u}(u|(0,1),(w,q)))^{\prime}}{u\overline{G}_{0,u}(u|(0,1),(w,q))}&\quad\mbox{if }u>w.\end{cases}

Note that
d\Psi(u)\geq 0
since the revenue function
u\to u\overline{G}_{0,u}(u|(0,1),(w,q))d\Psi(u)
is increasing in
[w,\infty).
Let us determine the condition on the parameters
a
and
b
so that
\Psi
is a distribution. For that we need the following

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}d\Psi(u)=1,

which implies that

\displaystyle a+b\log\left(\frac{\lim_{u\to\infty}u\overline{G}_{0,u}(u|(0,1),(w,q))}{w\overline{G}_{0,w}(w|(0,1),(w,q))}\right)=1.

Since
\overline{G}_{0,w}(w|(0,1),(w,q))d\Psi(u)=q
and
\lim_{u\to\infty}u\overline{G}_{0,u}(u|(0,1),(w,q))=\frac{w}{\frac{1}{q}-1}
, we get that

\displaystyle a+b\log\left(\frac{1}{1-q}\right)=1.

Hence the relation between
a
and
b
is as follows

\displaystyle b=\frac{1-a}{\log\left(\frac{1}{1-q}\right)}\quad\mbox{ and }a\mbox{ in }[0,1].

Using
Theorem

1
, we have

\displaystyle\inf_{F\in\pazocal{F}_{\alpha}(w,q)}R(\Psi,F)

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\inf_{F\in\pazocal{F}_{0}(w,q)}R(\Psi,F)

(E-7)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\min\Biggl\{\inf_{x\in[wq,w)}\frac{1}{x}\Biggl[\int_{0}^{x}ud\Psi(u)+\int_{x}^{w}u\overline{G}_{0,w}(u|(x,1),(w,q))d\Psi(u)\Biggl],

\displaystyle\inf_{x\in[w,\infty)}\frac{1}{x\overline{G}_{0,x}(x|(0,1),(w,q))}\int_{0}^{x}u\overline{G}_{0,x}(u|(0,1),(w,q))d\Psi(u)\Biggr\}.

We will analyze each term separately depending if
x\mbox{ in }[wq,w)
or
x\mbox{ in }[w,\infty).

Case 1:
x\mbox{ in }[wq,w)

We have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{x}\Biggl[\int_{0}^{x}ud\Psi(u)+\int_{x}^{w}u\overline{G}_{0,w}(u|(x,1),(w,q))d\Psi(u)\Biggl]\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(a)}}{{=}}\frac{aq}{x}\geq aq,

where the last inequality is due to the fact that
x\leq w
and
(a)
is due to
d\Psi(u)=0
for
u<w
,
d\Psi(w)=a
and
\overline{G}_{0,w}(w|(x,1),(w,q))=q
.

Hence we conclude that

\displaystyle\inf_{x\in[wq,w)}\frac{1}{x}\Biggl[\int_{0}^{x}ud\Psi(u)+\int_{x}^{w}u\overline{G}_{0,w}(u|(x,1),(w,q))d\Psi(u)\Biggl]\geq aq.

(E-8)

Case 2:
x\mbox{ in }[w,\infty)

Let us now analyze the second term, we have

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{x}u\overline{G}_{0,x}(u|(0,1),(w,q))d\Psi(u)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{w}u\overline{G}_{0,x}(u|(0,1),(w,q))d\Psi(u)+\int_{w}^{x}u\overline{G}_{0,x}(u|(0,1),(w,q))d\Psi(u)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(a)}}{{=}}

\displaystyle aq+b\int_{w}^{x}u\overline{G}_{0,x}(u|(0,1),(w,q))\frac{(u\overline{G}_{0,u}(u|(0,1),(w,q)))^{\prime}}{u\overline{G}_{0,u}(u|(0,1),(w,q))}du

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle awq+b\left[u\overline{G}_{0,u}(u|(0,1),(w,q))\right]_{w}^{x}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle awq+b\left(x\overline{G}_{0,x}(x|(0,1),(w,q))-wq\right),

(a)
is due to
d\Psi(u)=0
for
u<w
,
d\Psi(w)=a
and
w\overline{G}_{0,w}(w|(0,1),(w,q))=wq
. Hence we conclude that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{x\overline{G}_{0,x}(x|(0,1),(w,q))}\int_{0}^{x}u\overline{G}_{0,x}(u|(0,1),(w,q))d\Psi(u)

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle wq\frac{a-b}{x\overline{G}_{0,x}(x|(0,1),(w,q))}+b

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle(a-b)(1-q)+b,

where the last inequality we used the fact that
x\to x\overline{G}_{0,x}(x|(0,1),(w,q))
is non-decreasing in
[w,\infty)
and that
\lim_{x\to\infty}x\overline{G}_{0,x}(x|(0,1),(w,q))=\frac{wq}{1-q}.

Thus we conclude that

\displaystyle\inf_{x\mbox{ in }[w,+\infty)}\frac{1}{x\overline{G}_{0,x}(x|(0,1),(w,q))}\int_{0}^{x}u\overline{G}_{0,x}(u|(0,1),(w,q))d\Psi(u)

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle(a-b)(1-q)+b.

(E-9)

By combining (
E-7
), (
E-8
) and (
E-9
) we get that

\displaystyle\inf_{F\in\pazocal{F}_{\alpha}(w,q)}R(\Psi,F)\geq\min\{aq,(a-b)(1-q)+b\}.

Now let us set

\displaystyle a=\frac{q}{2(q-\frac{1}{2})\log\left(\frac{1}{1-q}\right)+q}.

Note that
a\mbox{ in }[0,1]
as
q\mbox{ in }[3/4,1],
this also leads to the fact that

\displaystyle(a-b)(1-q)+b=aq=\frac{q^{2}}{2(q-\frac{1}{2})\log\left(\frac{1}{1-q}\right)+q}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(a)}}{{\geq}}\frac{9}{16}\frac{1}{2(1-\frac{1}{2})\log\left(\frac{1}{1-q}\right)+1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{9}{16(\log\left(\frac{1}{1-q}\right)+1)}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(b)}}{{\geq}}

\displaystyle\frac{9}{32}\frac{1}{\log\left(\frac{1}{1-q}\right)},

where inequality (a) stems from the fact
q\mbox{ in }[3/4,1],
and in (b) we have used
\log\left(\frac{1}{1-q}\right)\geq 1.

Hence we we get that

\displaystyle\inf_{F\in\pazocal{F}_{\alpha}(w,q)}R(\Psi,F)\geq\frac{9}{32}\frac{1}{\log\left(\frac{1}{1-q}\right)}.

This conclude the lower bound.

Step 2: Upper bound

To show the upper bound we will introduce a family of “hard” cases. We consider the family of distributions
(F_{0}(\cdot|r,(w,q))\mbox{ in }\pazocal{F}_{0}(w,q))_{r\geq w}
and the following weight distribution:

\displaystyle d\lambda(r)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\begin{cases}0&\quad\text{if }r<w\\
\frac{1}{\log\left(\frac{1}{1-q}\right)}\frac{(r\overline{G}_{0,r}(r|(0,1),(w,q)))^{\prime}}{r\overline{G}_{0,r}(r|(0,1),(w,q))}&\quad\text{if }r\geq w\end{cases}

One can verify that
\int_{0}^{\infty}d\lambda(r)=1
and that
d\lambda(r)\geq 0.
Let us define

\displaystyle G(p)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{p\overline{F}_{0}(p|r,(w,q))}{\mbox{opt}(F_{0}(\cdot|r,(w,q)))}d\lambda(r).

Using Yao’s principle
(
Yao 1977
)
, we have

\displaystyle\sup_{\Psi\in\pazocal{P}}\inf_{F\in\pazocal{F}_{0}(w,q)}R(\Psi,F)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sup_{p\geq 0}G(p)=\sup_{p\geq 0}\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{p\overline{F}_{0}(p|r,(w,q))}{\mbox{opt}(F_{0}(\cdot|r,(w,q)))}d\lambda(r).

(E-10)

Note that

\displaystyle\sup_{p\geq 0}\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{p\overline{F}_{0}(p|r,(w,q))}{\mbox{opt}(F_{0}(\cdot|r,(w,q)))}d\lambda(r)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sup_{p\geq 0}\int_{w}^{\infty}\frac{p\overline{F}_{0}(p|r,(w,q))}{\mbox{opt}(F_{0}(\cdot|r,(w,q)))}d\lambda(r)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sup_{p\geq w}\int_{w}^{\infty}\frac{p\overline{F}_{0}(p|r,(w,q))}{\mbox{opt}(F_{0}(\cdot|r,(w,q)))}d\lambda(r),

where the last equality follows from the fact that
p\mapsto p\overline{F}_{0}(p|r,(w,q))
is increasing on
[0,w]
for any
r\geq w
.

Fix
p\geq w
and let us analyze the integral term. We have

\displaystyle\int_{w}^{\infty}\frac{p\overline{F}_{0}(p|r,(w,q))}{\mbox{opt}(F_{0}(\cdot|r,(w,q)))}d\lambda(r)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{p}^{\infty}\frac{p\overline{F}_{0}(p|r,(w,q))}{r\overline{G}_{0,r}(r|(0,1),(w,q))}\frac{1}{\log\left(\frac{1}{1-q}\right)}\frac{(r\overline{G}_{0,r}(r|(0,1),(w,q)))^{\prime}}{r\overline{G}_{0,r}(r|(0,1),(w,q))}dr

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\log\left(\frac{1}{1-q}\right)}p\overline{G}_{0,p}(p|(0,1),(w,q))\int_{p}^{\infty}\frac{(r\overline{G}_{0,r}(r|(0,1),(w,q)))^{\prime}}{(r\overline{G}_{0,r}(r|(0,1),(w,q)))^{2}}dr

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\log\left(\frac{1}{1-q}\right)}p\overline{G}_{0,p}(p|(0,1),(w,q))\left(\frac{1}{p\overline{G}_{0,p}(p|(0,1),(w,q))}-\lim_{r\to\infty}\frac{1}{r\overline{G}_{0,r}(r|(0,1),(w,q))}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\log\left(\frac{1}{1-q}\right)}\left(1-\left(\frac{1}{q}-1\right)\frac{p\overline{G}_{0,p}(p|(0,1),(w,q))}{w}\right)\leq\frac{1}{\log\left(\frac{1}{1-q}\right)}.

By using the last inequality, together with (
E-10
), we obtain the result.
∎

Appendix F
Proofs and auxiliary results for
Section

6

Proof of
Theorem

4
.

We aim to show that one can approximate the value of the maximin ratio via lower and upper bounds and we quantify the asymptotic error of this approximation as a function of the grid size
N>0
.

We will do that in different steps:

•

In a first step, we extend previous results to the interval uncertainty case:

–

We will first show in
Proposition

F-1
that in the interval uncertainty case, we reduce the family of worst case distributions by generalizing
Theorem

1
.

–

Under such a reduction, we then show in
Proposition

F-2
that we can still approximate the performance of any mechanism by its discrete version by generalizing
Proposition

2
.

•

In a second step, we derive lower bounds on the maximin ratio in the form of linear programs.

•

In a third step, we show that through an appropriate choice of the support of a discrete mechanism, one can approximate the maximin ratio arbitrarily closely through the lower bound.

Step 1.
We first reduce the possible set of worst-cases to consider by extending
Theorem

1
. For that, let us define the following subset of distributions

\displaystyle\pazocal{S}_{\alpha,w,q_{l},q_{h}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left\{F_{\alpha}(\cdot|r,(w,q_{l})):r\mbox{ in }\left[\underline{r}_{\alpha}(w,q_{l}),w\right)\right\}\cup\left\{F_{\alpha}(\cdot|r,(w,q_{h})):r\mbox{ in }\left[w,\overline{r}_{\alpha}(w,q_{h})\right]\right\}.

(F-1)

where we use the convention that whenever
\overline{r}_{\alpha}(w,q_{h})<w
,
[w,\overline{r}_{\alpha}(w,q_{h})]:=\emptyset
. We have the following result, whose proof is deferred to
Section

F.1
.

Proposition F-1
.

For any
q_{l},q_{h}\mbox{ in }(0,1)^{2}
such that
q_{l}\leq q_{h}
, and for any subset of mechanisms
\pazocal{P}^{\prime}\subseteq\pazocal{P}
,

\displaystyle\pazocal{R}(\pazocal{P}^{\prime},\pazocal{F}_{\alpha}(w,[q_{l},q_{h}]))=\pazocal{R}(\pazocal{P}^{\prime},\pazocal{S}_{\alpha,w,q_{l},q_{h}}).

In addition, the next proposition generalizes
Proposition

2
, and its proof is deferred to
Section

F.1
.

Proposition F-2
.

Let
q_{l},q_{h}\mbox{ in }(0,1)^{2}
such that
q_{l}\leq q_{h}
. Fix a mechanism
\Psi\mbox{ in }\pazocal{P}
,
N>1
, and any finite sequence of increasing reals
\mathbb{A}=\{a_{i}\}_{i=0}^{N}
such that
a_{0}=\underline{r}_{\alpha}(w,q_{l}),a_{N}\geq w
. Then there exists
\Psi_{\mathbb{A}}\mbox{ in }\pazocal{P}_{\mathbb{A}}
such that

\displaystyle\inf_{F\in\pazocal{F}_{\alpha}(w,[q_{l},q_{h}])}R(\Psi_{\mathbb{A}},F)\geq\inf_{F\in\pazocal{F}_{\alpha}(w,[q_{l},q_{h}])}R(\Psi,F)-\frac{\Delta(\mathbb{A})}{\underline{r}_{\alpha}(w,q_{l})}-\frac{\mathbf{1}\{a_{N}<\overline{r}_{\alpha}(w,q_{h})\}}{q_{h}(1+(q_{h}^{-1}-1)a_{N}/w)},

where
\Delta(\mathbb{A})=\sup_{i}\{a_{i}-a_{i-1}\}
.

Step 2.
Fix an arbitrary sequence of increasing reals
\mathbb{A}=\{a_{i}\}_{i=0}^{2N+1}
such that
a_{0}=\underline{r}_{\alpha}(w,q_{l})
,
a_{N+1}=w
and
a_{2N+1}\leq\overline{r}_{\alpha}(w,q_{h})
. Set
a_{2N+2}:=\overline{r}_{\alpha}(w,q_{h})
. Note that
\overline{r}_{\alpha}(w,q_{h})=\infty
when
\alpha=0
. With some abuse of notation, we will use intervals that include
\overline{r}_{\alpha}(w,q_{h})
. These should be interpreted as open when
\alpha=0
.

We next develop a lower bound on the maximin ratio
\pazocal{R}(\pazocal{P}_{\mathbb{A}},\pazocal{F}_{\alpha}(w,[q_{l},q_{h}]))
in the form of a linear program. Fix a mechanism
\Psi\mbox{ in }\pazocal{P}_{\mathbb{A}}
and denote by
p_{0},...,p_{2N+1}
the corresponding probabilities. We set
p_{2N+2}:=0
. Using
proposition

F-1
. Then we have:

\displaystyle\inf_{F\in\pazocal{F}_{\alpha}(w,[q_{l},q_{h}])}R(\Psi,F)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\min\Biggl\{\inf_{x\in[\underline{r}_{\alpha}(w,q_{l}),w)}\frac{1}{\mbox{opt}(F_{\alpha}(\cdot|x,(w,q_{l})))}\int_{0}^{\infty}u\overline{F}_{\alpha}(u|x,(w,q_{l}))d\Psi(u),

\displaystyle\inf_{x\in[w,\overline{r}_{\alpha}(w,q_{h})]}\frac{1}{\mbox{opt}(F_{\alpha}(\cdot|x,(w,q_{h})))}\int_{0}^{\infty}u\overline{F}_{\alpha}(u|x,(w,q_{h})))d\Psi(u)\Biggr\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\min\Biggl\{\min_{i=0,\cdots,N}\inf_{x\in[a_{i},a_{i+1})}\frac{1}{\mbox{opt}(F_{\alpha}(\cdot|x,(w,q_{l})))}\int_{0}^{\infty}u\overline{F}_{\alpha}(u|x,(w,q_{l}))d\Psi(u),

\displaystyle\min_{i=N+1,\cdots,2N+1}\inf_{x\in[a_{i},a_{i+1}]}\frac{1}{\mbox{opt}(F_{\alpha}(\cdot|x,(w,q_{h})))}\int_{0}^{\infty}u\overline{F}_{\alpha}(u|x,(w,q_{h})))d\Psi(u)\Biggr\}.

Note that, for
x\in[\underline{r}_{\alpha}(w,q_{l}),w)
,
\overline{F}_{\alpha}(\cdot|x,(w,q_{l}))
is non-decreasing in
x
and that the revenue function
u\mapsto u\overline{F}_{\alpha}(\cdot|x,(w,q_{l}))
is increasing in
u
on
[0,x)
and decreasing on
(x,w)
. In addition, note that, for
x\in[w,\overline{r}_{\alpha}(w,q_{h})]
, the revenue function
u\mapsto u\overline{F}_{\alpha}(u|x,(w,q_{h}))
is non-decreasing on
[0,x]
. We let
\mbox{opt}(F_{\alpha}(\cdot|a_{i+1}^{-},(w,q_{l})))=\lim_{x\to a_{i+1}^{-}}\mbox{opt}(F_{\alpha}(\cdot|x,(w,q_{l})))
for any
i=0,\cdots,N
.
Hence, we have

\displaystyle\inf_{F\in\pazocal{F}_{\alpha}(w,[q_{l},q_{h}])}R(\Psi,F)

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\min\Biggl\{\min_{i=0,\cdots,N}\frac{1}{\mbox{opt}(F_{\alpha}(\cdot|a_{i+1}^{-},(w,q_{l})))}\int_{0}^{\infty}u\overline{F}_{\alpha}(u|a_{i},(w,q_{l}))d\Psi(u),

(F-2)

\displaystyle\min_{i=N+1,\cdots,2N+1}\frac{1}{\mbox{opt}(F_{\alpha}(\cdot|a_{i+1}^{-},(w,q_{h})))}\int_{0}^{\infty}u\overline{F}_{\alpha}(u|a_{i},(w,q_{h}))d\Psi(u)\Biggr\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\min\Biggl\{\min_{i=0,\cdots,N}\frac{1}{\mbox{opt}(F_{\alpha}(\cdot|a_{i+1}^{-},(w,q_{l})))}\sum_{j=0}^{2N+1}a_{j}\overline{F}_{\alpha}(a_{j}|a_{i},(w,q_{l}))p_{j},

\displaystyle\min_{i=N+1,\cdots,2N+1}\frac{1}{\mbox{opt}(F_{\alpha}(\cdot|a_{i+1},(w,q_{h})))}\sum_{j=0}^{2N+1}a_{j}\overline{F}_{\alpha}(a_{j}|a_{i},(w,q_{h}))p_{j}\Biggr\},

where the equality simply stems from the fact that
\Psi\mbox{ in }\pazocal{P}_{\mathbb{A}}
.
The problem of maximizing over mechanisms in
\pazocal{P}_{\mathbb{A}}
is clearly lower bounded by the problem of maximizing the RHS above over
p_{0},...,p_{2N+1}
. The latter problem admits exactly

⁢
L
P

-int
as its epigraph formulation, and hence we have

\displaystyle\pazocal{R}(\pazocal{P}_{\mathbb{A}},\pazocal{F}_{\alpha}(w,[q_{l},q_{h}]))

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\underline{\pazocal{L}}_{\alpha,q_{l},q_{h},\mathbb{A}}.

Step 3.
We next establish that with a proper choice of sequence
\mathbb{A}
,
\underline{\pazocal{L}}_{\alpha,q_{l},q_{h},\mathbb{A}}
may be arbitrarily close to the maximin ratio
\pazocal{R}(\pazocal{P},\pazocal{F}_{\alpha}(w,[q_{l},q_{h}]))
. To do so, we will first develop an upper bound on
\pazocal{R}(\pazocal{P}_{\mathbb{A}},\pazocal{F}_{\alpha}(w,[q_{l},q_{h}]))
. Then, we will construct a particular sequence
\mathbb{A}
and establish for this sequence, the gap between
\pazocal{R}(\pazocal{P}_{\mathbb{A}},\pazocal{F}_{\alpha}(w,[q_{l},q_{h}]))
and
\underline{\pazocal{L}}_{\alpha,q_{l},q_{h},\mathbb{A}}
is small and that the gap between
\pazocal{R}(\pazocal{P}_{\mathbb{A}},\pazocal{F}_{\alpha}(w,[q_{l},q_{h}]))
and
\pazocal{R}(\pazocal{P},\pazocal{F}_{\alpha}(w,[q_{l},q_{h}]))
is also small. This will yield the result.

Suppose that
a_{0}>0
. Following the same reasoning as in step 2 above, we may also obtain an upper bound on
\inf_{F\in\pazocal{F}_{\alpha}(w,[q_{l},q_{h}])}R(\Psi,F)
. Indeed, we have

\displaystyle\inf_{F\in\pazocal{F}_{\alpha}(w,[q_{l},q_{h}])}R(\Psi,F)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\min\Biggl\{\min_{i=0,\cdots,N}\frac{1}{\mbox{opt}(F_{\alpha}(\cdot|a_{i},(w,q_{l})))}\int_{0}^{\infty}u\overline{F}_{\alpha}(u|a_{i+1}^{-},(w,q_{l}))d\Psi(u),

\displaystyle\min_{i=N+1,\cdots,2N+1}\frac{1}{\mbox{opt}(F_{\alpha}(\cdot|a_{i},(w,q_{h})))}\int_{0}^{\infty}u\overline{F}_{\alpha}(u|a_{i+1}^{-},(w,q_{h}))d\Psi(u)\Biggr\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\min\Biggl\{\min_{i=0,\cdots,N}\frac{1}{\mbox{opt}(F_{\alpha}(\cdot|a_{i},(w,q_{l})))}\sum_{j=0}^{2N+1}a_{j}\overline{F}_{\alpha}(a_{j}|a_{i+1}^{-},(w,q_{l}))p_{j},

\displaystyle\min_{i=N+1,\cdots,2N+1}\frac{1}{\mbox{opt}(F_{\alpha}(\cdot|a_{i},(w,q_{h})))}\sum_{j=0}^{2N+1}a_{j}\overline{F}_{\alpha}(a_{j}|a_{i+1}^{-},(w,q_{h}))p_{j}\Biggr\}.

With
u\overline{F}_{\alpha}(u|a_{i+1}^{-},(w,q_{l}))=\lim_{x\to a_{i+1}^{-}}u\overline{F}_{\alpha}(u|x,(w,q_{l}))
for any
u\geq 0
and
i=0,\cdots,2N+1
.
The problem of maximizing over mechanisms in
\pazocal{P}_{\mathbb{A}}
is clearly upper bounded by the problem of maximizing the RHS above over
p_{0},...,p_{2N+1}
. The epigraph formulation of the latter problem can be written as

\displaystyle\overline{\pazocal{L}}_{\alpha,q_{l},q_{h},\mathbb{A}}\>\>=\>\>\max_{\mathbf{p},c}

\displaystyle~~c

(
\pazocal{LP}
-int-up)

\displaystyle s.t.~~

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\mbox{opt}(F_{\alpha}(\cdot|a_{i},(w,q_{l})))}\sum_{j=0}^{2N+1}a_{j}\overline{F}_{\alpha}(a_{j}|a_{i+1}^{-},(w,q_{l}))p_{j}\geq c\quad i=0,...N,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\mbox{opt}(F_{\alpha}(\cdot|a_{i},(w,q_{h})))}\sum_{j=0}^{2N+1}a_{j}\overline{F}_{\alpha}(a_{j}|a_{i+1}^{-},(w,q_{h}))p_{j}\geq c\quad i=N+1,...2N+1,

\displaystyle\sum_{j=0}^{2N+1}p_{j}\leq 1,\quad p_{i}\geq 0\quad i=0,...2N+1.

Therefore, we have

\displaystyle\pazocal{R}(\pazocal{P}_{\mathbb{A}},\pazocal{F}_{\alpha}(w,[q_{l},q_{h}]))

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\overline{\pazocal{L}}_{\alpha,q_{l},q_{h},\mathbb{A}}.

Hence, we have established the following.

\displaystyle\underline{\pazocal{L}}_{\alpha,q_{l},q_{h},\mathbb{A}}\leq\pazocal{R}(\pazocal{P}_{\mathbb{A}},\pazocal{F}_{\alpha}(w,[q_{l},q_{h}]))\leq\overline{\pazocal{L}}_{\alpha,q_{l},q_{h},\mathbb{A}}.

We next quantify the gap
\overline{\pazocal{L}}_{\alpha,q_{l},q_{h},\mathbb{A}}-\underline{\pazocal{L}}_{\alpha,q_{l},q_{h},\mathbb{A}}
as a function the discretization grid size
N
for a particular sequence. For
N>1
,
b=\overline{r}_{\alpha}(w,q_{h})
if
\alpha\in(0,1]
,
b>w
if
\alpha=0
and
\eta\mbox{ in }(0,\underline{r}_{\alpha}(w,q_{l}))
, consider the following finite sequence of prices
\mathbb{A}=\{a_{i}\}_{i=0}^{2N+1}
in
[\underline{r}_{\alpha}(w,q_{l}),\min\{b,\overline{r}_{\alpha}(w,q_{h})\}]
:

\displaystyle a_{i}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\begin{cases}\underline{r}_{\alpha}(w,q_{l})+\frac{i}{N}\left((w-\eta)-\underline{r}_{\alpha}(w,q_{l})\right)&\quad\mbox{if }0\leq i\leq N,\\
w+\frac{i-(N+1)}{N}\left(\min\{b,\overline{r}_{\alpha}(w,q_{h})\}-w\right)&\quad\mbox{if }N+1\leq i\leq 2N+1.\\
\end{cases}

When fixing the probability weights
p
, let
\underline{c}(\textbf{p})
denote the maximum value achievable (as a function of
c
) in the inner problem in (

⁢
L
P

-int
). In particular, it can be expressed as the minimum in (
F-2
).

Let
p
correspond be a probability weight vector corresponding to an optimal solution to the upper bound linear program (

⁢
L
P

-int-up
).
We have

\displaystyle\overline{\pazocal{L}}_{\alpha,q_{l},q_{h},\mathbb{A}}-\underline{\pazocal{L}}_{\alpha,q_{l},q_{h},\mathbb{A}}\>\leq\>\overline{\pazocal{L}}_{\alpha,q_{l},q_{h},\mathbb{A}}-\underline{c}(\textbf{p}).

We next analyze upper bound the gap
\overline{\pazocal{L}}_{\alpha,q_{l},q_{h},\mathbb{A}}-\underline{c}(\textbf{p})
as a function of the constraints that lead to the minimum value when solving
\underline{c}(\textbf{p})
.

Case 1:
If
\underline{c}(\textbf{p})=\frac{1}{\mbox{opt}(F_{\alpha}(\cdot|a_{i+1}^{-},(w,q_{l})))}\sum_{j=0}^{2N+1}a_{j}\overline{F}_{\alpha}(a_{j}|a_{i},(w,q_{l}))p_{j}
for some
0\leq i\leq N-1
. Then we have

\displaystyle\overline{\pazocal{L}}_{\alpha,q_{l},q_{h},\mathbb{A}}-\underline{c}(\textbf{p})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\overline{\pazocal{L}}_{\alpha,q_{l},q_{h},\mathbb{A}}-\frac{1}{\mbox{opt}(F_{\alpha}(\cdot|a_{i+1}^{-},(w,q_{l})))}\sum_{j=0}^{2N+1}a_{j}\overline{F}_{\alpha}(a_{j}|a_{i},(w,q_{l}))p_{j}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\mbox{opt}(F_{\alpha}(\cdot|a_{i},(w,q_{l})))}\sum_{j=0}^{2N+1}a_{j}\overline{F}_{\alpha}(a_{j}|a_{i+1}^{-},(w,q_{l}))p_{j}

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{\mbox{opt}(F_{\alpha}(\cdot|a_{i+1}^{-},(w,q_{l})))}\sum_{j=0}^{2N+1}a_{j}\overline{F}_{\alpha}(a_{j}|a_{i},(w,q_{l}))p_{j}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(\frac{1}{\mbox{opt}(F_{\alpha}(\cdot|a_{i},(w,q_{l})))}-\frac{1}{\mbox{opt}(F_{\alpha}(\cdot|a_{i+1}^{-},(w,q_{l})))}\right)\sum_{j=0}^{2N}a_{j}\overline{F}_{\alpha}(a_{j}|a_{i+1}^{-},(w,q_{l}))p_{j}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{\mbox{opt}(F_{\alpha}(\cdot|a_{i+1}^{-},(w,q_{l})))}\sum_{j=0}^{2N+1}a_{j}\left[\overline{F}_{\alpha}(a_{j}|a_{i+1}^{-},(w,q_{l}))-\overline{F}_{\alpha}(a_{j}|a_{i},(w,q_{l}))\right]p_{j}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(\frac{a_{i+1}-a_{i}}{a_{i}}\right)\sum_{j=0}^{2N+1}\frac{a_{j}\overline{F}_{\alpha}(a_{j}|a_{i+1},(w,q_{l}))}{{\mbox{opt}(F_{\alpha}(\cdot|a_{i+1},(w,q_{l})))}}p_{j}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{\mbox{opt}(F_{\alpha}(\cdot|a_{i+1},(w,q_{l})))}\sum_{j=i+1}^{N+1}a_{j}\left[\overline{F}_{\alpha}(a_{j}|a_{i+1},(w,q_{l}))-\overline{F}_{\alpha}(a_{j}|a_{i},(w,q_{l}))\right]p_{j},

where in the last equality, we have used that
a_{i+1}=\mbox{opt}(F_{\alpha}(\cdot|a_{i+1}^{-},(w,q_{l})))
for
0\leq i\leq N-1
(cf.
Lemma

C-2
),
\overline{F}_{\alpha}(\cdot|a_{i+1}^{-},(w,q_{l}))=\overline{F}_{\alpha}(\cdot|a_{i+1},(w,q_{l}))
for
0\leq i\leq N-1
and the fact that
\overline{F}_{\alpha}(\cdot|a_{i+1},(w,q_{l}))=\overline{F}_{\alpha}(\cdot|a_{i},(w,q_{l}))
on
[0,a_{i}]
and on
(w,+\infty)
.
We analyze the two terms on the RHS above separately.

\displaystyle\left(\frac{a_{i+1}-a_{i}}{a_{i}}\right)\sum_{j=0}^{2N+1}\frac{a_{j}\overline{F}_{\alpha}(a_{j}|a_{i+1},(w,q_{l}))}{{\mbox{opt}(F_{\alpha}(\cdot|a_{i+1},(w,q_{l})))}}p_{j}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left(\frac{a_{i+1}-a_{i}}{a_{i}}\right)\sum_{j=0}^{2N+1}p_{j}\>\leq\>\frac{1}{N}\left(\frac{w-\eta-\underline{r}_{\alpha}(w,q_{l})}{\underline{r}_{\alpha}(w,q_{l})}\right),

where the first inequality follows from the definition of
opt
, and the second from the fact that
\mathbf{p}
belongs to the simplex, from definition and from lower bounding
a_{i}
by
a_{0}=\underline{r}_{\alpha}(w,q_{l})
.

Now, let for
j=i+1,...,N
,
g_{j}(x)=\overline{F}_{\alpha}(a_{j}|x,(w,q_{l}))
. Note that
g_{j}(\cdot)
is differentiable in
[a_{i},a_{j}]
with derivative bounded as follows

\displaystyle g_{j}^{\prime}(x)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(\Gamma_{\alpha}\left(\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q_{l}\right)\frac{a_{j}-x}{w-x}\right)\right)^{\prime}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q_{l}\right)\frac{(w-a_{j})}{(w-x)^{2}}\left(\Gamma_{\alpha}\left(\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q_{l}\right)\frac{a_{j}-x}{w-x}\right)\right)^{2-\alpha}\leq\frac{\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q_{l}\right)}{w-x}\leq\frac{\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q_{l}\right)}{w-\eta}.

We deduce that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\mbox{opt}(F_{\alpha}(\cdot|a_{i+1},(w,q_{l})))}\sum_{j=i+1}^{N+1}a_{j}\left[\overline{F}_{\alpha}(a_{j}|a_{i+1},(w,q_{l}))-\overline{F}_{\alpha}(a_{j}|a_{i},(w,q_{l}))\right]a_{j}p_{j}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{a_{i+1}}\sum_{j=i+1}^{N+1}\frac{\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q_{l}\right)}{w-\eta}(a_{i+1}-a_{i})a_{j}p_{j}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N}\left(\frac{w-\eta-\underline{r}_{\alpha}(w,q_{l})}{\underline{r}_{\alpha}(w,q_{l})}\right)w\frac{\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q_{l}\right)}{w-\eta}.

Hence, we have, in this case

\displaystyle\overline{\pazocal{L}}_{\alpha,q_{l},q_{h},\mathbb{A}}-\underline{\pazocal{L}}_{\alpha,q_{l},q_{h},\mathbb{A}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N}\left(\frac{w-\eta-\underline{r}_{\alpha}(w,q_{l})}{\underline{r}_{\alpha}(w,q_{l})}\right)\left[1+w\frac{\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q_{l}\right)}{w-\eta}\right].

Case 2:
Suppose
\underline{c}(\textbf{p})=\frac{1}{\mbox{opt}(F_{\alpha}(\cdot|a_{i+1}^{-},(w,q_{l})))}\sum_{j=0}^{2N+1}a_{j}\overline{F}_{\alpha}(a_{j}|a_{i},(w,q_{l}))p_{j}
for
i=N
. In this case, we have

\displaystyle\underline{c}(\textbf{p})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{w}\left(\sum_{j=0}^{N}a_{j}p_{j}+wq_{l}p_{N+1}\right),

and

\displaystyle\overline{\pazocal{L}}_{\alpha,q_{l},q_{h},\mathbb{A}}-\underline{\pazocal{L}}_{\alpha,q_{l},q_{h},\mathbb{A}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{w-\eta}\left(\sum_{j=0}^{N}a_{j}p_{j}+wq_{l}p_{N+1}\right)-\frac{1}{w}\left(\sum_{j=0}^{N}a_{j}p_{j}+wq_{l}p_{N+1}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left(\frac{1}{w-\eta}-\frac{1}{w}\right)\left(\sum_{j=0}^{N}a_{j}p_{j}+wq_{l}p_{N+1}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{\eta}{w-\eta}w\sum_{j=0}^{N+1}p_{j}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{\eta w}{w-\eta}.

Case 3:
Suppose
\underline{c}(\textbf{p})=\frac{1}{\mbox{opt}(F_{\alpha}(\cdot|a_{i+1},(w,q_{h})))}\sum_{j=0}^{2N+1}a_{j}\overline{F}_{\alpha}(a_{j}|a_{i},(w,q_{h}))p_{j}
for some
i=N+1,...,2N
.

\displaystyle\overline{\pazocal{L}}_{\alpha,q_{l},q_{h},\mathbb{A}}-\underline{\pazocal{L}}_{\alpha,q_{l},q_{h},\mathbb{A}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\mbox{opt}(F_{\alpha}(\cdot|a_{i},(w,q_{h})))}\sum_{j=0}^{2N+1}a_{j}\overline{F}_{\alpha}(a_{j}|a_{i+1}^{-},(w,q_{h}))p_{j}

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{\mbox{opt}(F_{\alpha}(\cdot|a_{i+1}^{-},(w,q_{h})))}\sum_{j=0}^{2N+1}a_{j}\overline{F}_{\alpha}(a_{j}|a_{i},(w,q_{h}))p_{j}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(\frac{1}{\mbox{opt}(F_{\alpha}(\cdot|a_{i},(w,q_{h})))}-\frac{1}{\mbox{opt}(F_{\alpha}(\cdot|a_{i+1},(w,q_{h})))}\right)\sum_{j=0}^{2N+1}a_{j}\overline{F}_{\alpha}(a_{j}|a_{i+1}^{-},(w,q_{h}))p_{j}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{\mbox{opt}(F_{\alpha}(\cdot|a_{i+1},(w,q_{h})))}\sum_{j=0}^{2N+1}a_{j}\left[\overline{F}_{\alpha}(a_{j}|a_{i+1}^{-},(w,q_{h}))-\overline{F}_{\alpha}(a_{j}|a_{i},(w,q_{h}))\right]p_{j}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\mbox{opt}(F_{\alpha}(\cdot|a_{i+1},(w,q_{h})))-\mbox{opt}(F_{\alpha}(\cdot|a_{i},(w,q_{h})))}{\mbox{opt}(F_{\alpha}(\cdot|a_{i},(w,q_{h})))}\sum_{j=0}^{2N+1}\frac{a_{j}\overline{F}_{\alpha}(a_{j}|a_{i+1},(w,q_{h}))}{\mbox{opt}(F_{\alpha}(\cdot|a_{i+1},(w,q_{h})))}p_{j},

where in the last equality, we have used that
\overline{F}_{\alpha}(\cdot|a_{i+1}^{-},(w,q_{h}))=\overline{F}_{\alpha}(\cdot|a_{i},(w,q_{l}))
on
\{a_{0},\dots,a_{i+1}\}
. We analyze the above term on the RHS.

\displaystyle\frac{\mbox{opt}(F_{\alpha}(\cdot|a_{i+1},(w,q_{h})))-\mbox{opt}(F_{\alpha}(\cdot|a_{i},(w,q_{h})))}{\mbox{opt}(F_{\alpha}(\cdot|a_{i},(w,q_{h})))}\sum_{j=0}^{2N+1}\frac{a_{j}\overline{F}_{\alpha}(a_{j}|a_{i+1},(w,q_{h}))}{\mbox{opt}(F_{\alpha}(\cdot|a_{i+1},(w,q_{h})))}p_{j}

\displaystyle\leq\left(\frac{a_{i+1}\overline{G}_{\alpha,a_{i+1}}(a_{i+1}|(0,1),(w,q_{h}))-a_{i}\overline{G}_{\alpha,a_{i}}(a_{i}|(0,1),(w,q_{h}))}{a_{i}\overline{G}_{\alpha,a_{i}}(a_{i}|(0,1),(w,q_{h}))}\right)\sum_{j=0}^{2N+1}p_{j}

\displaystyle\leq\left(\frac{a_{i+1}\overline{G}_{\alpha,a_{i+1}}(a_{i+1}|(0,1),(w,q_{h}))-a_{i}\overline{G}_{\alpha,a_{i}}(a_{i}|(0,1),(w,q_{h}))}{a_{i}\overline{G}_{\alpha,a_{i}}(a_{i}|(0,1),(w,q_{h}))}\right),

where the first inequality follows from the definition of
opt
and
\mbox{opt}(F_{\alpha}(\cdot|a_{i+1},(w,q_{h})))
, and the second from the fact that
\mathbf{p}
belongs to the simplex.

Now, let
g_{\alpha,q_{h}}(x)=x\overline{G}_{\alpha,x}(x|(0,1),(w,q_{h}))
. Note that
g_{\alpha,q_{h}}(\cdot)
is differentiable in
[w,\overline{r}_{\alpha}(w,q_{h}))
with derivative bounded as follows

\displaystyle g_{\alpha,q_{h}}^{\prime}(x)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(x\Gamma_{\alpha}\left(\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q_{h}\right)\frac{x}{w}\right)\right)^{\prime}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Gamma_{\alpha}\left(\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q_{h}\right)\frac{x}{w}\right)-\frac{\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q_{h}\right)x}{w}\Gamma_{\alpha}\left(\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q_{h}\right)\frac{x}{w}\right)^{2-\alpha}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Gamma_{\alpha}\left(\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q_{h}\right)\frac{x}{w}\right)\left(1-\frac{\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q_{h}\right)x}{w}\Gamma_{\alpha}\left(\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q_{h}\right)\frac{x}{w}\right)^{1-\alpha}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Gamma_{\alpha}\left(\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q_{h}\right)\frac{x}{w}\right)\left(1-\frac{\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q_{h}\right)x}{w\left(1+(1-\alpha)\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q_{h}\right)\frac{x}{w}\right)}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Gamma_{\alpha}\left(\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q_{h}\right)\frac{x}{w}\right)\left(1-\frac{\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q_{h}\right)\frac{x}{w}}{1+(1-\alpha)\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q_{h}\right)\frac{x}{w}}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Gamma_{\alpha}\left(\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q_{h}\right)\frac{x}{w}\right)\frac{1-\alpha\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q_{h}\right)\frac{x}{w}}{1+(1-\alpha)\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q_{h}\right)\frac{x}{w}}.

Therefore, since
x\leq\overline{r}_{\alpha}(w,q_{h}):=\frac{w}{\alpha\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q_{h}\right)}
, we have that

\displaystyle|g_{\alpha,q_{h}}^{\prime}(x)|=\left|\Gamma_{\alpha}\left(\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q_{h}\right)\frac{x}{w}\right)\frac{1-\alpha\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q_{h}\right)\frac{x}{w}}{1+(1-\alpha)\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q_{h}\right)\frac{x}{w}}\right|=\Gamma_{\alpha}\left(\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q_{h}\right)\frac{x}{w}\right)\frac{1-\alpha\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q_{h}\right)\frac{x}{w}}{1+(1-\alpha)\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q_{h}\right)\frac{x}{w}}\leq 1.

We deduce that

\displaystyle\left(\frac{a_{i+1}\overline{G}_{\alpha,a_{i+1}}(a_{i+1}|(0,1),(w,q_{h}))-a_{i}\overline{G}_{\alpha,a_{i}}(a_{i}|(0,1),(w,q_{h}))}{a_{i}\overline{G}_{\alpha,a_{i}}(a_{i}|(0,1),(w,q_{h}))}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{a_{i+1}-a_{i}}{g_{\alpha,q_{h}}(a_{i})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{\min\{b,\overline{r}_{\alpha}(w,q_{h})\}-w}{Ng_{\alpha,q_{h}}(w)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\min\{b,\overline{r}_{\alpha}(w,q_{h})\}-w}{Nwq}.

Hence, we have, in this case

\displaystyle\overline{\pazocal{L}}_{\alpha,q_{l},q_{h},\mathbb{A}}-\underline{\pazocal{L}}_{\alpha,q_{l},q_{h},\mathbb{A}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{\min\{b,\overline{r}_{\alpha}(w,q_{h})\}-w}{Nwq}.

Case 4:
Suppose
\underline{c}(\textbf{p})=\frac{1}{\mbox{opt}(F_{\alpha}(\cdot|a_{i+1},(w,q_{h})))}\sum_{j=0}^{2N+1}a_{j}\overline{F}_{\alpha}(a_{j}|a_{i},(w,q_{h}))p_{j}
for
i=2N+1
.

\displaystyle\overline{\pazocal{L}}_{\alpha,q_{l},q_{h},\mathbb{A}}-\underline{\pazocal{L}}_{\alpha,q_{l},q_{h},\mathbb{A}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\mbox{opt}(F_{\alpha}(\cdot|a_{2N+1},(w,q_{h})))}\sum_{j=0}^{2N+1}a_{j}\overline{F}_{\alpha}(a_{j}|a_{2N+1}^{-},(w,q_{h}))p_{j}

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{\mbox{opt}(F_{\alpha}(\cdot|a_{2N+2}^{-},(w,q_{h})))}\sum_{j=0}^{2N+1}a_{j}\overline{F}_{\alpha}(a_{j}|a_{2N},(w,q_{h}))p_{j}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(\frac{1}{\mbox{opt}(F_{\alpha}(\cdot|a_{2N+1},(w,q_{h})))}-\frac{1}{\mbox{opt}(F_{\alpha}(\cdot|a_{2N+2}^{-},(w,q_{h})))}\right)\sum_{j=0}^{2N+1}a_{j}\overline{F}_{\alpha}(a_{j}|a_{2N+2}^{-},(w,q_{h}))p_{j}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{\mbox{opt}(F_{\alpha}(\cdot|a_{2N+2}^{-},(w,q_{h})))}\sum_{j=0}^{2N+1}a_{j}\left[\overline{F}_{\alpha}(a_{j}|a_{2N+2}^{-},(w,q_{h}))-\overline{F}_{\alpha}(a_{j}|a_{2N+1},(w,q_{h}))\right]p_{j}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\mbox{opt}(F_{\alpha}(\cdot|a_{2N+2}^{-},(w,q_{h})))-\mbox{opt}(F_{\alpha}(\cdot|a_{2N+1},(w,q_{h})))}{\mbox{opt}(F_{\alpha}(\cdot|a_{2N+1},(w,q_{h})))}\sum_{j=0}^{2N+1}\frac{a_{j}\overline{F}_{\alpha}(a_{j}|a_{2N+2}^{-},(w,q_{h}))}{\mbox{opt}(F_{\alpha}(\cdot|a_{2N+2}^{-},(w,q_{h})))}p_{j},

where in the last equality, we have used the fact that
\overline{F}_{\alpha}(\cdot|a_{2N+2}^{-},(w,q_{h}))=\overline{F}_{\alpha}(\cdot|a_{2N+1},(w,q_{l}))
on
\{a_{0},\cdots,a_{2N+1}\}
.

We analyze the above term on the RHS in two separate cases
\alpha\in(0,1]
and
\alpha=0
.

In the case where
\alpha\in(0,1]
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{\mbox{opt}(F_{\alpha}(\cdot|a_{2N+2}^{-},(w,q_{h})))-\mbox{opt}(F_{\alpha}(\cdot|a_{2N+1},(w,q_{h})))}{\mbox{opt}(F_{\alpha}(\cdot|a_{2N+1},(w,q_{h})))}\sum_{j=0}^{2N+1}\frac{a_{j}\overline{F}_{\alpha}(a_{j}|a_{2N+2}^{-},(w,q_{h}))}{\mbox{opt}(F_{\alpha}(\cdot|a_{2N+2}^{-},(w,q_{h})))}p_{j}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left(\frac{\overline{r}_{\alpha}(w,q_{h})\overline{G}_{\alpha,\overline{r}_{\alpha}(w,q_{h})}(\overline{r}_{\alpha}(w,q_{h})|(0,1),(w,q_{h}))-a_{2N+1}\overline{G}_{\alpha,a_{2N+1}}(a_{2N+1}|(0,1),(w,q_{h}))}{a_{2N+1}\overline{G}_{\alpha,a_{2N+1}}(a_{2N+1}|(0,1),(w,q_{h}))}\right)\sum_{j=0}^{2N+1}p_{j}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left(\frac{\overline{r}_{\alpha}(w,q_{h})\overline{G}_{\alpha,\overline{r}_{\alpha}(w,q_{h})}(\overline{r}_{\alpha}(w,q_{h})|(0,1),(w,q_{h}))-a_{2N+1}\overline{G}_{\alpha,a_{2N+1}}(a_{2N+1}|(0,1),(w,q_{h}))}{a_{2N+1}\overline{G}_{\alpha,a_{i}}(a_{i}|(0,1),(w,q_{h}))}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{g_{\alpha,q_{h}}(\overline{r}_{\alpha}(w,q_{h}))-g_{\alpha,q_{h}}(a_{2N+1})}{g_{\alpha,q_{h}}(a_{2N+1})}\leq\frac{\overline{r}_{\alpha}(w,q_{h})-a_{2N+1}}{g_{\alpha,q_{h}}(w)}=\frac{\overline{r}_{\alpha}(w,q_{h})-w}{Nwq},

where the first inequality follows from the definition of
opt
, the second from the fact that
\mathbb{p}
belongs to the simplex, and the fourth from the fact that the derivative
g^{\prime}_{\alpha,q_{h}}(\cdot)
is bounded (established in the previous case).

In the case where
\alpha=0
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{\mbox{opt}(F_{\alpha}(\cdot|a_{2N+2}^{-},(w,q_{h})))-\mbox{opt}(F_{\alpha}(\cdot|a_{2N+1},(w,q_{h})))}{\mbox{opt}(F_{\alpha}(\cdot|a_{2N+1},(w,q_{h})))}\sum_{j=0}^{2N+1}\frac{a_{j}\overline{F}_{\alpha}(a_{j}|a_{2N+2}^{-},(w,q_{h}))}{\mbox{opt}(F_{\alpha}(\cdot|a_{2N+2}^{-},(w,q_{h})))}p_{j}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{\lim_{x\to\infty}\mbox{opt}(F_{0}(\cdot|x,(w,q_{h})))-\mbox{opt}(F_{0}(\cdot|b,(w,q_{h})))}{\mbox{opt}(F_{0}(\cdot|b,(w,q_{h})))}\sum_{j=0}^{2N+1}p_{j}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{\frac{1}{\frac{1}{q_{h}}-1}-\frac{b}{1+\left(\frac{1}{q_{h}}-1\right)b}}{\frac{b}{1+\left(\frac{1}{q_{h}}-1\right)b}}=\frac{1+\left(\frac{1}{q_{h}}-1\right)b}{\left(\frac{1}{q_{h}}-1\right)b}-1=\frac{1}{\left(\frac{1}{q_{h}}-1\right)b},

where the first inequality follows from the definition of
opt
, the second from the fact that
\mathbf{p}
belongs to the simplex, and the definition of
\mbox{opt}(F_{\alpha}(\cdot|a_{2N+1}^{-},(w,q_{h})))
.
Hence, we have, in this case

\displaystyle\overline{\pazocal{L}}_{\alpha,q_{l},q_{h},\mathbb{A}}-\underline{\pazocal{L}}_{\alpha,q_{l},q_{h},\mathbb{A}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{q_{h}}{(1-q_{h})b}.

We are now in a position to combine all cases and conclude.

If
\alpha\in(0,1]
, we have established that

\displaystyle\overline{\pazocal{L}}_{\alpha,q_{l},q_{h},\mathbb{A}}-\underline{\pazocal{L}}_{\alpha,q_{l},q_{h},\mathbb{A}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\max\left\{\frac{1}{N}\left(\frac{w-\eta-\underline{r}_{\alpha}(w,q_{l})}{\underline{r}_{\alpha}(w,q_{l})}\right)\left[1+w\frac{\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q_{l}\right)}{w-\eta}\right],\frac{\eta w}{w-\eta},\frac{\overline{r}_{\alpha}(w,q_{h})-w}{Nwq}\right\}.

Recall that
Proposition

F-2
implies that

\displaystyle\pazocal{R}(\pazocal{P}_{\mathbb{A}},\pazocal{F}_{\alpha}(w,[q_{l},q_{h}]))

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\pazocal{R}(\pazocal{P},\pazocal{F}_{\alpha}(w,[q_{l},q_{h}]))\>\leq\>\pazocal{R}(\pazocal{P}_{\mathbb{A}},\pazocal{F}_{\alpha}(w,[q_{l},q_{h}]))+\frac{\Delta(\mathbb{A})}{\underline{r}_{\alpha}(w,q_{l})}.

Noting that
\Delta(\mathbb{A})=\max\{\frac{w-\eta-\underline{r}_{\alpha}(w,q_{l})}{N},\eta,\frac{\overline{r}_{\alpha}(w,q_{h})-w}{N}\}
, we have

\displaystyle\underline{\pazocal{L}}_{\alpha,q_{l},q_{h},\mathbb{A}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\pazocal{R}(\pazocal{P},\pazocal{F}_{\alpha}(w,[q_{l},q_{h}]))

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\pazocal{R}(\pazocal{P}_{\mathbb{A}},\pazocal{F}_{\alpha}(w,[q_{l},q_{h}]))+\frac{\Delta(\mathbb{A})}{\underline{r}_{\alpha}(w,q_{l})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\underline{\pazocal{L}}_{\alpha,q_{l},q_{h},\mathbb{A}}+\left(\overline{\pazocal{L}}_{\alpha,q_{l},q_{h},\mathbb{A}}-\underline{\pazocal{L}}_{\alpha,q_{l},q_{h},\mathbb{A}}\right)+\max\left\{\frac{w-\eta-\underline{r}_{\alpha}(w,q_{l})}{N\underline{r}_{\alpha}(w,q_{l})},\eta,\frac{\overline{r}_{\alpha}(w,q_{h})-w}{N\underline{r}_{\alpha}(w,q_{l})}\right\}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\underline{\pazocal{L}}_{\alpha,q_{l},q_{h},\mathbb{A}}+\max\left\{\frac{1}{N}\left(\frac{w-\eta-\underline{r}_{\alpha}(w,q_{l})}{\underline{r}_{\alpha}(w,q_{l})}\right)\left[1+w\frac{\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q_{l}\right)}{w-\eta}\right],\frac{\eta w}{w-\eta},\frac{\overline{r}_{\alpha}(w,q_{h})-w}{Nwq}\right\}

\displaystyle+\max\left\{\frac{w-\eta-\underline{r}_{\alpha}(w,q_{l})}{N\underline{r}_{\alpha}(w,q_{l})},\eta,\frac{\overline{r}_{\alpha}(w,q_{h})-w}{N\underline{r}_{\alpha}(w,q_{l})}\right\}.

By choosing
\eta=\frac{w}{\sqrt{N}}
, we obtain

\displaystyle\underline{\pazocal{L}}_{\alpha,q_{l},q_{h},\mathbb{A}}\leq\pazocal{R}(\pazocal{P},\pazocal{F}_{\alpha}(w,[q_{l},q_{h}]))\leq\underline{\pazocal{L}}_{\alpha,q_{l},q_{h},\mathbb{A}}+\mathscr{O}(\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}).

Suppose now
\alpha=0
. In this case.

\displaystyle\overline{\pazocal{L}}_{\alpha,q_{l},q_{h},\mathbb{A}}-\underline{\pazocal{L}}_{\alpha,q_{l},q_{h},\mathbb{A}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\max\left\{\frac{1}{N}\left(\frac{w-\eta-\underline{r}_{\alpha}(w,q_{l})}{\underline{r}_{\alpha}(w,q_{l})}\right)\left[1+w\frac{\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q_{l}\right)}{w-\eta}\right],\frac{\eta w}{w-\eta},\frac{b-w}{Nwq},\frac{q_{h}}{(1-q_{h})b}\right\}.

Using again
Proposition

F-2
and the fact that
\Delta(\mathbb{A})=\max\{\frac{w-\eta-\underline{r}_{\alpha}(w,q_{l})}{N},\eta,\frac{b-w}{N}\}
, we have

\displaystyle\underline{\pazocal{L}}_{\alpha,q_{l},q_{h},\mathbb{A}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\pazocal{R}(\pazocal{P},\pazocal{F}_{\alpha}(w,[q_{l},q_{h}]))

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\pazocal{R}(\pazocal{P}_{\mathbb{A}},\pazocal{F}_{\alpha}(w,[q_{l},q_{h}]))+\frac{\Delta(\mathbb{A})}{\underline{r}_{\alpha}(w,q_{l})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\underline{\pazocal{L}}_{\alpha,q_{l},q_{h},\mathbb{A}}+\left(\overline{\pazocal{L}}_{\alpha,q_{l},q_{h},\mathbb{A}}-\underline{\pazocal{L}}_{\alpha,q_{l},q_{h},\mathbb{A}}\right)+\max\left\{\frac{w-\eta-\underline{r}_{\alpha}(w,q_{l})}{N\underline{r}_{\alpha}(w,q_{l})},\eta,\frac{b-w}{N\underline{r}_{\alpha}(w,q_{l})}\right\}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\underline{\pazocal{L}}_{\alpha,q_{l},q_{h},\mathbb{A}}+\max\left\{\frac{1}{N}\left(\frac{w-\eta-\underline{r}_{\alpha}(w,q_{l})}{\underline{r}_{\alpha}(w,q_{l})}\right)\left[1+w\frac{\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q_{l}\right)}{w-\eta}\right],\frac{\eta w}{w-\eta},\frac{b-w}{Nwq},\frac{q_{h}}{(1-q_{h})b}\right\}

\displaystyle+\max\left\{\frac{w-\eta-\underline{r}_{\alpha}(w,q_{l})}{N\underline{r}_{\alpha}(w,q_{l})},\eta,\frac{b-w}{N\underline{r}_{\alpha}(w,q_{l})}\right\}.

By choosing
\eta=\frac{w}{\sqrt{N}}
and
b=w\sqrt{N}
, we obatin

\displaystyle\underline{\pazocal{L}}_{\alpha,q_{l},q_{h},\mathbb{A}}\leq\pazocal{R}(\pazocal{P},\pazocal{F}_{\alpha}(w,[q_{l},q_{h}]))\leq\underline{\pazocal{L}}_{\alpha,q_{l},q_{h},\mathbb{A}}+\mathscr{O}(\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}).

This concludes the proof.
∎

F.1
Proofs of auxiliary results

Proof of
Proposition

F-1
.
.

First we show that

\displaystyle\inf_{F\in\pazocal{F}_{\alpha}(w,[q_{l},q_{h}])}R(\Psi,F)=\inf_{q\in[q_{l},q_{h}]}\inf_{F\mbox{ in }\pazocal{F}_{\alpha}(w,q)}R(\Psi,F)

Let
q\in[q_{l},q_{h}]
and
F\in\pazocal{F}_{\alpha}(w,q)
, we have:

\displaystyle R(\Psi,F)

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\inf_{F\in\pazocal{F}_{\alpha}(w,[q_{l},q_{h}])}R(\Psi,F)

\displaystyle\implies\inf_{F\in\pazocal{F}_{\alpha}(w,q)}R(\Psi,F)

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\inf_{F\in\pazocal{F}_{\alpha}(w,[q_{l},q_{h}])}R(\Psi,F)

\displaystyle\implies\inf_{q\in[q_{l},q_{h}]}\inf_{F\in\pazocal{F}_{\alpha}(w,q)}R(\Psi,F)

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\inf_{F\in\pazocal{F}_{\alpha}(w,[q_{l},q_{h}])}R(\Psi,F).

Let
\epsilon>0
and
F_{\epsilon}\in\pazocal{F}_{\alpha}(w,[q_{l},q_{h}])
such that:

\displaystyle\inf_{F\in\pazocal{F}_{\alpha}(w,[q_{l},q_{h}])}R(\Psi,F)

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle R(\Psi,F_{\epsilon})-\epsilon

\displaystyle\implies\inf_{F\in\pazocal{F}_{\alpha}(w,[q_{l},q_{h}])}R(\Psi,F)

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\inf_{F\in\pazocal{F}_{\alpha}(w,\{\overline{F}_{\epsilon}(w)\})}R(\Psi,F)-\epsilon

\displaystyle\implies\inf_{F\in\pazocal{F}_{\alpha}(w,[q_{l},q_{h}])}R(\Psi,F)

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\inf_{q\in[q_{l},q_{h}]}\inf_{F\in\pazocal{F}_{\alpha}(w,q)}R(\Psi,F)-\epsilon,

by taking
\epsilon\rightarrow 0
, we obtain the desired result.

Let
F\in\pazocal{F}_{\alpha}(w,[q_{l},q_{h}])
and
q=\overline{F}(w)\in[q_{l},q_{h}]
, by
Theorem

1
, we have:

\displaystyle\inf_{F\in\pazocal{F}_{\alpha}(w,q)}R(\Psi,F)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\min\Biggl\{\inf_{x\in[\underline{r}_{\alpha}(w,q),w)}\frac{1}{\mbox{opt}(F_{\alpha}(\cdot|x,(w,q)))}\int_{0}^{\infty}u\overline{F}_{\alpha}(u|x,(w,q))d\Psi(u),

\displaystyle\inf_{x\in[w,\overline{r}_{\alpha}(w,q)]}\frac{1}{\mbox{opt}(F_{\alpha}(\cdot|x,(w,q)))}\int_{0}^{\infty}u\overline{F}_{\alpha}(u|x,(w,q)))d\Psi(u)\Biggr\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\min\Biggl\{\inf_{x\in[\underline{r}_{\alpha}(w,q),w)}\frac{1}{x}\Biggl[\int_{[0,x)}ud\Psi(u)+\int_{[x,w]}u\overline{G}_{\alpha,w}(u|(x,1),(w,q))d\Psi(u)\Biggl],

\displaystyle\inf_{x\in[w,\overline{r}_{\alpha}(w,q)]}\frac{1}{x\overline{G}_{\alpha,x}(x|(0,1),(w,q))}\int_{[0,x]}u\overline{G}_{\alpha,x}(u|(0,1),(w,q))d\Psi(u)\Biggr\}.

Using the non-decreasing monotonicity of the functions
q\to\underline{r}_{\alpha}(w,q)=\frac{w}{\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q\right)+1},q\to\overline{r}_{\alpha}(w,q)=\frac{w}{\alpha\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q\right)}
, we have:

\displaystyle\inf_{F\in\pazocal{F}_{\alpha}(w,q)}R(\Psi,F)

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\min\Biggl\{\inf_{x\in[\underline{r}_{\alpha}(w,q_{l}),w)}\frac{1}{x}\Biggl[\int_{[0,x)}ud\Psi(u)+\int_{[x,w]}u\overline{G}_{\alpha,w}(u|(x,1),(w,q))d\Psi(u)\Biggl],

\displaystyle\inf_{x\in[w,\overline{r}_{\alpha}(w,q_{h})]}\frac{1}{x\overline{G}_{\alpha,x}(x|(0,1),(w,q))}\int_{[0,x]}u\overline{G}_{\alpha,x}(u|(0,1),(w,q))d\Psi(u)\Biggr\}.

We have, for fixed
(u,x)
such that
x\in[\underline{r}_{\alpha}(w,q_{l}),w),u\in[x,w)
, the following function is clearly non-decreasing

\displaystyle q\to\overline{G}_{\alpha,w}(u|(x,1),(w,q))=\Gamma_{\alpha}\left(\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q\right)\frac{u-x}{w-x}\right).

We have, for fixed
(u,x)
such that
x\in[w,\overline{r}_{\alpha}(w,q_{h})],u\in[w,x]
, the following function is non-increasing

\displaystyle q\to\frac{u\overline{G}_{\alpha,x}(u|(0,1),(w,q))}{x\overline{G}_{\alpha,x}(x|(0,1),(w,q))}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{u\Gamma_{\alpha}\left(\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q\right)\frac{u}{w}\right)}{x\Gamma_{\alpha}\left(\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q\right)\frac{x}{w}\right)}=\begin{cases}\frac{u}{x}(\frac{u}{x}+\frac{x-u}{x(1+(1-\alpha)\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q\right)\frac{x}{w})})^{\frac{1}{\alpha-1}}&\quad\mbox{if }\alpha\in[0,1)\\
\frac{u}{x}q^{\frac{u-x}{w}}&\quad\mbox{if }\alpha=1.\end{cases}

Using the monotonicity of the above functions we get :

\displaystyle\inf_{F\mbox{ in }\pazocal{F}_{\alpha}(w,q)}R(\Psi,F)

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\min\Biggl\{\inf_{x\in[\underline{r}_{\alpha}(w,q_{l}),w)}\frac{1}{x}\Biggl[\int_{[0,x)}ud\Psi(u)+\int_{[x,w]}u\overline{G}_{\alpha,w}(u|(x,1),(w,q_{l}))d\Psi(u)\Biggl],

\displaystyle\inf_{x\in[w,\overline{r}_{\alpha}(w,q_{h})]}\frac{1}{x\overline{G}_{\alpha,x}(x|(0,1),(w,q))}\int_{[0,x]}u\overline{G}_{\alpha,x}(u|(0,1),(w,q_{h}))d\Psi(u)\Biggr\}.

Since the right hand-side does not depend on
q
, we take the minimum on
q

\displaystyle\inf_{F\in\pazocal{F}_{\alpha}(w,[q_{l},q_{h}])}R(\Psi,F)

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\min\Biggl\{\inf_{x\in[\underline{r}_{\alpha}(w,q_{l}),w)}\frac{1}{x}\Biggl[\int_{[0,x)]}ud\Psi(u)+\int_{[x,w]}u\overline{G}_{\alpha,w}(u|(x,1),(w,q_{l}))d\Psi(u)\Biggl],

\displaystyle\inf_{x\in[w,\overline{r}_{\alpha}(w,q_{h})]}\frac{1}{x\overline{G}_{\alpha,x}(x|(0,1),(w,q))}\int_{[0,x]}u\overline{G}_{\alpha,x}(u|(0,1),(w,q_{h}))d\Psi(u)\Biggr\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\min\Biggl\{\inf_{x\in[\underline{r}_{\alpha}(w,q_{l}),w)}\frac{1}{\mbox{opt}(F_{\alpha}(\cdot|x,(w,q_{l})))}\int_{0}^{\infty}u\overline{F}_{\alpha}(u|x,(w,q_{l}))d\Psi(u),

\displaystyle\inf_{x\in[w,\overline{r}_{\alpha}(w,q_{h})]}\frac{1}{\mbox{opt}(F_{\alpha}(\cdot|x,(w,q_{h})))}\int_{0}^{\infty}u\overline{F}_{\alpha}(u|x,(w,q_{h})))d\Psi(u)\Biggr\}.

This concludes the proof.
∎

Proof of
Proposition

F-2
.
.

Fix
q\mbox{ in }[q_{l},q_{h}]
. Then, using
proposition

2
, there exists
\Psi_{\mathbb{A}}\mbox{ in }\pazocal{P}_{\mathbb{A}}
such that

\displaystyle\inf_{F\in\pazocal{F}_{\alpha}(w,q)}R(\Psi_{\mathbb{A}},F)\geq\inf_{F\in\pazocal{F}_{\alpha}(w,q)}R(\Psi,F)-\frac{\Delta(\mathbb{A})}{a_{1}}-\frac{1}{q(1+(q^{-1}-1)a_{N}/w)}\mathbf{1}\{a_{N}<\overline{r}_{\alpha}(w,q)\},

where
\Delta(\mathbb{A})=\sup_{i}\{a_{i}-a_{i-1}\}
. We have the following function

q\to-\frac{1}{q(1+(q^{-1}-1)a_{N}/w)}=-\frac{1}{\frac{a_{N}}{w}+q(1-\frac{a_{N}}{w})},

is non-increasing because
a_{N}>w
therefore

-\frac{1}{q(1+(q^{-1}-1)a_{N}/w)}\geq-\frac{1}{q_{h}(1+(q_{h}^{-1}-1)a_{N}/w)},

moreover we have
q\to\overline{r}_{\alpha}(w,q)=\frac{w}{\alpha\Gamma_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(q\right)}
is non-decreasing, therefore

\mathbf{1}\{a_{N}<\overline{r}_{\alpha}(w,q)\}\leq\mathbf{1}\{a_{N}<\overline{r}_{\alpha}(w,q_{h})\}.

Hence, since
-\frac{1}{q(1+(q^{-1}-1)a_{N}/w)}<0
, we have

-\frac{\mathbf{1}\{a_{N}<\overline{r}_{\alpha}(w,q)\}}{q(1+(q^{-1}-1)a_{N}/w)}\geq-\frac{\mathbf{1}\{a_{N}<\overline{r}_{\alpha}(w,q_{h})\}}{q_{h}(1+(q_{h}^{-1}-1)a_{N}/w)}.

Using these lower bounds, we obtain

\displaystyle\inf_{F\mbox{ in }\pazocal{F}_{\alpha}(w,q)}R(\Psi_{\mathbb{A}},F)\geq\inf_{F\mbox{ in }\pazocal{F}_{\alpha}(w,q)}R(\Psi,F)-\frac{\Delta(\mathbb{A})}{\underline{r}_{\alpha}(w,q_{l})}-\frac{\mathbf{1}\{a_{N}<\overline{r}_{\alpha}(w,q_{h})\}}{q_{h}(1+(q_{h}^{-1}-1)a_{N}/w)},

taking the infinimum over
q
from both sides concludes the proof.
∎

Appendix G
Upper bound linear program and implementation parameters

In this section, we show that one can obtain an upper bound on the maxmin ratio
\pazocal{R}(\pazocal{P},\pazocal{F}_{\alpha}(w,[q_{l},q_{h}]))
by solving a linear program. Fix an arbitrary sequence of increasing reals
\mathbb{A}=\{a_{i}\}_{i=0}^{2N}
such that
a_{0}=0,a_{1}=\underline{r}_{\alpha}(w,q_{l})
,
a_{N+1}=w
and
a_{2N}\leq\overline{r}_{\alpha}(w,q_{h})
. Set
a_{2N+1}:=\overline{r}_{\alpha}(w,q_{h})
.

Fix a mechanism
\Psi\mbox{ in }\pazocal{P}
and denote by
p_{j+1}=\int_{I_{j}}d\Psi(u)
where we define the intervals
(I_{j})_{j=0,\cdots,2N}
as follows:
